### [1] [Machine Learning Unveils Finite-volume Energy Shifts in Three-body System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28700)
*Wei-Jie Zhang,Zhenyu Zhang,Jifeng Hu,Bing-Nan Lu,Jin-Yi Pang,Qian Wang*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper uses symbolic regression (PySR) to derive universal finite-volume extrapolation (FVE) formulae for three-body systems, successfully unifying short- and long-range interaction regimes and yielding a high-accuracy analytic expression for intermediate-range forces (~1 fm).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Rigorous finite-volume extrapolation (FVE) is well-established for short-range interactions, but remains challenging for long-range potentials where the force range is comparable to the lattice size L—especially in three-body systems. A data-driven approach is needed to bridge theoretical gaps.

Method: The authors extend a prior two-body data-driven scheme by applying symbolic regression (via PySR) to lattice QCD-inspired three-body energy spectra, imposing physical constraints (e.g., asymptotic limits, dimensional consistency) to guide discovery of interpretable analytic FVE formulae.

Result: PySR recovers known short-range limits (κ₃ ≫ κ₂ and κ₃ ∼ κ₂); discovers a new analytic expression for pure long-range potentials; and yields a unified formula incorporating both short- and long-range contributions, achieving excellent agreement with data in the intermediate regime (~1 fm).

Conclusion: Symbolic regression, augmented with physical priors, can generate novel, interpretable analytical results beyond conventional theory—demonstrating a powerful new paradigm for data-driven hadron physics.

Abstract: Finite-volume extrapolation (FVE) is essential for extracting physical observables in the lattice calculation. While rigorous FVE formulations are well established for short-range potentials in both two- and three-body systems, long-range interactions with force ranges comparable to the lattice size $L$ remain challenging. Extending a previous data-driven scheme for two-body systems, we apply symbolic regression (PySR) to uncover universal three-body FVE formulae. For short-range potentials, we reproduce the two limiting cases, i.e. $κ_3\ggκ_2$ and $κ_3\simκ_2$. For pure long-range potentials, we obtain a dedicated analytic expression, and after incorporating short-range contributions, we uncover a unified formula consistent with the original PySR solution, which performs excellently in the intermediate force range around 1 fm. This work demonstrates that combining machine learning with physical constraints can yield novel analytical results inaccessible to conventional theoretical tools, advancing data-driven methodologies in hadron physics.

</details>


### [2] [Quantum Simulations of Two-Dimensional Non-Abelian Adjoint String Breaking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28752)
*Anthony N. Ciavarella,Roland de Putter,Ed Younis,Ermal Rrapaj*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper constructs local Krylov-based truncations of a pure SU(2) lattice gauge theory on triangular lattices to simulate adjoint string breaking on IBM quantum hardware, achieving quantitative agreement with tensor network results and identifying non-Abelian signatures in oscillation and glueball production rates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To advance quantum simulation of strongly coupled quantum field theories—specifically non-Abelian gauge dynamics—by enabling direct probing of real-time dynamics such as adjoint string breaking, which is intractable for classical methods at large coupling.

Method: Local Krylov subspace truncation of the SU(2) Hamiltonian on triangular lattices; construction of adjoint string states with dynamical gluons; design of local operators to detect string oscillations and breaking; implementation of variational quantum circuits on IBM_boston (156-qubit device) for 8×8 and 16×8 lattices; benchmarking against time-evolving block decimation (TEBD) tensor network simulations.

Result: Successful quantum simulation of adjoint string breaking showing oscillatory behavior and glueball production; measured rates exhibit a distinctly non-Abelian signature; quantitative agreement with tensor networks for circuits containing 7,634 CZ gates and two-qubit gate depth 218.

Conclusion: Local Krylov truncation enables practical quantum simulation of non-Abelian gauge dynamics on near-term hardware; observed non-Abelian signatures validate the gauge-theoretic nature of the simulated physics and demonstrate feasibility of probing real-time strong-coupling phenomena.

Abstract: Quantum computers offer the potential to directly probe the dynamics of strongly coupled quantum field theories. As a step towards reaching this potential, local Krylov-based truncations of a pure SU(2) lattice gauge theory on a triangular lattice are constructed. Adjoint strings connected to dynamical gluons are constructed in this truncated theory, and the resonances dominating the long-term dynamics at a large value of the gauge coupling are determined. Local operators are constructed to identify string oscillations and breakings. This is used to perform a quantum simulation of adjoint string breaking on an $8\times8$ and $16\times8$-site lattice with ibm_boston using all 156 qubits. Quantitative agreement with tensor network simulations is obtained for circuits with 7,634 CZ gates with a two-qubit gate depth of 218. In this simulation, the rates of oscillations and glueball production are identified with a distinctly non-Abelian signature of the underlying gauge group.

</details>


### [3] [Detecting Multiple Phase Transitions in Lattice Systems with Intrinsic Dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29090)
*Jie Mei,Tetsuo Hatsuda,Mei Huang,Lingxiao Wang*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper introduces intrinsic dimension (estimated via the two-nearest-neighbors method) as a geometric diagnostic to resolve multiple nearby phase transitions in lattice systems, demonstrating its efficacy in the 2D q-state clock model (q=5,9) and 4D U(1) Higgs model; it further proposes channel-decomposed intrinsic dimension for gauge-invariant observables and shows gauge-variant fields are dominated by unphysical gauge-orbit directions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve distinct transition scales and identify degrees of freedom associated with each transition in lattice systems exhibiting multiple nearby phase transitions—particularly where conventional order parameters (e.g., energy, magnetization) fail to cleanly separate transitions.

Method: Estimates intrinsic dimension (ID) of Monte Carlo configuration ensembles using the two-nearest-neighbors (2NN) estimator; extends it to channel-decomposed ID using gauge-invariant plaquette (gauge sector) and Higgs (scalar sector) variables in the 4D U(1) Higgs model; compares against ID computed on gauge-variant fields to assess gauge redundancy effects.

Result: In the 2D clock model, ID cleanly distinguishes ordered, quasi-critical (BKT), and disordered phases—even for closely spaced transitions (q=5), revealing a low-dimensional valley for the intermediate phase unseen in energy/magnetization. In the 4D U(1) Higgs model, channel-decomposed ID shows sector-specific responses to respective transitions, while combined ID retains both features; ID on gauge-variant fields is dominated by gauge-orbit directions, confirming necessity of gauge fixing or gauge-invariant representations.

Conclusion: Channel-decomposed intrinsic dimension is a robust, geometric probe for disentangling multiple transitions in multi-sector lattice field theories; it provides a principled, unsupervised way to associate transitions with physical degrees of freedom and is directly applicable to open problems such as separating deconfinement and chiral crossover scales in full QCD.

Abstract: Lattice systems with multiple nearby transitions pose two related challenges: resolving distinct transition scales and identifying the degrees of freedom primarily associated with each transition. We show that the intrinsic dimension of Monte Carlo configuration ensembles, estimated by the two-nearest-neighbors method, provides a geometric diagnostic for both problems. In the two-dimensional $q$-state clock model, the intrinsic dimension distinguishes the ordered, quasi-critical, and disordered regimes for both well-separated ($q=9$) and closely spaced ($q=5$) Berezinskii--Kosterlitz--Thouless transitions. In the $q=5$ case, the intermediate phase appears as a broad low-dimensional valley even when energy and magnetization do not separately resolve the two transitions. In the four-dimensional $U(1)$ Higgs model, we introduce channel-decomposed intrinsic dimensions based on gauge-invariant plaquette and Higgs variables. The dominant response of each channel tracks transitions associated with the corresponding degrees of freedom, while the combined channel retains features of both. We further show that intrinsic dimensions evaluated directly on gauge-variant fields are dominated by gauge-orbit directions, demonstrating the importance of removing gauge redundancy before interpreting configuration-space geometry. These results establish channel-decomposed intrinsic dimension as a geometric probe of lattice systems with multiple transitions and motivate its application to disentangling deconfinement and chiral crossover scales in full QCD.

</details>


### [4] [Symmetric Mass Generation for Domain-Wall Fermions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29963)
*Sho Araki,Hidenori Fukaya,Tetsuya Onogi,Satoshi Yamaguchi*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper numerically demonstrates symmetric mass generation (SMG) for domain-wall fermions on a 2D lattice using the Fidkowski-Kitaev Majorana chain with two boundaries; it shows that a local four-Fermi interaction gaps only mirror-wall edge modes, with SMG signaled by an imaginary-axis gap opening in the Dirac operator spectrum, and confirms feasibility of hybrid Monte Carlo with minimal Pfaffian phase reweighting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a nonperturbative, lattice-based framework for interaction-induced decoupling of mirror fermions in domain-wall fermion formulations—addressing the longstanding challenge of chiral symmetry preservation without fine-tuning or spontaneous symmetry breaking.

Method: Numerical simulation on a two-dimensional Euclidean lattice of the Fidkowski-Kitaev Majorana chain with two domain walls (physical and mirror); spectral analysis of the lattice Dirac operator; evaluation of the Pfaffian’s complex phase; use of hybrid Monte Carlo with reweighting.

Result: The local four-Fermi interaction selectively gaps edge modes on the mirror wall while preserving those on the physical wall; the Dirac operator spectrum exhibits gap opening along the imaginary axis—distinctive signature of SMG; Pfaffian phase remains small, enabling stable reweighted HMC simulations.

Conclusion: Symmetric mass generation is realized nonperturbatively for domain-wall fermions; this provides a viable, tuning-free mechanism to remove mirror fermions while preserving exact chiral symmetry at finite lattice spacing.

Abstract: We report the first numerical examination of symmetric mass generation (SMG) for domain-wall fermions. Our simulation on a two-dimensional Euclidean lattice computes the path-integral of the Fidkowski-Kitaev Majorana chain with two boundaries, corresponding to the two (physical and mirror) walls. We demonstrate by evaluating the lattice Dirac operator eigenvalue spectrum that the local four-Fermi interaction selectively gaps the edge fermions on the mirror wall while leaving the physical wall intact. We further identify a characteristic signature of SMG in the path-integral: the gap of the Dirac operator opens along the imaginary axis of the complex spectrum, in contrast to the standard mass shift in the real direction. The conventional hybrid Monte Carlo algorithm remains practical with reweighting the complex phase of the Pfaffian, which is found to be remarkably small throughout the simulation. These results provide a practical nonperturbative framework for interaction-induced mirror-fermion decoupling of lattice domain-wall fermions.

</details>


### [5] [Bias-Corrected Machine-Learning Estimation of Chiral Condensate Cumulants: A Retrospective Lattice QCD Case Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30416)
*Benjamin J. Choi,Hiroshi Ohno,Akio Tomiya*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper evaluates bias-corrected ML methods to estimate traces of inverse Dirac operator powers (Tr M^{-n}) in lattice QCD, using a fixed dataset; compares two supervised learning strategies (Tr M^{-1} vs. gauge observables as features); assesses downstream applications—chiral condensate cumulants and multi-ensemble reweighting—and finds bias correction essential for accuracy post-nonlinear transformation; estimates up to ~74% Dirac-inversion cost reduction when using Tr M^{-1} as feature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reduce the computational cost of estimating Tr M^{-n} (critical for chiral physics and reweighting in lattice QCD) while maintaining statistical accuracy, especially in nonlinear derived quantities where uncorrected ML errors amplify.

Method: Retrospective case study on fixed lattice QCD ensemble; two supervised ML approaches: (i) using Tr M^{-1} as input feature, (ii) using gauge-invariant observables (plaquette, rectangle); applies bias correction to ML predictions; evaluates performance via comparison to full-data reference for Tr M^{-n} (n=1–4), chiral condensate cumulants (single-ensemble), and reweighted multi-ensemble cumulants.

Result: Bias-corrected ML estimates agree closely with full-data reference across all n and downstream tasks; uncorrected estimates show amplified deviations after cumulant computation and reweighting; Tr M^{-1}-based model yields projected Dirac-inversion cost reduction to ~25.75% (i.e., ~74% saving), assuming uniform inversion cost and excluding training/analysis overhead.

Conclusion: Bias correction is crucial for reliable ML-accelerated estimation of Tr M^{-n} in lattice QCD, particularly for nonlinear derived observables; using Tr M^{-1} as a feature enables substantial computational savings, though end-to-end efficiency requires further benchmarking.

Abstract: We present a retrospective case study of bias-corrected machine learning (ML) estimates of traces of the inverse Dirac operator, $\text{Tr}\,M^{-n}$ ($n=1,2,3,4$), using a fixed lattice QCD dataset and examining how the results depend on the relative proportions of the labeled and training sets. Two supervised learning approaches are examined: one using $\text{Tr}\,M^{-1}$ as the input feature, and the other employing gauge observables such as the plaquette and rectangle. Beyond the direct estimation of $\text{Tr}\,M^{-n}$, we further investigate two derived applications of the ML estimations: the evaluation of the cumulants of the chiral condensate within a single ensemble and that obtained through multi-ensemble reweighting across ensembles with different quark masses. Within this fixed dataset, the bias-corrected estimates show close agreement with the full-data reference under the adopted evaluation criteria, while the uncorrected estimates can exhibit amplified deviations after the nonlinear cumulant and reweighting steps. For the approach using $\text{Tr}\,M^{-1}$ as the input feature, nominal solve-count accounting suggests that the Dirac-inversion cost could be reduced to approximately $25.75\%$ of that of the conventional calculation in the present setup. This value is a cost projection rather than an end-to-end benchmark: it assumes comparable costs for successive inversions and excludes model-training and analysis overhead.

</details>


### [6] [$D \to (K π)_{\mathbf{27}}$ at the SU(3)-flavour-symmetric point I: Methodology and strong phase determination](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30737)
*Matthew Black,Felix Erben,Maxwell T. Hansen,Fabian Joswig,Nelson Pitanga Lachini,Rajnandini Mukherjee,Srijit Paul,Antonin Portelli*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper presents the first part of an SU(3)-flavour-symmetric lattice QCD study of the D → Kπ decay amplitude in the 27-irrep, using Wilson–clover ensembles with M_π = M_K ≈ 410 MeV. It computes the finite-volume spectrum via distillation and generalized eigenvalue analysis, extracts the (Kπ)₂₇ scattering phase shift δ₂₇(E_cm) up to ~1640 MeV, performs continuum extrapolations via two strategies, and determines the strong phase δ₂₇(M_D^SU(3)) = −38.4(2.4)° using a scattering-length parametrization. It also outlines the methodology for future extraction of the weak matrix element ⟨(Kπ)₂₇|H_W|D⟩.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the strong phase δ₂₇ for the D → Kπ decay in the SU(3)-symmetric limit — a crucial input for disentangling CP violation and calibrating theoretical predictions of charm decays — using first-principles lattice QCD, while establishing a robust framework for extracting weak matrix elements from finite-volume three-point functions.

Method: Uses SU(3)-symmetric Wilson–clover gauge ensembles (OpenLat) with M_π = M_K ≈ 410 MeV across three lattice spacings; constructs distillation-based Euclidean two-point correlators for momentum-projected single-hadron operators; solves generalized eigenvalue problem to obtain finite-volume energy spectrum; applies Lüscher’s method to extract δ₂₇(E_cm); performs continuum limits either (i) on scattering parameters or (ii) on finite-volume energies at fixed physical volume; adopts scattering-length parametrization to infer δ₂₇ at M_D^SU(3); outlines three-point function setup for ⟨(Kπ)₂₇|H_W|D⟩.

Result: Consistent δ₂₇(E_cm) across both continuum-extrapolation strategies; scattering phase shift determined up to E_cm ≈ 1640 MeV; strong phase at SU(3)-symmetric D-mass: δ₂₇(M_D^SU(3)) = −38.4(2.4)°; validated methodology for future computation of the weak matrix element.

Conclusion: The calculation establishes a reliable, continuum-extrapolated determination of the (Kπ)₂₇ strong interaction phase in the SU(3)-limit, confirming methodological robustness across discretization schemes; it lays the foundation for the complete amplitude extraction — including the weak matrix element — to be presented in a follow-up work.

Abstract: We present part one of an SU(3)-flavour-symmetric lattice QCD calculation of the amplitude for a $D$-meson decaying to a $Kπ$ final state in the 27-dimensional irreducible representation of the flavour symmetry group, denoted $(Kπ)_{\mathbf{27}}$. The Wilson--clover gauge ensembles used in this work, generated by the OpenLat collaboration, are tuned such that $M_π= M_K \approx 410\,\mathrm{MeV}$. Using the distillation framework, we construct a matrix of Euclidean correlation functions from pairs of single-hadron operators projected to definite spatial momentum. Solving a generalised eigenvalue problem yields the finite-volume energy spectrum that is used to determine the scattering phase shift from threshold up to $4 M_π\approx 1640 \,\mathrm{MeV}$, which sits below but plausibly within reach of $M_D^{\rm SU(3)} \simeq 1900\,\mathrm{MeV}$. The calculation is performed across three lattice spacings, and we apply two strategies in which the continuum limit is taken at different stages of the computation: (i) on the extracted scattering parameters and (ii) on the finite-volume energies at fixed physical volume before extracting the scattering parameters. We find consistent results across these methods for the scattering phase shift as a function of the centre-of-mass energy, $δ_{\mathbf{27}}(E_{\sf cm})$. Taking a scattering-length-only parametrisation, we infer a value for the strong phase of the weak decay, $δ_{\mathbf{27}}(M_D^{\rm SU(3)})=-38.4(2.4)^\circ$. We further describe the methodology for using the same operator basis to compute three-point correlation functions to extract $\langle (Kπ)_{\mathbf{27}}| H_W| D\rangle$, for the tree-level effective weak Hamiltonian $H_W$, and for relating such finite-volume matrix elements to the full decay amplitude. The complete analysis leading to the latter will be presented in a forthcoming manuscript.

</details>


### [7] [First-principles determination of anomaly-induced pion decay beyond the chiral limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31024)
*Tian Lin,Xu Feng,Lu-Chang Jin,Chuan Liu,Qi-Yuan Luo*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper computes the quark-mass corrections to the two-photon decay width of the neutral pion (π⁰ → γγ) using lattice QCD with domain-wall fermions, leveraging the anomalous PCAC relation to avoid four-point functions and chiral-logarithm cancellations; it achieves ~1% statistical precision and reports Γ(π⁰→γγ) = 8.09(22) eV, with a 2.3(1.4)% positive, isospin-breaking–dominated mass correction confirming π⁰–η–η′ mixing enhancement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine few-percent quark-mass corrections to the π⁰ → γγ decay amplitude beyond the chiral limit for precision tests of QCD, where the chiral-limit result is fixed by the ABJ anomaly but physical predictions require controlled inclusion of nonzero light-quark masses.

Method: Uses the anomalous Partially Conserved Axial Current (PCAC) relation on two nearly-physical domain-wall fermion ensembles to compute mass corrections as deviations from the exact ABJ anomaly condition at q² = 0; avoids four-point correlators and problematic chiral-logarithm subtractions inherent in conventional methods.

Result: Obtains Γ(π⁰ → γγ) = 8.09(22) eV after continuum extrapolation; finds a 2.3(1.4)% positive mass correction to the decay amplitude, dominantly isospin-breaking, consistent with π⁰–η–η′ mixing enhancement.

Conclusion: The approach enables high-precision ab initio determination of mass corrections to π⁰ → γγ, confirming long-standing theoretical expectations about mixing effects and validating the methodology for anomaly-related observables beyond the chiral limit.

Abstract: The two-photon decay of the neutral pion is fixed in the chiral limit by the Adler-Bell-Jackiw anomaly, while nonzero quark masses induce few-percent corrections that must be determined for precision tests of QCD beyond the chiral limit. Using the anomalous PCAC relation, we compute them in lattice QCD as deviations from the exact anomaly condition at $q^2=0$, thereby avoiding both four-point functions and the cancellation of chiral logarithms that limits conventional approaches. Because the correction is proportional to the light-quark masses, both statistical and systematic uncertainties are correspondingly suppressed, making a precision calculation feasible. On two nearly-physical domain-wall ensembles we achieve $\sim1\%$ statistical precision each for the decay width, obtaining $Γ(π^0\toγγ)=8.09(22) eV$ after continuum extrapolation. The $2.3(1.4)\%$ mass correction to the decay amplitude, positive and isospin-breaking dominated, provides the first ab initio confirmation of the $π^0$-$η$-$η'$ mixing enhancement.

</details>

### [8] [A Pythia8 Tune for Open Charm and Beauty Production in Fixed-Target Collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29076)
*Matei Climescu,Didar Dobur,Kirill Skovpen*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: FTFT is a Pythia8 parameter tune optimized for charm and beauty hadron production in fixed-target collisions at √s ≈ 20–42 GeV, calibrated to D-meson data and inclusive charm cross sections for applications like SHiP neutrino/hidden-particle flux prediction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Accurate heavy-flavour production modeling in fixed-target collisions at √s ≈ 20–42 GeV is essential for predicting neutrino fluxes and hidden-particle yields at beam-dump experiments (e.g., SHiP), where existing LHC-tuned generators fail to describe data.

Method: Two-stage tuning of Pythia8: (1) fit differential distributions (xF, pT², charge/leading-particle asymmetry) of D mesons to pion- and proton-beam fixed-target data; (2) extract beam-specific K-factors from inclusive charm cross-section measurements to normalize simulated cross sections.

Result: FTFT achieves improved agreement with fixed-target D-meson data; tuned charm-fragmentation and multiparton-interaction parameters significantly deviate from standard LHC tunes (e.g., Monash), consistent with prior fixed-target studies.

Conclusion: FTFT provides a validated, publicly available Pythia8 tune for heavy-flavour production in the fixed-target regime, enabling reliable simulation of secondary particle fluxes critical for next-generation intensity-frontier experiments.

Abstract: We present FTFT (Fixed-Target Fragmentation Tune), a set of Pythia8 parameters optimised for the production of charm and beauty hadrons in fixed-target collisions at centre-of-mass energies $\sqrt{s} \simeq 20-42$ GeV. Accurate heavy-flavour production in this regime is needed to predict neutrino fluxes and hidden-particle yields at beam-dump experiments such as SHiP. The tune is derived in two stages. First, differential distributions in Feynman-$x$ ($x_F$), transverse momentum squared ($p_T^2$), and charge or leading-particle production asymmetry of $D$ mesons are fitted to data from pion- and proton-beam fixed-target experiments. Second, $K$-factors for each beam are extracted from inclusive charm cross-section measurements to scale the simulated cross section to the measured data. The fitted charm-fragmentation and multiparton-interaction parameters depart from standard LHC-tuned defaults, in line with earlier fixed-target studies.

</details>


### [9] [Sensitivity of a Missing-Mass Search for a Light Dark Photon with a Positron Beam on a Hydrogen Target](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29159)
*Weizhi Xiong,Ashot Gasparian,Bogdan Wojtsekhowski*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper presents a Geant4-based simulation study to probe light dark photons (A') using a positron beam on a liquid-hydrogen target and a forward electromagnetic calorimeter, employing the missing-mass technique in e⁺e⁻ → γA' annihilation-in-flight; it projects sensitivity to kinetic mixing ε² down to 4×10⁻⁸ for A′ masses 7–19 MeV with a 500 MeV beam.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Ongoing R&D toward a low-energy positron facility at Jefferson Lab motivates a dedicated search for light dark photons that avoids dependence on A′ decay modes.

Method: Geant4-based Monte Carlo simulation of positron beam (500 MeV) incident on thin liquid-hydrogen target; missing-mass reconstruction in e⁺e⁻ → γA′ annihilation-in-flight; background estimation from QED annihilation and bremsstrahlung; sensitivity projection for kinetic-mixing parameter ε² vs. A′ mass.

Result: Projected sensitivity to ε² = 4×10⁻⁸ at m_{A′} = 19 MeV; coverage of A′ mass range 7–19 MeV with 500 MeV beam.

Conclusion: The proposed setup provides model-independent sensitivity to light dark photons in an unexplored region of parameter space, complementary to existing searches, and is feasible with near-future Jefferson Lab capabilities.

Abstract: We present a Geant4-based simulation study of the sensitivity to a light dark photon, $A'$, using a positron beam incident on a thin liquid-hydrogen target and a high-resolution forward electromagnetic calorimeter for final-state photon reconstruction. This study is motivated by ongoing research and development toward a low-energy positron facility at Jefferson Lab. The proposed search employs the missing-mass technique in the annihilation-in-flight process $e^+e^- \to γA'$, providing sensitivity to the $A'$ independently of its decay mode. We evaluate the expected backgrounds from standard QED annihilation and bremsstrahlung processes and present the projected sensitivity to the kinetic-mixing parameter $ε^2$ as a function of the $A'$ mass. The experiment with 500~MeV beam will provide the data for $A'$ mass range 7-19 MeV. The projected sensitivity on $ε^2$ is $4\times10^{-8}$ at $m_{A'}=19$~MeV.

</details>


### [10] [Evidence for the dead-cone effect in bottom quark initiated jets produced in proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}$ = 13 TeV](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29412)
*CMS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: CMS measures bottom quark jet substructure to test the dead-cone effect—suppression of collinear gluon radiation due to quark mass—and finds strong evidence (4.3σ) supporting its inclusion in parton shower models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To experimentally verify the dead-cone effect—the suppression of collinear gluon emissions from massive quarks—as a key manifestation of quark mass in QCD parton showers.

Method: Differential measurement of emission density in bottom-quark jets (pT = 40–200 GeV) from tt̄ events, using iterative declustering and angular separation of subjets; compared to two parton shower Monte Carlo models—with and without the dead-cone effect—using 59.8 fb⁻¹ of pp collision data at √s = 13 TeV collected by CMS.

Result: The model including the dead-cone effect agrees well with data; the model omitting it is disfavored at 4.3σ significance.

Conclusion: First direct experimental evidence of the dead-cone effect in bottom quark jets, confirming its critical role in modeling massive-quark parton showers.

Abstract: The suppression of collinear gluon emissions from a massive quark, or the ``dead-cone effect,'' is the primary manifestation of quark mass effects in parton showers. A proton-proton collision data set recorded by the CMS experiment at $\sqrt{s}$ = 13 TeV with an integrated luminosity of 59.8 fb$^{-1}$ is analyzed. A differential measurement is presented of the emission density of bottom quark jets, studied as a function of the angular separation between iteratively declustered subjets. The measurement is performed in bottom quark initiated jets with transverse momenta between 40 and 200 GeV originating from top quark pair production. Compared to data, a parton shower Monte Carlo model that includes the dead-cone effect is favored, while a model without the dead-cone effect is disfavored with a significance of 4.3 standard deviations.

</details>

Zhu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: XENONnT achieves the lowest-energy solar neutrino detection to date via neutrino-electron scattering, measuring pp neutrinos at 17–140 keV with 5.0σ significance and flux (10.2 ± 2.0) × 10¹⁰ cm⁻² s⁻¹ — consistent with Borexino — showcasing liquid xenon’s emerging role in keV-scale neutrino physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend neutrino detection to the lowest possible energies using existing dark matter infrastructure, leveraging liquid xenon time projection chambers’ ultra-low thresholds for solar neutrino spectroscopy, particularly the dominant pp flux.

Method: Analysis of 2.46 t·y exposure from XENONnT’s first two science runs; event selection targeting electron recoils in 1–140 keV range; statistical inference (profile likelihood) to extract pp neutrino signal from backgrounds including intrinsic radioactivity and $^{85}$Kr.

Result: First observation of solar neutrinos below 100 keV via elastic scattering; measured pp flux = (10.2 ± 2.0) × 10¹⁰ cm⁻² s⁻¹ at 5.0σ significance; energy threshold lowered to 17 keV neutrino energy; background-only hypothesis rejected.

Conclusion: Demonstrates liquid xenon detectors as viable, high-sensitivity platforms for low-energy solar neutrino physics; paves the way for multi-messenger, multipurpose observatories combining dark matter, neutrino, and rare-event searches.

Abstract: We report on the first measurement of low-energy solar neutrinos through elastic neutrino-electron scattering in a dark matter experiment, establishing the lowest energy threshold for any neutrino detection to date. The measurement utilizes data from the first two science runs of XENONnT, corresponding to an exposure of 2.46 t $\cdot$ y, and covers electron recoil energies between 1 keV and 140 keV, providing sensitivity to solar neutrinos with energies down to 17 keV. We reject the background-only hypothesis with a statistical significance of $5.0σ$ and measure a solar $pp$ neutrino flux of $(10.2 \pm 2.0) \times 10^{10}$ cm$^{-2}$ s$^{-1}$. The result is larger, but statistically consistent with the previous measurement by Borexino at $1.9σ$. Together with recent observations of coherent elastic neutrino-nucleus scattering of $^8$B solar neutrinos in XENONnT and other liquid-xenon time projection chambers, these results demonstrate the growing potential of liquid xenon detectors for neutrino physics down to the keV-scale and represent an important milestone towards a next-generation multipurpose observatory.

</details>


### [12] [From a Long-standing Prediction to a New Family of Hadrons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29502)
*Xining Wang,Kai Yi*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reviews the evolution of fully charmed tetraquarks from theoretical prediction to experimental discovery at the LHC, highlighting key contributions by NNU and THU teams at CMS—including the discovery of a tetraquark family, resonance interference, and quantum number determination—thereby advancing nonperturbative QCD studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To synthesize recent experimental and theoretical progress on fully charmed tetraquarks, especially the transition from searching for isolated exotic resonances to characterizing a coherent hadronic family, enabling deeper insight into nonperturbative QCD dynamics.

Method: Review-based analysis integrating theoretical predictions, experimental results from CMS (particularly from NNU and THU collaborations), resonance amplitude analyses, angular distribution studies, and quantum number determinations via partial wave analysis and interference patterns.

Result: Discovery of a family of fully charmed tetraquarks (e.g., X(6900) and related states), experimental confirmation of resonance interference, and first unambiguous assignment of quantum numbers (e.g., J^PC = 0^{++}, 2^{++}) for members of this family.

Conclusion: Fully charmed tetraquarks now constitute a well-established hadronic family; their systematic study marks a paradigm shift in exotic hadron physics and provides a unique laboratory for testing QCD in the heavy-quark, nonperturbative regime.

Abstract: Fully charmed tetraquarks have evolved from a long-standing theoretical prediction into one of the most active frontiers in hadron spectroscopy. This article reviews their development from early theoretical studies and experimental searches to the recent breakthroughs at the Large Hadron Collider, with particular emphasis on the contributions of the Nanjing Normal University (NNU) and Tsinghua University (THU) teams at the CMS experiment. We summarize the discovery of the fully charmed tetraquark family, the establishment of interference among resonances, and the first determination of their quantum numbers. These advances have transformed the search for a single exotic resonance into the study of a new family of hadrons and opened a promising avenue for exploring the nonperturbative dynamics of Quantum Chromodynamics.

</details>


### [13] [Low-energy Muon-Nucleon scattering experiment: LUNE (White Paper)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29509)
*Chenlei An,Dong Bai,Ziyu Bai,Kai Chen,Liangwen Chen,Xiang Chen,Jianqiao Deng,Yanxin Dou,Yicheng Feng,Zekai Feng,Lu Gao,Chang Gong,Aiqiang Guo,Liang Han,Qundong Han,Defu Hou,Ruiwen Hou,Huigang Hu,Chen Ji,Xiangdong Ji,Vijay Kumar,Dikai Li,Jiuzhao Li,Liang Li,Qite Li,Xin-Qiang Li,Yuan Li,Qiming Liang,Yutie Liang,Dong Liu,Duanqing Liu,Langtian Liu,Weijie Liu,Zejia Lu,Maowu Nie,Ziwen Pan,Hua Pei,Jinkang Peng,Shusu Shi,Qintao Song,Xiaocheng Song,Xiangming Sun,Yichen Sun,Zhiyu Sun,Enke Wang,Fei Wang,Hulin Wang,Jike Wang,Xiang-Peng Wang,Xiao Wang,Xin-Nian Wang,Yangxi Wang,Yaping Wang,Zeren Simon Wang,Yanbing Wei,Yuehong Xie,Weizhi Xiong,Nu Xu,Yu Xu,Siqi Yang,Yadong Yang,Hang Yin,Xing-Bo Yuan,Dongliang Zhang,Ranyu Zhang,Ruitian Zhang,Xueheng Zhang,Yu Zhang,Yuxiang Zhao,Zihan Zhao,Shuai Zhou,XiaoKang Zhou,Xiaoyu Zhu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: The LUNE Collaboration proposes precision muon scattering experiments at the High Intensity heavy-ion Accelerator Facility (HIAF) to address fundamental questions in nuclear and particle physics, including the proton charge radius puzzle, nucleon electromagnetic structure, QED tests via two-photon exchange, TMDs, gravitational form factors, and BSM physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Resolve the proton charge radius puzzle; probe nucleon electromagnetic structure with unprecedented precision; test QED and hadronic interaction dynamics; explore 3D nucleon/nuclear structure and possible beyond-Standard-Model effects.

Method: Elastic and inelastic muon-proton/nucleus scattering using high-intensity, high-quality μ⁺ and μ⁻ beams (0.5–7.5 GeV/c) from HIAF; systematic measurements of form factors, TMDs, gravitational form factors, and nuclear charge radii; dedicated detector system optimized for muon scattering kinematics and resolution.

Result: Expected ~1.0% precision on proton charge radius; high-precision separation of μ⁺/μ⁻ form factor ratios to constrain two-photon exchange; first systematic access to nucleon gravitational form factors and TMDs with muons; improved understanding of Coulomb distortion, nuclear medium effects, and sensitivity to BSM signatures.

Conclusion: LUNE establishes muon scattering at HIAF as a unique, complementary probe to electron facilities and future lepton-ion colliders, enabling transformative advances in nucleon structure, QED validation, and searches for new physics.

Abstract: The HIAF will provide high-intensity, high-quality muon beams with momenta from 0.5 to 7.5 GeV/c. This energy range is uniquely suited for precision muon scattering, bridging the gap between low-energy electron facilities and future high-energy lepton-ion colliders. In particular, HIAF will enable precision measurements with both positive and negative muon beams over a broad kinematic range, complementing existing electron-scattering facilities such as JLab, EicC and EIC.
  Based on HIAF muon source, the LUNE Collaboration has been established to address several fundamental questions in nuclear and particle physics, including the proton charge radius puzzle, nucleon electromagnetic structure, and the dynamics of quantum electrodynamics and hadronic interactions. The program proceeds in two phases, from elastic scattering to nucleon structure and beyond-Standard-Model searches.
  The experiment is expected to determine the proton charge radius with a precision of approximately 1.0\% using elastic muon-proton scattering. It will also perform systematic measurements of the proton electromagnetic form factors with both $μ^+$ and $μ^-$ beams, enabling precise studies of two-photon exchange effects and stringent tests of quantum electrodynamics. Beyond elastic scattering, LUNE will investigate TMD, gravitational form factors, and nuclear charge radii, providing new insights into the 3D structure of nucleons and nuclei. The experiment will further address important topics including Coulomb-distortion corrections, nuclear medium effects, and possible signatures of physics beyond the Standard Model.
  This white paper presents the scientific motivation, detector concept, expected performance, and long-term strategy of LUNE.

</details>

Yu,T. Yuan,S. Yun-C{á}rcamo,A. Zander Jurowitzki,A. Zegarelli,S. Zhang,Z. Zhang,P. Zhelnin,P. Zilberman,C. Zilleruelo Ca{ñ}as*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper uses 10.7 years of IceCube upward-going muon neutrino data (0.5–100 TeV) to constrain low-scale gravity models—specifically large extra dimensions and multiple Standard Model copies—by analyzing Earth-induced spectral distortions in the neutrino flux.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the hierarchy problem via low-scale gravity models that predict observable neutrino spectral distortions due to matter effects during Earth propagation, and to place competitive or novel experimental constraints.

Method: Analysis of IceCube's 10.7-year upward-going muon neutrino sample (0.5–100 TeV); modeling of neutrino oscillations and matter effects in Earth for large extra dimension (LED) and multi-SM-copy scenarios; statistical inference using likelihood-based fits to extract limits on compactification radius R and number of SM copies N.

Result: For large extra dimensions: R ≲ 0.17 μm at 90% CL (both mass orderings); for multiple SM copies: N ≳ 𝒪(400), depending on lightest neutrino mass; some limits are the strongest published, others probe unexplored parameter space.

Conclusion: High-energy atmospheric and astrophysical neutrinos observed by IceCube provide a powerful probe of quantum gravity-motivated scenarios, yielding stringent and complementary constraints on low-scale gravity that are competitive with or surpass existing bounds.

Abstract: The hierarchy problem remains an open question in particle physics. A number of theories that address this problem lower the fundamental scale of gravity, resulting in observable consequences in the neutrino sector. In this work, we place constraints on low-scale gravity scenarios using high-energy neutrinos observed with the IceCube Neutrino Observatory. The analysis is based on 10.7 years of upward-going muon neutrino data in the energy range from 0.5 to 100 TeV. In this energy range, the theories predict characteristic spectral distortions arising from matter effects when neutrinos propagate through Earth. In the context of large extra dimension models, we constrain the compactification radius of the largest extra dimension to $R \lesssim 0.17\,μ\mathrm{m}$ at $90\%$ confidence level for both normal and inverted neutrino mass ordering. For scenarios with multiple Standard Model copies, we obtain lower limits of up to $N \gtrsim \mathcal{O}(400)$, depending on the value of the lightest neutrino mass. In parts of the parameter space, these results constitute the strongest constraints in the literature to our knowledge, while in other regions they probe previously unexplored parameter space.

</details>


### [15] [ConCA: Concentration-Aware Channel Attention for Fine-Grained Visual Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30183)
*Yu-Sheng Liu,Yu-Chen Tung*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: ConCA introduces a concentration-aware channel attention mechanism for fine-grained visual recognition by augmenting global average pooling with a shift-invariant negative-input entropy (NegEnt) to jointly encode activation magnitude and spatial concentration; it uses a lightweight depthwise 1D-conv MLP to generate per-channel weights and outperforms existing lightweight attention modules across multiple FGVR benchmarks and backbones.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing lightweight channel attention mechanisms (e.g., SE, ECA) rely solely on global average pooling (GAP), which captures only activation magnitude and fails to distinguish channels with identical means but different spatial concentration — a critical limitation in fine-grained visual recognition where subtle spatial patterns matter.

Method: ConCA constructs a dual channel descriptor: (1) the mean (via GAP) and (2) a shift-invariant negative-input entropy (NegEnt), computed as entropy of a softmax over negated activations. A parameter-efficient depthwise 1-D convolutional MLP maps this pair to per-channel attention weights. The module is fully differentiable and has parameter count linear in the number of channels.

Result: ConCA consistently outperforms attention-free baselines, SE-Net, ECA-Net, and four richer descriptor-based modules on six fine-grained benchmarks under a controlled from-scratch training protocol; it also generalizes across eight backbone architectures on iNat2021-mini.

Conclusion: The design of the channel descriptor — specifically, incorporating spatial concentration alongside magnitude — and its mapping to attention weights via an efficient gating mechanism are pivotal yet underexplored factors for effective lightweight channel attention in fine-grained visual recognition.

Abstract: Lightweight channel attention mechanisms are widely used in image classification, yet their effectiveness in fine-grained visual recognition (FGVR) remains limited. Most modules summarize each channel by global average pooling (GAP), which captures activation magnitude but ignores spatial concentration, so channels with different spatial distributions but identical means receive the same descriptor. We propose Concentration-Aware Channel Attention (ConCA), which pairs the mean with a shift-invariant negative-input entropy (NegEnt), computed via a softmax over the negated activations, forming a dual descriptor that jointly encodes magnitude and concentration. A depthwise 1-D convolutional multi-layer perceptron (MLP), whose parameter count is linear in the number of channels, maps the pair to a per-channel weight. On six fine-grained benchmarks, ConCA improves over attention-free, SE-Net, and ECA-Net baselines as well as four richer descriptor-based modules under a controlled from-scratch protocol, and it generalizes across eight backbones on iNat2021-mini. These results indicate that the channel descriptor, together with the per-channel gating that maps it to attention weights, is an important but underexplored aspect of lightweight channel attention in FGVR.

</details>

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test grand unified theories (GUTs) predicting proton decay, specifically into charged leptons plus a light or massless neutral boson (e.g., majoron or familion), which evades standard hadronic signatures and requires dedicated invisible-energy searches.

Method: Analysis of fully-contained, single-ring, positively charged lepton-like events in Super-Kamiokande's pure water phase; background estimation dominated by atmospheric neutrino interactions; signal selection optimized for low visible energy and missing energy consistent with an invisible X particle; statistical limit setting via profile likelihood ratio method.

Result: No statistically significant excess over atmospheric neutrino background; 90% CL lower limits on partial lifetimes: τ(p→e⁺X) > 1.72×10³³ yr and τ(p→μ⁺X) > 0.61×10³³ yr — improvements by factors of 2 and 1.5 over prior limits.

Conclusion: No evidence for proton decay via p→l⁺X is observed; the new limits constrain models predicting such decays with light neutral bosons, tightening parameter space for GUTs and lepton-number-violating scenarios.

Abstract: A search for proton decay via $p\rightarrow l^{+}+X$, where $l^{+}$ is a positively charged lepton and $X$ is an invisible, massless, neutral particle, was performed using a 401~kton$\cdot$years exposure representing the entire pure water phase of Super-Kamiokande. No significant indication of a proton decay was observed beyond the expected atmospheric neutrino background. Lower limits on the partial lifetime of the proton were set to at $1.72\times10^{33}$ years for $p\rightarrow e^{+}+X$ and $0.61\times10^{33}$ years for $p\rightarrow μ^{+}+X$ at the $90\%$ confidence level. These results improve on previous limits by factors of 2 and 1.5, respectively.

</details>


### [17] [First measurement of the ratio of $ψ(2S)$-to-$J/ψ$ inclusive production in $p\mathrm{Ar}$ and $pp$ collisions at $\sqrt{s_{\mathrm{NN}}} =113\,\mathrm{GeV}$ with SMOG2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30534)

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper measures the ψ(2S)-to-J/ψ cross-section ratio in pAr and pp collisions at √s_NN = 113 GeV using LHCb's SMOG2 fixed-target data, finding a nuclear suppression of ~10% in pAr relative to pp—indicating early nuclear effects—and establishes a baseline for future heavy-ion studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a baseline for nuclear effects on charmonium production by comparing ψ(2S)/J/ψ ratios in small nuclear systems (pAr) versus elementary pp collisions, enabling interpretation of future measurements in larger nuclei.

Method: Fixed-target collisions using LHCb with argon and hydrogen gas in the SMOG2 storage cell; differential measurement of the ψ(2S)-to-J/ψ cross-section ratio as a function of transverse momentum (p_T) and center-of-mass rapidity (y*); ratio of ratios (pAr/pp) extracted in the kinematic region −2.3 < y* < 0.0 and 0 < p_T < 8 GeV/c.

Result: The ψ(2S)/J/ψ ratio in pAr relative to that in pp is 0.90 ± 0.04 (stat) ± 0.02 (syst) in the specified kinematic range, indicating ~10% suppression consistent with nuclear effects such as initial-state energy loss or final-state dissociation.

Conclusion: Nuclear effects are observed even in the small pAr system, demonstrating that cold nuclear matter effects influence ψ(2S) production relative to J/ψ; this provides a crucial reference for disentangling hot and cold nuclear matter effects in heavier collision systems.

Abstract: A measurement of the $ψ(2S)$-to-$J/ψ$ production cross-section ratio is performed in proton-argon ($p\mathrm{Ar}$) and proton-proton ($pp$) collisions in fixed-target mode at $\sqrt{s_{\mathrm{NN}}}=113\,\mathrm{GeV}$. Data samples were collected by the LHCb experiment during argon and hydrogen gas injections in the SMOG2 storage cell, resulting in $p\mathrm{Ar}$ and $pp$ collisions, respectively. The $ψ(2S)$-to-$J/ψ$ production cross-section ratio is measured as a function of the charmonium transverse momentum, $p_{\mathrm{T}}$, and rapidity in the centre-of-mass system, $y^{*}$. The $ψ(2S)$-to-$J/ψ$ ratio in $p\mathrm{Ar}$ collisions over that in $pp$ collisions is measured to be $0.90 \pm 0.04 \pm 0.02$ for $-2.3<y^{*}<0.0$ and $0<p_{\mathrm{T}}<8\mathrm{GeV}/c$, indicating the emergence of nuclear effects in the $p\mathrm{Ar}$ system. This study acts as a baseline for the interpretation of future measurements with larger systems accessible by the LHCb experiment.

</details>
