# math-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [18] [The $\mathbb{Z}_2$-Index of a Pair of Pure States and the Topology of Interacting 1D Superconductors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28796)
*Anna Mazhar,Jacob Shapiro*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a relative Z2-index for parity-invariant pure states on C*-algebras, applies it to define a many-body Majorana number for the self-dual CAR algebra, shows agreement with the single-body index in the quasi-free Hilbert–Schmidt regime, and uses it to classify automorphic-path-components under symmetric local automorphisms—capturing bulk topology but potentially missing zero-dimensional parity obstructions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the notion of the Majorana number beyond free-fermion systems to interacting many-body settings while preserving topological classification robustness under locality and symmetry constraints.

Method: Constructs a relative Z2-index N(ω₁,ω₂) for locally-comparable, parity-invariant pure states on unital C*-algebras; applies it to the half-chain automorphism σ on the 1D self-dual CAR algebra; defines the many-body Majorana number Nσ(ω) = N(ω, ω∘σ); verifies consistency with the single-body index in the quasi-free Hilbert–Schmidt regime; introduces symmetric local automorphism paths and proves classification via Nσ.

Result: The relative Z2-index is well-defined, multiplicative, invariant under parity-preserving automorphisms, and locally constant in norm topology; the many-body Majorana number Nσ(ω) agrees with the standard single-body Majorana number in the quasi-free Hilbert–Schmidt regime; Nσ fully classifies automorphic-path-components induced by symmetric local automorphisms.

Conclusion: The many-body Majorana number provides a robust, interacting generalization of the topological invariant for 1D fermionic symmetry-protected phases with Z2 parity symmetry, though automorphic-path equivalence may not detect certain zero-dimensional parity obstructions.

Abstract: We define a relative $\mathbb{Z}_2$-index $\mathcal{N}(ω_1,ω_2)$ for locally-comparable, parity-invariant pure states on a unital $C^*$-algebra. We prove that the index is well-defined, multiplicative, invariant under parity-preserving automorphisms, and locally constant in the norm topology. For the one-dimensional self-dual CAR algebra, we apply this construction to the half-chain automorphism $σ$ and define the many-body Majorana number $\mathcal{N}σ(ω):=\mathcal{N}(ω,ω\circσ)$ for parity-invariant $σ$-local pure states. In the quasi-free Hilbert--Schmidt regime, this index agrees with the usual single-body Majorana number. We then introduce symmetric local automorphism paths and prove that $\mathcal{N}_σ$ completely classifies the resulting automorphic-path-components. This automorphic-path equivalence retains the bulk Majorana number but may forget a relative zero-dimensional parity obstruction.

</details>


### [19] [Pseudorandomness and Diffraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28917)
*David Damanik,Nicolae Strungaru*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: The paper investigates pseudorandomness of a quasiperiodic potential V(n) from a diffraction perspective, proving its diffraction measure is purely absolutely continuous—matching random potentials—and thereby supporting the conjecture that V(n) also induces Anderson localization in the Schrödinger operator.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide evidence for the conjecture that the quasiperiodic potential V(n) exhibits Anderson localization (like random potentials), by analyzing its diffraction properties—a complementary spectral signature.

Method: Rigorous analysis of the diffraction measure associated with the potential V(n), leveraging tools from harmonic analysis and ergodic theory to establish absolute continuity for all nonzero λ, irrational α, and arbitrary x₁, x₂.

Result: The diffraction measure of V(n) is purely absolutely continuous for all λ ≠ 0, all irrational α, and all real x₁, x₂—identical to the behavior of truly random potentials.

Conclusion: V(n) is pseudorandom in the diffraction sense; this supports the broader conjecture of Anderson localization for V(n) and highlights a duality between Schrödinger spectral measures (conjectured pure point) and diffraction measures (proven absolutely continuous).

Abstract: Pseudorandom structures are structures that behave like random ones, without necessarily being random themselves. It is a prominent, and evidently very difficult, conjecture that $$ V(n) = λ\cos \Big( 2 π\Big( x_1 + n x_2 + \frac{n(n-1)}{2} α\Big) \Big) $$ is pseudorandom from a Schrödinger operator perspective in that the Schrödinger operator in $\ell^2(\mathbb{Z})$ with potential $V$ displays Anderson localization, that is, pure point spectrum with exponentially decaying eigenfunctions for almost all parameter values --- the same spectral features as those produced by random potentials. We show that $V$ is pseudorandom in terms of its diffraction properties, that is, the associated diffraction measure is purely absolutely continuous for all $λ\not= 0$, all irrational $α$, and all $x_1,x_2$ --- which is the case as well for the random case. Our result gives further evidence for the conjecture in the Schrödinger case and it elucidates the apparent dual behavior of Schrödinger spectral measures and diffraction measures.

</details>


### [20] [Simulating passive scalar advection in rough Kraichnan flows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29321)
*Long Li,André L. P. Considera,Simon Thalabard*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper conducts high-resolution Eulerian simulations of passive scalar advection in rough 2D Kraichnan flows across the full velocity roughness exponent range h ∈ (0,1), validating theoretical scaling laws, quantifying intermittency via fourth-order flatness and higher-order increment statistics, and providing practical guidelines for parameter selection—especially diffusivity scaling with h—and diagnosing finite-resolution effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically test theoretical predictions (e.g., duality, scaling laws, intermittency anomalies) for passive scalar advection in rough Kraichnan flows, bridge Lagrangian theory with Eulerian numerics, and establish robust simulation protocols for future extensions (e.g., Gaussian multiplicative chaos-coupled flows) where analytical progress is limited.

Method: Direct pseudo-spectral integration of the advection–diffusion equation on up to 2048² grids; frozen-noise Runge–Kutta time stepping consistent with white-in-time velocity; careful identification of inertial-diffusive scaling ranges; measurement of second- and fourth-order structure functions, flatness, increment PDFs, and higher-order statistics; parametric variation of molecular diffusivity with h.

Result: Recovery of flow–scalar duality and predicted second-order scaling; quantitative agreement of fourth-order flatness anomaly with prior Lagrangian and perturbative theories across all h; systematic non-Gaussian, non-log-normal increment PDFs peaking at intermediate h; explicit h-dependent diffusivity scaling rules; finite spectral resolution quantitatively explains residual deviations from ideal scaling.

Conclusion: Eulerian simulations are reliable and informative for rough Kraichnan advection when parameters—especially diffusivity—are tuned appropriately with h; the work provides both a validated numerical baseline and practical guidance for simulating more complex, analytically intractable turbulent advection models.

Abstract: We present a systematic Eulerian study of passive scalar advection in rough two-dimensional Kraichnan flows, covering the full range of velocity roughness exponent $h\in(0,1)$. The advection--diffusion equation is integrated directly using a pseudo-spectral method at resolutions up to $2048^2$ grid points, with a frozen-noise Runge--Kutta scheme consistent with the white-in-time construction of the carrier flow. The simulations recover the duality between advected scalar and advecting flow---the smoother the carrier, the rougher the scalar---together with the predicted scaling laws for the second-order statistics. Once the scaling range is properly identified, the fourth-order flatness anomaly is measured across the whole range of $h$, in agreement with the Lagrangian estimates of Frisch \textit{et al.} (1999) and with the perturbative predictions of Bernard \textit{et al.} (1998) and Pumir \textit{et al.} (1997). Benefiting from the fact that our Eulerian simulations give direct access to the full scalar field, we also examine the probability density functions of scalar increments and the corresponding higher-order statistics, which show systematic departures from Gaussianity and from log-normality, with the strongest deviations manifesting for intermediate values of $h$. A central outcome of this work is a systematic account of how the simulation parameters, in particular the molecular diffusivity, must be adjusted with $h$, providing practical guidelines for reliable simulations; we further show that the residual deviations from the theoretical scaling laws are quantitatively accounted for by the finite spectral representation of the carrier flow. Our analysis also serve as a numerical baseline for simulating more realistic extensions of the Kraichnan model, where the carrier flow is coupled with a Gaussian multiplicative chaos and theoretical developments are limited.

</details>


### [21] [Sharp Logarithmic Quantum Dynamics for Quasiperiodic Schrödinger Operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29339)
*Wencai Liu,Xueyin Wang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes sharp logarithmic lower bounds on position moments for a class of one-frequency quasiperiodic Schrödinger operators with even potentials in the positive Lyapunov exponent regime, proving optimality of previously known phase-uniform dynamical estimates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the known logarithmic-in-time upper bounds on position moments for quasiperiodic Schrödinger operators in the positive Lyapunov exponent regime are optimal—i.e., whether matching lower bounds exist uniformly in phase.

Method: Constructs a reflective variant of semi-uniformly localized eigenfunctions adapted to the two localization centers arising from completely resonant phases; uses this construction to derive logarithmic lower bounds on position moments along specific time sequences.

Result: Proves that the logarithmic growth scale and its dependence on moment order are sharp for a class of one-frequency quasiperiodic Schrödinger operators with even potentials, thereby establishing optimality of prior phase-uniform dynamical bounds.

Conclusion: Dynamical localization fails to be uniform in phase for these operators, and the best possible phase-uniform control of position moments is indeed logarithmic—no faster decay or boundedness can be achieved uniformly.

Abstract: Dynamical localization requires all position moments of a quantum wavepacket to remain bounded in time, but for quasiperiodic Schrödinger operators such bounds are generally not uniform in phase. In the positive Lyapunov exponent regime, the best known phase-uniform estimates instead grow on a logarithmic scale. We prove that both the logarithmic scale and the dependence on the moment order are sharp for a class of one-frequency quasiperiodic Schrödinger operators with even potentials. Our main ingredient is a reflective version of semi-uniformly localized eigenfunctions, adapted to the two localization centers forced by a completely resonant phase, from which we obtain matching logarithmic lower bounds along sequences of times.

</details>


### [22] [Simultaneous Reconstructions of (x,t) -Dependent Coefficients and Initial Conditions of Parabolic Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29377)
*Michael V. Klibanov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper addresses the first inverse problem involving simultaneous reconstruction of an (x,t)-dependent coefficient and unknown initial condition in a general linear second-order parabolic PDE, with formally determined (n+1)-dimensional data; it establishes uniqueness and stability estimates—including novel logarithmic stability for time-reversed initial conditions—and identifies applications in Mean Field Games and epidemic tracking.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations of prior work—where unknown coefficients were assumed to depend only on space or only on time, and initial conditions were typically known—the authors tackle the more realistic and mathematically challenging case of jointly reconstructing both a spatiotemporal coefficient and an unknown initial condition from formally determined data.

Method: Analysis of a general linear second-order parabolic equation; derivation of Carleman-type estimates to prove uniqueness and conditional stability (specifically logarithmic stability) for the inverse problem; treatment of time-reversed data to obtain stability for initial conditions.

Result: Uniqueness theorem and conditional stability estimates (including new logarithmic stability estimates) for the simultaneous reconstruction problem; first such results for initial conditions of parabolic equations/inequalities with time-reversed data.

Conclusion: The proposed inverse problem is well-posed in the conditional stability sense; the theoretical framework extends existing methodologies and enables novel applications in opinion dynamics (Mean Field Games) and spatiotemporal epidemic modeling.

Abstract: This is the first publication, in which an inverse problem of the simultaneous reconstruction of an unknown (x,t) - dependent coefficient and an unknown initial condition in a general linear parabolic equation of the second order is considered. The input data depend on (n+1) variables and are, therefore, formally determined ones. Stability estimates and uniqueness theorem are obtained for this inverse problem. As a by-product, logarithmic stability estimates for initial conditions of parabolic equations and inequalities with time reversed data are obtained for the first time.
  All known publications about inverse problems for parabolic equations with formally determined input data and unknown coefficients assume that those coefficients depend either only on x or only on t. In addition, the initial condition is assumed to be known, except of two publications cited in the text.
  The inverse problem of this paper has two potential applications. The first one is in forecasting of public opinions in the framework of the Mean Field Games theory. The second one is in tracking spatiotemporal outbreaks of epidemics.

</details>


### [23] [Kubo Formulas and Bulk-Edge Correspondence for Curved Boundaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29524)
*Aren Martinian,Tom Stoiber*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a general bulk-edge correspondence for topological insulators using Roe algebras and a non-separable KK-theory framework, proving equivalence among real-space index pairings—including Fredholm and Kubo-type formulas—under arbitrary boundaries and relating them to the standard dual Dirac operator via topological degree.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the equivalence of various real-space expressions (e.g., Fredholm index pairings, Kubo formulas) for integer invariants classifying strong topological insulators, especially in settings with curved or arbitrary boundaries.

Method: Employs Roe algebras and a variant of KK-theory adapted to non-separable C*-algebras; constructs index pairings with general position-space Dirac operators; leverages coarse geometry (coarse equivalence to ℝ^d) and topological degree of symbol functions.

Result: Proves a general bulk-edge correspondence for index pairings under arbitrary boundaries; shows such pairings equal—up to explicit multiplicity given by topological degree—the pairings with the standard dual Dirac operator; derives a generalized Kubo formula expressing the index pairing via a real-space trace formula.

Conclusion: The integer topological invariants admit unified real-space interpretations across boundary geometries; their robustness stems from coarse geometric and K-theoretic structure, reconciling disparate formulations (Fredholm, Kubo, half-space projections) within a single analytic framework.

Abstract: Strong topological insulators are classified by integer invariants which admit different real-space expressions. Inspired by recent work studying these invariants for spaces with curved boundaries, we revisit the problem of equivalence of the various expressions, including Fredholm index pairings and Kubo formulas involving half-space projections. Using Roe algebras and a variant of KK-theory suitable for non-separable $C^*$-algebras, we prove a general form of bulk-edge correspondence for the index pairing with a general position-space Dirac operator in the presence of arbitrary boundaries. For spaces coarsely equivalent to $\mathbb{R}^d$, we show that up to multiplicity, these pairings are equal to those obtained with the standard dual Dirac operator, with the multiplicity explicitly given as the topological degree of the symbol function. In addition, we prove a generalized Kubo formula which computes the general index pairing by a real-space formula.

</details>


### [24] [A Morse-Family Integrator for Hamilton--Jacobi Dynamics Across Caustics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29645)
*F. Jiménez Alburquerque,M. Leok,C. Sardón,X. Zhao*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a geometric framework for implicit discrete Hamiltonian systems using discrete Morse families and Lagrangian relations, enabling consistent treatment of constrained, degenerate, and singular dynamics; it extends discrete Hamilton–Jacobi theory to Type-II generating families and applies it to optical wavefront propagation through fold caustics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unified geometric foundation for discrete Hamiltonian mechanics that naturally handles implicit, constrained, degenerate, and singular (e.g., caustic) systems—cases where standard symplectic maps or single-valued generating functions fail.

Method: Constructs a discrete analogue of Tulczyjew's triple using discrete Morse families and Lagrangian relations; defines discrete dynamics via Lagrangian submanifolds rather than explicit symplectomorphisms; develops Type-II discrete Hamilton–Jacobi theory via propagation of Lagrangian submanifolds; generalizes to singular projections using composition with Morse families as generating families.

Result: A coordinate-free, implicit geometric framework for discrete Hamiltonian systems; a Type-II discrete Hamilton–Jacobi equation linking consecutive generating functions; a generalized construction handling multivaluedness near singularities (e.g., fold caustics); a symplectic ray integrator coupled with a regular representation of multivalued wavefronts.

Conclusion: Lagrangian submanifolds—not symplectic maps—are the natural primitive objects for discrete Hamiltonian dynamics; Morse families provide the essential tool to extend discrete Hamilton–Jacobi theory to singular and multivalued settings, unifying numerical integration and geometric wave propagation.

Abstract: We develop a geometric framework for implicit discrete Hamiltonian systems based on discrete Morse families, Lagrangian relations, and discrete analogues of Tulczyjew's triple. The main idea is to regard the Lagrangian submanifold defining the discrete dynamics, rather than an explicit symplectic evolution map, as the fundamental geometric object. This viewpoint naturally accommodates implicit, constrained, and degenerate discrete systems.
  Within this framework, we formulate a Type--II discrete Hamilton--Jacobi theory in terms of the propagation of Lagrangian submanifolds between consecutive discrete steps. When these submanifolds are locally represented by exact one-forms $dW_k$ and $dW_{k+1}$, the resulting equations provide a discrete Hamilton--Jacobi relation between consecutive generating functions. More generally, when the projection onto configuration space becomes singular and a single-valued generating function ceases to exist, we show that the evolution can be described by the composition of Type--II discrete dynamics with Morse families. This yields a generating family for the propagated Lagrangian submanifold without requiring the dynamics to be represented as a graph.
  As an application, we consider the propagation of optical wavefronts through fold caustics. A Type--II discrete Hamiltonian yields a symplectic ray integrator, while a Morse family represents the multivalued wavefront near the caustic. Their composition provides a discrete propagation rule for the complete Lagrangian manifold across the singularity. In this way, the same geometric construction simultaneously provides a discrete Hamiltonian integrator and a regular representation of multivalued Hamilton--Jacobi solutions.

</details>


### [25] [Homogeneous attractive Bose-Einstein condensates with repulsive three-body interactions: the one-dimensional case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29874)
*Dinh-Thi Nguyen*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives the cubic-quintic nonlinear Schrödinger functional as the mean-field limit for a 1D attractive two-body + repulsive three-body Bose gas using intermediate Hartree theory, and analyzes how the three-body term affects ground-state energy and mass concentration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously understand how repulsive three-body interactions modify the mean-field description and ground-state properties of an otherwise attractive 1D Bose gas, where standard Gross–Pitaevskii theory fails due to collapse.

Method: Intermediate Hartree theory is employed to take the N → ∞ mean-field limit of the many-body Hamiltonian; variational analysis of the resulting cubic-quintic NLS functional is used to estimate ground-state energy and study mass concentration.

Result: The homogeneous cubic-quintic NLS functional is rigorously derived as the mean-field limit; the repulsive three-body term stabilizes the system, yielding finite ground-state energy and preventing complete mass collapse—leading to controlled mass concentration phenomena.

Conclusion: Repulsive three-body interactions provide essential stabilization in attractive 1D Bose gases, enabling a well-defined mean-field limit described by the cubic-quintic NLS functional, with quantifiable effects on energetics and spatial localization of the ground state.

Abstract: We investigate the behavior of a homogeneous one-dimensional Bose gas composed of $N$ identical bosons experiencing attractive two-body and repulsive three-body interactions. Utilizing intermediate Hartree theory, we rigorously derive the homogeneous cubic-quintic nonlinear Schrödinger functional as the mean-field limit of the many-body Hamiltonian. Particular attention is given to the impact of the repulsive three-body term on ground state energy estimates and mass concentration properties

</details>


### [26] [Homogeneous attractive Bose-Einstein condensates with repulsive three-body interactions: the two-dimensional case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29875)
*Dinh-Thi Nguyen*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper rigorously derives the cubic-quintic nonlinear Schrödinger functional as the mean-field limit for a 2D homogeneous Bose gas with competing two-body (attractive) and three-body (repulsive) interactions, using Hartree theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the rigorous mean-field limit of a 2D Bose gas with competing two- and three-body interactions, where standard Gross–Pitaevskii theory fails due to instability from pure attraction.

Method: Intermediate Hartree theory applied to a homogeneous 2D Bose gas; rigorous asymptotic analysis in the large-N limit to derive the effective cubic-quintic NLS energy functional.

Result: Derivation of the homogeneous cubic-quintic nonlinear Schrödinger functional as the leading-order effective description in the mean-field limit.

Conclusion: The interplay of attractive pair and repulsive three-body interactions stabilizes the system and yields a well-defined mean-field limit described by a cubic-quintic NLS functional—extending beyond standard cubic-only GP theory.

Abstract: We consider a homogeneous Bose gas composed of $N$ identical bosons occupying an infinite two-dimensional space. This gas exhibits both an attractive two-body interaction and a repulsive three-body interaction. Via the intermediate Hartree theory, we rigorously derive the homogeneous cubic-quintic nonlinear Schroedinger functional as the mean-field limit of the model. Our investigation focuses on the system's behavior in relation to the two-body interaction.

</details>


### [27] [Local Density Approximation and Other Limit Regimes for a Homogeneous Bose Gas with Repulsive Three-Body Interactions in Low-Dimensional Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29876)
*Thi Anh Thu Doan,Dinh-Thi Nguyen*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies minimizers of an energy functional modeling a quantum Bose gas with competing two-body attraction and three-body repulsion in low dimensions (d ≤ 2); it proves existence of minimizers at fixed effective statistics parameter and derives a Thomas-Fermi-type effective model in the large-statistics-parameter limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand ground-state behavior of a quantum Bose gas with competing attractive (cubic) and repulsive (quintic) nonlinearities—physically corresponding to two-body attraction and three-body repulsion—in low spatial dimensions where standard mean-field approximations break down.

Method: Variational analysis of a mass-subcritical (cubic) plus mass-supercritical (quintic) energy functional in ℝ^d, d ≤ 2; rigorous asymptotic analysis in the regime where the effective statistics parameter (scaling the quintic term relative to kinetic energy) becomes large; derivation of an effective Thomas-Fermi-type limit via Gamma-convergence or energy scaling arguments.

Result: Existence of energy minimizers for each fixed effective statistics parameter; emergence of a Thomas-Fermi-like density profile in the large-parameter limit; characterization of other asymptotic regimes governed by the interplay between cubic, quintic, and kinetic terms.

Conclusion: The competition between cubic attraction and quintic repulsion stabilizes the system in d ≤ 2, enabling well-defined ground states; the large-effective-statistics limit suppresses kinetic effects, yielding a local, density-only effective theory analogous to Thomas-Fermi approximation.

Abstract: In the whole space of low spatial dimensions, namely $d \leq 2$, we study the minimizers of an energy functional with an attractive cubic nonlinearity and a repulsive quintic nonlinearity, which describes a quantum Bose gas with a two-body attraction and a three-body repulsion. We prove the existence of minimizers at fixed effective statistics parameter. In the limit of a large effective statistics parameter of the mass-critical nonlinearity (with respect to the kinetic energy), we derive an effective Thomas-Fermi-like model for the homogeneous Bose gas. We also consider other limit regimes depending on the mass-critical nonlinearity.

</details>


### [28] [Multiple Nonlinear Waves by (Quantum) Neural Networks: Checking the AI supremacy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29894)
*Luigi Martina,Riccardo Caricato,Riccardo Della Torre*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper evaluates the capability of quantum physics-informed neural networks (QPINNs) to solve the Korteweg-de Vries (KdV) equation—modeling multiple wave propagation in a weakly nonlinear medium—with periodic boundary conditions, using analytical high-genus solutions as challenging benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether QPINNs are mature enough for solving evolutionary PDEs, particularly in challenging regimes where high-genus analytical solutions of the KdV equation serve as rigorous numerical benchmarks.

Method: Numerical experiments with QPINNs applied to the KdV equation under periodic boundary conditions; evaluation via defined indicators of merit.

Result: Quantitative performance metrics (indicators of merit) from numerical experiments assessing QPINN accuracy, stability, and convergence on multi-wave KdV solutions.

Conclusion: The study provides empirical evidence on the current readiness—and limitations—of QPINNs for solving complex, analytically tractable but numerically demanding evolutionary PDEs like the KdV equation.

Abstract: To test the potential of so-called quantum physics-informed neural network (QPINN) technology for solving evolutionary partial differential equations, we consider the problem of multiple wave propagation in a weakly nonlinear medium described by the Korteweg-de Vries equation with periodic boundary conditions. Although this problem is solvable analytically, high-genus solutions could represent a rather challenging testbed for numerical methods. Therefore, our idea is to test whether a QPINN is sufficiently mature for application in this context by providing a set of indicators of merit and performing several numerical experiments.

</details>


### [29] [Uniqueness of universal quantum dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30012)
*M. Y. Avetisyan,R. L. Mkrtchyan*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves the uniqueness of universal quantum dimension formulae under certain assumptions, showing that quantum dimensions of classical algebras admit a universal representation in Vogel's sense as products/ratios of q-dimension factors with linear arguments in Vogel's parameters α, β, γ, and deriving simple criteria ensuring uniqueness of all known such formulae.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish rigorous uniqueness conditions for universal quantum dimension formulae—central objects in quantum group theory and knot invariants—by leveraging Vogel's universal Lie algebra formalism and structural properties of q-dimensions.

Method: Analytical derivation using Vogel's parametrization of simple Lie algebras; decomposition of quantum dimensions into q-dimension factors of the form [aα + bβ + cγ]_q with c ∈ {0,1,2}; application of symmetry and functional constraints to deduce uniqueness criteria.

Result: Proof that, under mild assumptions, universal quantum dimension formulae are uniquely determined by their behavior on classical series (A, B, C, D) and the structure of their q-factor decomposition; all previously known universal quantum dimensions satisfy these uniqueness criteria.

Conclusion: The universality and uniqueness of quantum dimension formulae are tightly constrained by their analytic form in Vogel's plane, implying a deep rigidity in the structure of quantum invariants across Lie algebras.

Abstract: We prove, under some assumptions, the uniqueness of universal quantum dimension formulae. We first show that the quantum dimensions of classical algebras have a specific form that can be represented universally, in Vogel's sense, as the product/ratio of q-dimension factors with arguments $aα+bβ+cγ$ where $c=0, 1, 2$ and $a, b$ are integers. On this basis, we derive simple uniqueness criteria, according to which all known universal quantum dimensions are unique.

</details>


### [30] [On the equivalence between the Polchinski flow and the Connes-Kreimer approaches to perturbative renormalisation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31049)
*Yvain Bruned,Paul Laubie,Aurélien Minguella*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a rigorous correspondence between the Polchinski functional renormalisation group flow and the Connes-Kreimer Hopf-algebraic approach to perturbative renormalisation, showing they yield identical renormalised theories; it constructs a decorated-graph-based ansatz satisfying the Polchinski equation and derives the most general known form of the renormalised potential using forest duality and morphism properties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify two conceptually distinct frameworks for perturbative renormalisation—Polchinski's differential RG flow and Connes-Kreimer's algebraic (Hopf-algebraic) renormalisation—by proving their equivalence at the level of renormalised Green’s functions and effective potentials.

Method: Constructs a Feynman-diagram (decorated graph)-based ansatz for the effective action; employs combinatorial renormalisation via subgraph contraction; proves it satisfies the Polchinski flow equation by leveraging multiple renormalisation-compatible morphisms and a newly introduced duality formula for forests of decorated graphs.

Result: Demonstrates exact equivalence between Polchinski flow and Connes-Kreimer renormalisation for general Euclidean QFTs; obtains the most general explicit form of the renormalised potential in the literature; establishes forest duality as a key combinatorial tool for RG analysis.

Conclusion: The Polchinski equation admits a combinatorial solution grounded in the Connes-Kreimer Hopf algebra structure, confirming deep structural unity between functional RG methods and algebraic renormalisation; this bridges analytic and algebraic perspectives on UV completion and renormalisation group flow.

Abstract: We prove a correspondence between the Polchinski flow and the Connes-Kreimer approaches to perturbative renormalisation, in the sense that the first yields the same renormalisation as the latter. More precisely, we show that an ansatz based on decorated graphs (Feynman diagrams),with a combinatorial renormalisation procedure solves the Polchinski equation. This result holds for very general Euclidean quantum field theories. We are able to derive from this the form of the renormalised potential which is, to the best of our knowledge, the most general one in the literature. The main ingredients of the proof are multiple morphism properties with respect to the renormalisation, as well as a novel duality formula for forests of decorated graphs.

</details>


### [31] [Boundary Quantum Knizhnik-Zamolodchikov Equations and Integrability of Quantum Field Theories with Time-Dependent Bulk and Boundary Coupling Strengths](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31080)
*Parameshwar R. Pasnoori*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the generalized Bethe ansatz framework to quantum many-body systems with time-dependent bulk couplings *and* time-dependent open boundary conditions, deriving integrable boundary conditions and reducing the time-dependent Schrödinger equation to boundary quantum Knizhnik–Zamolodchikov (BqKZ) equations; the exact wavefunction follows from solving BqKZ, and static RG invariants map to dynamical invariants.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the recently developed time-dependent Bethe ansatz framework—previously restricted to periodic boundaries—to physically relevant open boundary conditions while preserving exact solvability and integrability.

Method: Extension of the generalized Bethe ansatz formalism to open boundaries with explicit time dependence; derivation of compatibility conditions for integrable time-dependent boundary fields; reduction of the time-dependent Schrödinger equation to matrix difference equations (BqKZ); identification of dynamical invariants via correspondence with static renormalization-group invariants.

Result: Exact integrable time-dependent boundary conditions are obtained; the time-dependent many-body problem is mapped onto the BqKZ equations; the exact wavefunction is constructively determined as their solution; a one-to-one correspondence is established between static RG invariants and dynamical invariants of the time-dependent model.

Conclusion: The generalized Bethe ansatz is robust under extension to open, time-dependent boundaries, yielding an exact, integrable framework where dynamics are encoded in solvable BqKZ equations and conserved quantities are inherited from the static theory’s RG structure.

Abstract: The generalized Bethe ansatz framework formulated in [P. R. Pasnoori, Phys. Rev. B 112, L060409 (2025)] provides a unified framework to find exact solutions to quantum many-body systems with time-dependent coupling strengths with periodic boundary conditions. In this work we extend this framework to the case of open boundary conditions where in addition to the time-dependent interactions in the bulk, the boundary conditions are explicitly time-dependent. We show that for integrable time-dependent bulk coupling strengths, the generalized Bethe ansatz framework provides the time-dependent boundary conditions compatible with integrability and reduces the time-dependent Schrodinger equation to a set of matrix difference equations called the boundary quantum Knizhnik-Zamolodchikov (BqKZ) equations. The solution to the BqKZ equations provides the explicit form of the exact wavefunction. We further show that the RG invariants of the corresponding static model identify with the dynamical invariants in the time-dependent model.

</details>


### [32] [Rotating-wave approximation for spin-boson models with structured fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31090)
*Aitor Balmaseda,Davide Lonigro,Juan Manuel Pérez-Pardo*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives state-dependent bounds on the difference between two quantum evolutions governed by unbounded Hamiltonians with a shared form domain, using a novel second integration-by-parts technique at the sesquilinear form level—bypassing restrictive invariant domain assumptions. The bound depends on the norm of the time-integrated generator difference (not the integral of the norm), capturing cancellation from fast oscillations. As an application, it yields a fully explicit, parameter-dependent quantitative error bound for the rotating-wave approximation in structured spin-boson models—valid on a dense subspace, uniform in frequency, and exact in the high-frequency limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish rigorous, state-dependent error bounds for quantum dynamics generated by unbounded Hamiltonians without requiring common invariant operator domains—a limitation of standard approaches—and to quantify the rotating-wave approximation (RWA) in physically realistic, structured spin-boson models where bosonic dispersion and form factors are general.

Method: A second integration by parts performed on sesquilinear forms (not operators), leveraging the common form domain; careful high-frequency scaling analysis; estimation via norms of time-integrated generator differences rather than pointwise norms; application of functional analytic tools for self-adjoint extensions and form perturbations.

Result: A new state-dependent bound on the unitary evolution difference that is sensitive to oscillatory cancellations; a fully explicit, frequency-uniform quantitative error bound for the RWA in spin-boson models with arbitrary massive dispersion relations on general measure spaces and admissible form factors; validity on a dense subspace; convergence to exactness as frequency → ∞.

Conclusion: The form-level integration-by-parts technique significantly weakens domain requirements for quantum evolution comparison, enabling robust, physically meaningful error estimates; the RWA is rigorously justified quantitatively in broad spin-boson settings, with explicit, model-parameter-dependent constants independent of frequency scale.

Abstract: We derive state-dependent bounds on the difference between two quantum evolutions generated by unbounded Hamiltonians sharing a common form domain. The main technical tool is a second integration by parts, performed at the level of sesquilinear forms rather than at the operator level, which removes the need for a common invariant operator domain. The resulting estimate involves the norm of the time-integrated difference of the two generators, rather than the integral of its norm, and is therefore sensitive to the averaging effects produced by fast-oscillating terms. As an application we prove a quantitative bound on the rotating-wave approximation for spin-boson models with a structured boson field, described by an arbitrary massive dispersion relation on a general measure space and by a suitable class of form factors. The proof involves a careful analysis of the high-frequency scaling. The bound holds on a dense subspace of states, is fully explicit, and all the constants entering it depend only on the parameters of the model and not on the frequency scale, so that the approximation becomes exact in the limit of large frequency.

</details>


### [33] [The Dunkl two-photon/Schrödinger algebras and their applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31104)
*Francisco J. Herranz,Danilo Latini*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces Dunkl-extended versions of the two-photon and Schrödinger algebras, constructs their Casimir invariants (cubic for the full algebras, quadratic for subalgebras), builds Fock-space and holomorphic representations for the Dunkl two-photon algebra, and provides a vector field realization of the Dunkl-Schrödinger algebra leading to six Dunkl differential-difference equations—including a Dunkl heat-Schrödinger equation—in 1+1 dimensions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize symmetry algebras of quantum optical and nonrelativistic systems by incorporating reflection symmetries via Dunkl operators, thereby enriching algebraic structure and enabling new differential-difference equations with reflection covariance.

Method: Algebraic construction of reflection-extended Lie algebras using Dunkl operators; derivation of polynomial Casimir invariants via commutator analysis; representation theory on Dunkl harmonic oscillator Fock space and Dunkl-Fock-Bargmann space; explicit vector field realization using Dunkl derivatives; systematic identification and Casimir computation for subalgebras.

Result: Dunkl two-photon and Dunkl-Schrödinger algebras are defined and shown isomorphic; cubic Casimir invariant derived for both; multiple quadratic Casimirs computed for key subalgebras; Fock and holomorphic representations constructed; vector field realization yields six nontrivial Dunkl differential-difference equations in 1+1D, including a Dunkl heat-Schrödinger equation.

Conclusion: The Dunkl extensions provide a consistent, reflection-covariant generalization of fundamental symmetry algebras, enabling novel operator realizations and a family of physically relevant Dunkl-type evolution equations—demonstrating the utility of Dunkl calculus in extending classical algebraic structures and their applications in mathematical physics.

Abstract: The work introduces the novel Dunkl two-photon and Dunkl-Schrödinger algebras as reflection-extended counterparts of the standard six-dimensional two-photon and Schrödinger algebras. These extensions are realized through the presence of an involutive generator representing a reflection, and they reduce to the undeformed two-photon and Schrödinger algebras in the absence of this additional generator in an appropriate limit. For both algebraic structures, which are shown to be isomorphic, we derive the associated cubic polynomial Casimir invariant. Furthermore, we identify several structurally significant subalgebras and compute their corresponding quadratic Casimir invariants. Representations of the Dunkl two-photon algebra are constructed on the Fock space of the one-dimensional Dunkl harmonic oscillator and subsequently translated into holomorphic realizations within the associated Dunkl-Fock-Bargmann representation. Finally, for the Dunkl-Schrödinger algebra, we present an explicit vector field realization in terms of the Dunkl spatial derivative, while keeping time continuous. When applied to the Casimir invariants of the aforementioned subalgebras, this realization yields a family of six nontrivial Dunkl differential-difference equations in $1+1$ dimensions, including, in particular, a Dunkl version of the well-known heat-Schrödinger equation.

</details>


### [34] [Exactly flat bands beyond the chiral limit in Bistritzer--MacDonald Hamiltonians](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31147)
*Zhen Huang,Kevin D. Stubbs*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs a family of nonchiral tunneling potentials for which the twisted bilayer graphene Hamiltonian retains exactly flat zero-energy bands—contradicting a conjecture (Open Problem 3 in Zworski’s survey)—and proves completeness of this family; in contrast, the standard Bistritzer–MacDonald model with first-harmonic potentials generically lacks exact flatness away from isolated nonchiral couplings, shown via a novel invertibility criterion based on projected perturbation analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve Open Problem 3 from Zworski's survey—which asks whether exact flat bands persist beyond the chiral limit in twisted bilayer graphene—and to characterize all admissible nonchiral tunneling potentials that preserve exact flatness at zero energy.

Method: Constructs an explicit family of nonchiral tunneling potentials ensuring two zero-energy Bloch eigenstates for all momenta and all real nonchiral couplings; proves completeness via symmetry and spectral analysis; introduces and applies an invertibility criterion for projected perturbations to rule out exact flatness in the standard BM model; uses local Taylor expansion to verify the criterion for first-harmonic potentials.

Result: A complete characterization of nonchiral potentials admitting exactly flat zero-energy bands; existence of infinitely many counterexamples to Open Problem 3; proof that standard BM Hamiltonians with first-harmonic tunneling lack exact flatness except possibly at isolated, non-accumulating nonchiral coupling values.

Conclusion: Exact flatness is not exclusive to the chiral limit: it persists robustly for a precisely characterized class of nonchiral potentials, while being highly fragile—generic and non-isolated—in the physically common first-harmonic BM model.

Abstract: The chiral limit of the Bistritzer--MacDonald model for twisted bilayer graphene has exactly flat bands at magic angles. We show that exact flatness can persist beyond the chiral limit with nonchiral tunnelling potentials in the usual symmetry class. We construct a family of nonchiral tunnelling potentials for which the Hamiltonian has at least two zero-energy states at every Bloch momentum and for every real value of the nonchiral coupling. This gives a family of counterexamples to Open Problem~3 of Zworski's survey \cite{ZworskiSurvey}. Conversely, we prove that every admissible potential with this property belongs to this family, thereby obtaining a complete characterization. By contrast, we show that the standard BM model, namely, BM Hamiltonians with first harmonic tunnelling potentials, does not admit exactly flat bands at the magic angle except possibly at a discrete set of nonchiral coupling strengths, with no finite accumulation point. To prove this, we introduce an explicit invertibility criterion. The criterion requires the projected perturbation to have a nonzero scalar coefficient at some Bloch momentum, obstructing exact flatness for general nonchiral tunnelling. A local Taylor expansion argument verifies the criterion for the standard BM potentials. For the family constructed above, however, the projected perturbation vanishes at every Bloch momentum, so the invertibility criterion fails.

</details>

### [35] [Reimagining Gravity: Generalized Symmetries, Double Copy, and Spinning Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28736)
*Joon-Hwi Kim*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This dissertation addresses three open problems in gravity—generalized symmetries in dynamical GR as an EFT, field-theoretic origin of the double copy, and exact EOM/Lagrangians for spinning black holes in external fields—using three complementary perspectives: Lorentz one-form symmetry (Part I), diffeomorphism gauge structure linked to color-kinematics duality (Part II), and chiral (self-dual/anti-self-dual) decomposition (Part III), yielding a rigorous derivation of Kerr from Taub-NUT constituents and a pole-free chiral Lagrangian for spinning black hole Compton amplitudes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve three foundational open questions: (1) existence of generalized symmetries in dynamical gravity as a low-energy effective field theory; (2) field-theoretic explanation of the tree-level double copy relating GR and Yang-Mills; (3) exact equations of motion and Lagrangians for 4D spinning black holes in external fields within point-particle effective theory.

Method: Three distinct theoretical frameworks are employed: (I) treating gravity as a Lorentz gauge theory to construct a one-form global symmetry; (II) analyzing gravity as a diffeomorphism gauge theory in relation to color-kinematics duality and double copy; (III) decomposing 4D gravity into self-dual and anti-self-dual sectors, applying chiral formalism to derive black hole metrics (e.g., Kerr from Taub-NUT pair) and Compton amplitudes via Lagrangian methods.

Result: A Lorentz one-form symmetry is established in dynamical gravity; the Kerr metric is rigorously derived as a composition of self-dual and anti-self-dual Taub-NUT solutions, elevating the Newman-Janis algorithm; a dynamical probe analogue of that algorithm is proposed; and a chiral Lagrangian is constructed yielding pole-free, factorization-consistent Compton amplitudes for spinning black holes across all helicities.

Conclusion: The three perspectives collectively demonstrate that structural insights—from generalized symmetries and double copy to chiral decomposition—provide unified, rigorous foundations for classical and effective descriptions of spinning black holes and deepen the quantum-field-theoretic understanding of gravity beyond standard geometric paradigms.

Abstract: General relativity is a century-old subject. Yet modern explorations through generalized symmetries, scattering amplitudes, and effective field theory have raised open problems, motivating a reassessment of conventional views on gravitation, spacetime, and spin. First, do generalized symmetries exist in dynamical gravity as a low-energy EFT? Second, is there a field-theoretic explanation for the tree-level double copy relationship between general relativity and Yang-Mills theory? Third, what are the exact equations of motion or Lagrangians of four-dimensional spinning black holes in external fields, in point-particle effective theory? In this dissertation, three different perspectives on gravity are developed to shed light on each of these puzzles.
  In Part I, we view gravity as a gauge theory of Lorentz group to establish a one-form symmetry of dynamical gravity. In Part II, we investigate how far gravity can be viewed as a gauge theory of diffeomorphisms, in connection with color-kinematics duality and double copy. In Part III, we view four-dimensional gravity as a nonlinear interaction between self-dual and anti-self-dual parts. We apply this view to the derivation of four-dimensional spinning black hole solutions as well as their dynamics in point-particle effective theory. First, we derive a new self-dual black hole metric from classical double copy and show that the Kerr metric represents a pair of self-dual and anti-self-dual Taub-NUT solutions, elevating the Newman-Janis algorithm to a rigorous derivation. Second, we propose a dynamical probe counterpart of Newman-Janis algorithm. We give a Lagrangian derivation of spinning black hole Compton amplitudes that exhibit correct factorizations and are free of spurious poles for all helicity configurations while developing a chiral formalism that manifests and maximally utilizes the simplicity of the self-dual sector.

</details>


### [36] [Carroll-Cotton Tensors and Gravitational Radiation at Null Infinity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28741)
*Adrien Fiorucci,Simon Pekar,P. Marios Petropoulos,Matthieu Vilatte*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces Cotton tensors on conformal Carroll geometries via gauging the conformal Carroll algebra, yielding a conformally covariant measure of non-flatness and a geometric characterization of gravitational radiation at null infinity.

  <summary>Details</summary>

Method: Gauging the conformal Carroll algebra to construct first-principles Cotton tensors on conformal Carroll geometries.

Result: A conformally covariant Cotton tensor defined on conformal Carroll manifolds; it serves as a natural geometric proxy for gravitational wave emission and deviations from stationarity, distinct from and more covariant than the Bondi news.

Conclusion: Carroll-Cotton tensors are the appropriate geometric objects to encode gravitational radiation at null infinity in a manifestly conformally covariant and intrinsic Carrollian framework.

Abstract: We provide a first-principles definition of Cotton tensors on conformal Carroll geometries by gauging the conformal Carroll algebra. On the mathematical side, this analysis yields a conformally covariant object designed to quantify deviations from conformal flatness in Carrollian geometries. On the physical side, it offers a powerful geometric tool to describe gravitational radiative degrees of freedom at the conformal boundary of four-dimensional asymptotically flat spacetimes. Unlike the Bondi news, whose fully-covariant definition requires amendment by supplementary data that we identify with the Geroch tensor, the Carroll-Cotton tensors offer the natural geometric objects to quantify deviations from stationarity due to the emission of gravitational waves.

</details>


### [37] [OPE and correlation functions in a generally covariant form](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28743)
*Arpit Das,Anatoly Konechny,Naveen Balaji Umasankar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies Euclidean conformal field theories (CFTs) on curved D ≥ 3 manifolds, extending scalar OPE constructions to conformally flat spaces and refining the short-distance ansatz for three-point functions on non-conformally flat spaces to ensure local covariant OPE.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize CFT structures—particularly the operator product expansion (OPE) and short-distance behavior of correlation functions—to curved backgrounds beyond conformally flat ones, ensuring consistency with locality and covariance.

Method: Uses conformal symmetry and the ambient space formalism; extends prior OPE descendant constructions for scalars on conformally flat spaces; analyzes three-point function asymptotics on generic curved spaces using a generalized Parisini–Skenderis–Withers ansatz and imposes local covariance constraints.

Result: Derives a general expression for descendant contributions to the scalar-channel OPE on conformally flat spaces; identifies necessary corrections to the Parisini–Skenderis–Withers ansatz for three-point functions on non-conformally flat spaces to guarantee a well-defined local covariant OPE.

Conclusion: A local covariant OPE in curved-space CFT requires modifications to existing short-distance ansätze, revealing new geometric dependence beyond Weyl invariants and highlighting subtleties in extending flat-space CFT axioms to generic Riemannian geometries.

Abstract: In this paper we consider some general aspects of Euclidean conformal field theories on curved spaces of dimension $D\ge 3$. We first look at the OPE of scalar primary fields on conformally flat spaces. Extending the results of \cite{Konechny:2026bqg}, we give a general construction of the descendants' contributions to the OPE in the scalar channel. We then discuss CFTs on non-conformally flat spaces in the ambient space formalism. We investigate the short-distance behaviour of three-point functions on such spaces using the general ansatz proposed by Parisini, Skenderis, and Withers \cite{Parisini:2022wkb, Parisini:2023nbd}. We find that some additional corrections need to be added to the ansatz to ensure the existence of a local covariant OPE.

</details>


### [38] [Holographic warped CFTs from General Relativity on AdS$_3$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28751)
*Kristiansen Lara,Ricardo Troncoso*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces new boundary conditions for 3D Einstein gravity with negative cosmological constant, yielding a canonical algebra of Virasoro ⊕ u(1) Kac-Moody without redefinitions; it identifies Euclidean AdS₃ as the ground state, derives a warped modular S-transformation linking Euclidean BTZ and thermal AdS₃, and obtains the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy via a warped Cardy formula.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct physically meaningful, non-redefined boundary conditions for 3D AdS gravity that naturally yield a standard Virasoro ⊕ u(1) asymptotic symmetry algebra and support a precise microscopic entropy counting matching the Bekenstein-Hawking result.

Method: Hamiltonian analysis of asymptotic symmetries; derivation of canonical generators and their Poisson algebra; imposition of diffeomorphism equivalence between Euclidean BTZ and thermal AdS₃ to fix modular transformations of chemical potentials; application of a warped generalization of the Cardy formula.

Result: A new set of boundary conditions yielding the semidirect sum Virasoro ⋉ u(1) algebra in standard form; warped modular S-transformation emerges from Euclidean BTZ–thermal AdS₃ diffeomorphism; warped Cardy formula exactly reproduces Bekenstein-Hawking entropy with Euclidean AdS₃ as ground state.

Conclusion: The proposed boundary conditions provide a consistent, redefinition-free framework for warped CFT duals in AdS₃ gravity, unifying geometric identification of states (BTZ/AdS) with modular covariance and exact entropy matching—offering a robust alternative to Brown-Henneaux and Compere-Song-Strominger prescriptions.

Abstract: We introduce a new set of boundary conditions for three-dimensional Einstein gravity with negative cosmological constant. The canonical generators plainly realize the semidirect sum of Virasoro and $u(1)$ Kac-Moody algebras in its standard form, without the need of additional redefinitions of the global charges. We show that requiring Euclidean BTZ and thermal AdS$_3$ to be diffeomorphic precisely induces the warped modular $S$-transformation of the chemical potentials. The corresponding warped Cardy formula then exactly reproduces the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy, where the ground state is identified as Euclidean AdS$_3$. An explicit comparison with the boundary conditions of Brown-Henneaux and Compere-Song-Strominger is also addressed.

</details>


### [39] [Action-angle variables and phase space formulation of Hermitian matrix models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28942)
*Arghya Chattopadhyay*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a phase space description for large-N Hermitian matrix models using orthogonal polynomial recursions, linking semiclassical action-angle variables to eigenvalue-momentum coordinates; it validates the framework in one-cut (including Gaussian) and symmetric quartic two-cut phases, and outlines its extension to multicut (finite-gap) cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a direct, recursion-based phase space formulation for large-N matrix models—bypassing traditional saddle-point or loop-equation methods—and unify perspectives from orthogonal polynomials, spectral curves, and Wigner transforms.

Method: Derives semiclassical action-angle variables from the Jacobi (three-term) recursion lattice of orthogonal polynomials; maps them canonically to eigenvalue-momentum phase space; computes momentum profile via zero-density of polynomials; cross-validates using Wigner transform of Christoffel-Darboux kernel and planar spectral curve; extends to two-cut case using period-two recursion and Bloch band analysis.

Result: A consistent phase space structure emerges: in the one-cut phase, momentum is fully determined by zero density; the Wigner transform and spectral curve reproduce identical geometry; in the symmetric quartic two-cut phase, two disjoint phase space components arise, each with action equal to a partial 't Hooft coupling; finite-gap generalization is formulated for arbitrary multicut phases.

Conclusion: Orthogonal polynomial recursions provide a fundamental, geometrically transparent route to phase space in matrix models; the resulting semiclassical structure unifies zero distribution, spectral geometry, and topological expansion, and naturally accommodates multi-band (multicut) regimes.

Abstract: We develop a phase space description of large $N$ Hermitian one matrix models directly from the recursions satisfied by the orthogonal polynomials. In the one-cut phase, the recursion lattice naturally gives rise to a semiclassical action-angle pair, which can be mapped canonically to eigenvalue and momentum variables. We show that the resulting momentum profile is determined by the density of zeros of the orthogonal polynomials, and that the same phase space structure is reproduced from the Wigner transform of the Christoffel-Darboux projector and from the planar spectral curve. The Gaussian model provides a simple consistency check of the proposal. We then extend the construction to the symmetric quartic two-cut phase, where a period-two Jacobi recursion produces two Bloch bands and two disconnected phase space components with actions given by the partial 't Hooft couplings. Finally, we outline the finite-gap generalization appropriate to multicut phases.

</details>


### [40] [Flat connections on moduli spaces I: Local (1,0)-extension of the DHS connection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29169)
*Eric D'Hoker,Benjamin Enriquez,Oliver Schlotterer,Federico Zerbini*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper initiates a program to extend the smooth, modular-invariant flat DHS connection on the configuration space of n points on a genus-h Riemann surface to a global flat connection on the full Teichmüller space T_{h,n}, valued in derivations of the infinite-dimensional Lie algebra ĥt_{h,n}; it isolates and solves the local equations governing the holomorphic Teichmüller component L.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a global flat connection on the Teichmüller space T_{h,n} that extends the known flat DHS connection J_DHS — which is only defined on the configuration space and invariant under Sp(2h,Z) — while preserving modularity and compatibility with the fibration T_{h,n} → T_h.

Method: Uses the fibration structure of Teichmüller space T_{h,n} over T_h to decompose a candidate extended connection into three components; isolates the differential-geometric and Lie-algebraic constraints on the holomorphic Teichmüller piece L, then solves them locally via explicit coordinate-adapted construction.

Abstract: The flat DHS connection $\mathcal J_{\mathrm{DHS}}$ constructed in arXiv:2602.01461 is smooth on the configuration space of $n$ points on a fixed compact Riemann surface $Σ$ of arbitrary genus $h$, takes values in an infinite-dimensional Lie algebra $\hat{\mathfrak t}_{h,n}$ and is invariant under the modular group $\mathrm{Sp}(2h,\mathbb Z)$. This paper is the first in a series for a program whose goal is to extend the connection $\mathcal J_{\mathrm{DHS}}$ to a global flat connection on the Teichmüller space $\mathcal T_{h,n}$ valued in the Lie algebra of derivations of $\hat{\mathfrak t}_{h,n}$. Upon the choice of local coordinates adapted to the map $\mathcal T_{h,n}\to \mathcal T_h$, such a connection splits into three pieces: $\mathcal J_{\mathrm{DHS}}$, a piece $\mathcal L$ corresponding to holomorphic directions of $\mathcal T_h$ and a third piece corresponding to anti-holomorphic directions in $\mathcal T_h$. In this paper, we isolate the system of equations satisfied by $\mathcal L$ and obtain its solution locally and explicitly. The construction of a global extension of $\mathcal J_{\mathrm{DHS}}$ to $\mathcal T_{h,n}$ and the extension of the meromorphic connection of arXiv:1112.0864 to $\mathcal T_{h,n}$, are relegated to future publications in this series.

</details>


### [41] [One-loop renormalization group flow of the translation-invariant noncommutative Yukawa theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29213)
*Karim Bouchachia,Smain Kouadik*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the one-loop renormalization group flow of a noncommutative pseudoscalar Yukawa theory on 4D Euclidean Moyal space, showing its one-loop renormalizability and solving the coupled beta-function system analytically via symmetry-adapted variables and invariants; it reveals UV restoration and IR amplification of Moyal ordering symmetry, with dynamical suppression (but not removal) of the non-planar infrared singularity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish renormalizability and understand the RG dynamics of noncommutative Yukawa interactions—specifically how Moyal-star vertex ordering ambiguity affects coupling evolution and IR behavior—in contrast to commutative counterparts.

Method: One-loop perturbative RG analysis on Moyal space; derivation and analytic solution of coupled beta functions for two independent Yukawa couplings $g_1$, $g_2$; variable decoupling via $u = g_1^2 + g_2^2$, $v = g_1^2 - g_2^2$; Riccati reduction on symmetric surface; identification of exact invariant $\mathcal{I} = (g_1 g_2)^3 / (g_1^2 - g_2^2)^4$; closed-form integration of dimensionful parameters along symmetric critical trajectory.

Result: Proof of one-loop renormalizability; analytic solutions for all beta functions; discovery of UV-attractive symmetric surface ($r=0$) and IR-attractive asymmetric fixed directions ($r=\pm1$); dynamical logarithmic suppression of the non-planar $1/(\theta^2 p^2)$ coefficient as $p \to 0$; closed-form solution for mass and field-strength parameters on the symmetric critical trajectory.

Conclusion: The theory is one-loop renormalizable; RG flow intrinsically distinguishes between planar and non-planar sectors, dynamically restoring Moyal ordering symmetry in the UV while enhancing asymmetry in the IR; this suppresses—but does not eliminate—the characteristic non-planar IR singularity, indicating need for additional IR-improving mechanisms.

Abstract: We study the one-loop renormalization group flow of the translation-invariant noncommutative pseudoscalar Yukawa theory on four-dimensional Euclidean Moyal space. The two inequivalent orderings of the Moyal-star Yukawa vertex give rise to two independent couplings $g_{1}$ and $g_{2}$, whose beta functions form a coupled nonlinear system; every one-loop divergence is absorbed by a counterterm already present in the action, establishing one-loop renormalizability of the theory. We solve the one-loop system analytically: it decouples in the variables $u=g_{1}^{2}+g_{2}^{2}$ and $v=g_{1}^{2}-g_{2}^{2}$, the quartic coupling is obtained by a Riccati reduction on the symmetric surface $g_{1}=g_{2}$, and the generic asymmetric flow is reduced to a single quadrature by the exact invariant $\mathcal{I}=(g_{1}g_{2})^{3}/(g_{1}^{2}-g_{2}^{2})^{4}$. Two dynamical consequences follow. The ratio $r=v/u$ has a UV-attractive symmetric surface $r=0$ and IR-attractive asymmetric directions $r=\pm1$, so that the flow restores the symmetry between the two Moyal orderings towards the ultraviolet and amplifies any initial ordering asymmetry towards the infrared, as a fractional power of the logarithm. In consequence the Yukawa-induced coefficient of the non-planar $1/(θ^{2}p^{2})$ structure is dynamically suppressed towards $p\rightarrow 0$; the singularity itself is not removed, and remains the task of the IR-improving term. We also solve the dimensionful sector ($M^{2}$, $m$, $a^{2}$) in closed form on the symmetric critical trajectory, and compare throughout with the commutative theory.

</details>


### [42] [Inequalities and Positivity in Modular Flowed Entanglement Entropy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29329)
*Liangyu Chen*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces the modular entanglement shift (MES)—the change in entanglement entropy under modular flow—and proves an exact entropy balance: MES_A + MES_B equals the mutual information generated. In 2D CFT vacuum with two disjoint intervals, symmetry enforces MES_A = MES_B ≥ 0, shown via conformal transport and Araki relative entropy; the orbit is identified as a Connes cocycle orbit, with implications for AdS/CFT.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how entanglement entropy evolves under modular flow—a physically natural but non-standard dynamical evolution—beyond fixed quantum channels, and to uncover structural constraints (e.g., sign, balance) imposed by symmetry and quantum information principles in QFT and holography.

Method: Analytical derivation of entropy balance using modular Hamiltonian dynamics; application of global conformal involution symmetry in 2D CFT; regularization via conformal transport; reformulation using Araki relative entropy; identification of modular-flow orbits with Connes cocycles.

Result: 1) Exact entropy balance: MES_A + MES_B = I(A:B) along the orbit, hence nonnegative sum; 2) For two disjoint intervals in 2D CFT vacuum, MES_A = MES_B ≥ 0, proven both with conformally transported cutoff and intrinsically via Araki relative entropy; 3) Modular-flow orbit equals a Connes cocycle orbit.

Conclusion: Modular evolution induces signed but balanced entanglement shifts; symmetry can enforce nonnegativity even without generic monotonicity; the MES framework bridges modular theory, entanglement geometry, and algebraic QFT—with potential relevance to bulk reconstruction and dynamics in AdS/CFT.

Abstract: The modular Hamiltonian plays an important role in quantum information theory, quantum field theory, and the AdS/CFT correspondence. In this paper, we study a specific dynamical setup: starting from the tensor product of the reduced density matrices of a reference state, we evolve this product state under the modular flow generated by the reference state itself. We then investigate the resulting change in the entanglement entropy of a given region, which we call the modular entanglement shift (MES). Unlike evolution under a fixed quantum channel, this modular evolution obeys no general principle requiring the MES to have a definite sign. For a bipartition into subsystems A and B, we establish an exact entropy balance: the sum of the MESs for A and B equals the mutual information generated along the orbit. Consequently, their sum is nonnegative, even though either individual shift may be negative. For two disjoint intervals in the vacuum of a two-dimensional conformal field theory, however, we prove that a global conformal involution exchanging the intervals forces the two MESs to be equal. It follows that each MES is nonnegative along the modular flow. We establish this result both using a conformally transported regulator and intrinsically in terms of Araki relative entropy. Finally, we identify the modular-flow orbit with a Connes cocycle orbit and discuss possible applications to the AdS/CFT correspondence.

Abstract: We study the holographic duality between free vector models and Higher-Spin Gravity in 4-dimensional de Sitter space.   <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the structure of genuine multipartite entanglement in topological quantum field theories, specifically whether Rényi multi-entropies collapse onto lower-order information measures (e.g., bipartite/tripartite) beyond special symmetric cases like key-ring links.

Conclusion: The failure of multi-entropy collapse is not governed solely by zero-divisors in ℤ_k but reflects a subtle interplay between Rényi index n and the full arithmetic structure (e.g., p-adic valuations) of the Chern–Simons level k.

Abstract: We study genuine multi-entropy in Abelian $U(1)_k$ Chern-Simons theory. For key-ring link states, where only the linking numbers between one distinguished component $K$ and the remaining $\mathtt{q}-1$ components are nonzero, we derive an exact closed-form expression for the $\mathtt{q}$-partite Rényi multi-entropy for general $\mathtt{q}$, level $k$, and Rényi index $n$. For $\mathtt{q}=4$, this shows that the genuine multi-entropy $\mathrm{GM}^{(4)}_n$ collapses exactly onto the tripartite information $I_{3,n}$ for all $n$, while for $\mathtt{q}=5$ it is likewise completely determined, for all $n$, by a linear combination of tripartite and bipartite Rényi multi-entropies. We then go beyond the key-ring class and study general four-component link states with arbitrary pairwise linking numbers. A numerical scan over Chern--Simons levels $2\leq k\leq24$ shows that the all-$n$ collapse found analytically for key-ring states does not survive for generic link states. Remarkably, the collapse remains exact at $n=2$ for every level examined. At $n=3$, violations occur, within the scanned range, only when $3\mid k$, with a further dependence on the $3$-adic valuation of $k$. At $n=4$ and $n=5$, violations occur for every level examined, with rates that vary strongly with $k$. These results show that the breakdown is not controlled simply by the zero-divisor structure of composite $\mathbb Z_k$, but instead exhibits a nontrivial joint dependence on the Rényi index and the arithmetic structure of the Chern--Simons level.

</details>

Second, we discuss the phenomenon of an emergent boundary gauge invariance for PEC and PMC plates in Yang-Mills theory. Abstract: We study a local constrained problem at matrix size $N=5$ in the Lorentzian IIB (IKKT) matrix model and numerically continue a one-dimensional physical modulus. For an exact fixed-$Q_ρ$ constrained solution family, the stationary identity implies constant classical action; the 23 accepted backgrounds realize this relation to the quoted precision. Across the sampled branch, the spectrum of the Lorentzian extent operator remains common to numerical precision while microscopic noncommutative invariants vary.
  We evaluate finite-dimensional one-loop fluctuations along this branch. At fixed bosonic background, the fermionic Grassmann integral is exact and gives $\operatorname{Pf}\mathcal M_F$; its magnitude is invariant under color-gauge and connected proper-Lorentz transformations and varies along the modulus. The complete boson--ghost--fermion quotient measure defines a Lorentz-representative-independent density one-form rather than a scalar potential. Thus quantum fluctuation data distinguish backgrounds unresolved by the coarse quadratic spectrum.
  We also apply a framed higher-$L$ SO(5) structural probe. At the reference background, the ordered $3+6$ spectral separation is larger at $L=2,3$ than at $L=1$, and larger still in the canonical large-$L$ limit. This is a frame-defined diagnostic, not an intrinsic observable or a higher-$N$ solution. The Pfaffian and higher-$L$ responses are closely correlated along the sampled branch, which we treat only as descriptive. These results suggest that microscopic noncommutative structure and quantum fluctuations may contain information relevant to dimensional dynamics, without establishing dimensional selection.

### [51] [BMN Spread Complexity Across Phase Transitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29816)

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>

Result: Identifies three distinct dynamical regimes: (i) low-T gapped phase with oscillatory, non-chaotic complexity growth; (ii) high-T continuum phase with maximal scrambling (λ_K = π/β, saturating the bound); (iii) intermediate regime with sub-leading √n correction to Lanczos coefficients, yielding sub-exponential crossover.

Conclusion: The interplay between mass gap, temperature, and chemical potential qualitatively reshapes quantum complexity evolution—transitioning from integrable-like dynamics to universal chaos—and reveals how emergent symmetries and spectral structure constrain scrambling in gauge/gravity duals.

Abstract: We investigate the dynamical behavior and spread complexity of quantum states within the mass-deformed BMN matrix model equipped with an emergent $U(1)$ global charge at finite temperature $β^{-1}$ and chemical potential $ν$. Focusing on the strong-coupling regime ($μ\gg 1$), we model the dynamics via a charged Thermofield Double (cTFD) state and trace the evolution of spread complexity across three distinct thermodynamic regimes. In the low-temperature gapped phase ($βμ\gg 1$), discrete mass-gap bound states dominate, trapping wavepacket dispersion and producing non-chaotic, oscillatory early-time growth. Conversely, in the high-temperature continuum phase ($βμ\ll 1$), thermal excitations overwhelm the mass gap, driving a transition to a continuous advection field that exhibits maximal chaotic scrambling with a Krylov Lyapunov exponent $λ_K = π/ β$ that saturates the universal bound. In the intermediate temperature regime ($βμ\sim 1, βν\sim 1$), the interplay between mass-gap bound states and the continuous thermal background induces a sub-leading correction to the Lanczos coefficients $b_n \sim \fracπβ n + γ\sqrt{n}$, governing a continuous sub-exponential crossover before full chaotic thermalization. </details>
