<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover structural constraints on supersymmetric horizons without assuming Killing spinor norm constancy, by leveraging intrinsic properties of Killing-spinor bilinears on a compact horizon section.

Method: Analysis of Killing-spinor bilinear identities on a compact connected section of a supersymmetric M-horizon; application of Spin(7) representation theory to decompose flux; derivation of pointwise and integral constraints; examination of rigidity, algebraic flux relations, and symmetry obstructions.

Result: A fundamental pointwise identity linking warp factor Δ, rotation one-form h, and spinor norms; three consequences: (i) single-point rigidity implying global geometric/flux constraints; (ii) Δ expressed solely via Spin(7) 7-plets w₇ and 𝒴₇ with degenerate positivity; (iii) constant-sharing budget between non-staticity and flux mismatch; plus integral formulas and two no-go theorems.

Conclusion: The unreduced bilinear identity encodes deep geometric and physical rigidity: horizon structure, flux content, and supersymmetry breaking are tightly coupled at the pointwise level — eliminating reliance on global spinor norm assumptions and enabling sharp characterizations of staticity, angular momentum, and symmetry obstructions.

Abstract: On a compact connected section ${\cal S}$ of a supersymmetric $M$-horizon the Killing-spinor bilinears give a pointwise identity relating the warp factor $Δ$, the rotation one-form $h$ and the two spinor norms, with nothing assumed about the norm of the Killing spinor. We derive three consequences of that unreduced identity.
  First, a single-point rigidity theorem: if $Δ$ and $h$ vanish at one point of the section, then the flux vanishes identically, ${\cal S}$ is Ricci-flat and the horizon is $\mathbb{R}^{1,1}\times{\cal S}$. A single point replaces the usual global spinorial hypothesis.
  Second, the ${\rm Spin}(7)$ decomposition of the flux fixes $h$ algebraically and exhibits the invariant flux content of the scalar square $Δ=4Φ^2$ known in an adapted gauge,
  $Δ=\frac{1}{108}\|w_{\bf 7}\pmσ({\cal Y}_{\bf 7})\|^2$.
  Only the two ${\bf 7}$-summands $w_{\bf 7}$, ${\cal Y}_{\bf 7}$ reach the warp factor, and the square is degenerate rather than definite: it vanishes on the linear subspace $w_{\bf 7}=\mpσ({\cal Y}_{\bf 7})$ rather than at the origin, so positivity of $Δ$ yields no case list.
  Third, the two combine into a pointwise budget: the single constant of supersymmetry is shared between the deviation from staticity and the ${\rm Spin}(7)$ mismatch of the flux, each bounded by that constant.
  The same identities characterise the constancy hypothesis, equivalent to $V=-fh$ for the bilinear one-form $V$, which fails on the static branch of known warped $AdS_2$ solutions; they fix a weighted integral of the warp factor; and they reduce the Komar angular momentum of the horizon to a positive bilinear integral. Two no-go statements, for hidden symmetries built from the Killing spinor and for a second isometry from its bilinears, are stated with their hypotheses.

</details>


### [53] [Carrollian Wave Equations for Arbitrary Spin: Anyons and the Exotic Particle on the Noncommutative Plane](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29931)

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs the Carrollian limit of the Cortés--Plyushchay wave equations for planar anyons, yielding a first-order linear system for 'Carrollian anyons'—particles with arbitrary real spin in the ultra-relativistic (c→0) limit. It recovers Carrollian bosons/fermions at (half-)integer spins, identifies a square-root structure of the Carroll wave equation, and shows how scaling spin with c leads to the exotic Carroll algebra with noncommuting boosts and coordinates. Crucially, it contrasts Galilean and Carroll limits via a shared similarity transformation: Galilean eliminates negative-energy modes; Carroll retains both relativistic energy branches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically derive and characterize Carrollian analogues of relativistic anyons—especially their spin structure, wave equations, and symmetry algebras—and clarify how Carrollian physics emerges as a distinct ultra-relativistic limit alongside, and in contrast to, the non-relativistic Galilean limit.

Method: Takes the Carrollian (c → 0) limit of the Cortés--Plyushchay wave equations by grading spin-tower components by powers of c and fixing rest energy; applies a Carrollian analogue of the Jackiw--Nair limit (scaling spin with c); performs symmetry analysis to identify the resulting algebraic structure (standard vs. exotic Carroll algebra); compares with Galilean limit via a common similarity transformation.

Result: A first-order linear wave system for Carrollian anyons of arbitrary real spin; identification of the standard Carroll wave equation (∂ₜ² + E²)Φ = 0 as its second-order descendant with two flat energy branches (±E); derivation of the exotic Carroll algebra featuring a second central charge, non-commuting boosts, and noncommutative position space when spin scales with c; demonstration that Galilean and Carroll limits bifurcate from one similarity transformation—Galilean projects out negative-energy modes, while Carroll preserves both.

Conclusion: Carrollian anyons constitute a consistent, rich class of ultra-relativistic quantum particles with arbitrary spin, governed by a first-order square root of the Carroll wave equation. Their symmetry structure bifurcates into standard and exotic variants depending on spin scaling, and the Carroll limit fundamentally differs from Galilean by retaining full relativistic spectral degeneracy—revealing a deeper structural duality between non-relativistic and ultra-relativistic contractions of Poincaré symmetry.

Abstract: We construct the Carrollian limit of the Cortés--Plyushchay wave equations for planar anyons, grading the spin-tower components by powers of $c$ and taking the limit at fixed rest energy. The result is a linear system describing Carrollian particles of any real spin --- \emph{Carrollian anyons} --- and, at (half-)integer spin, Carrollian bosons, fermions and higher-spin fields.
  The system is a first-order square root of the standard Carroll wave equation $(\partial_t^2+E^2)Φ=0$, whose spectrum consists of two flat branches at plus and minus the rest energy. Scaling the spin along with $c$, in the Carrollian analogue of the Jackiw--Nair limit, yields instead the exotic Carroll algebra, with a second central charge, non-commuting boosts, and observable coordinates spanning a noncommutative plane. We show how the Galilean and Carrollian theories branch from one common similarity transformation: the Galilean limit expels the negative-energy modes, whereas the Carroll limit retains both relativistic branches.

</details>


### [54] [Admissible higher-spin algebras in flat space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30039)
*Dmitry Ponomarev*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper classifies admissible higher-spin algebras in 4D Minkowski space that admit massless higher-spin fields as representations, identifying two types—collinear and half-collinear—and explicitly solving the Jacobi identity to obtain two Lie algebra families plus a commutative associative collinear algebra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify higher-spin algebras compatible with massless higher-spin field representations in 4D Minkowski space, under physically motivated simplifying assumptions.

Method: Imposing admissibility (i.e., existence of a massless higher-spin field representation) and structural ansätze (collinear and half-collinear), then solving the Jacobi identity for Lie algebras and analyzing associativity and commutativity.

Result: Two distinct classes—collinear and half-collinear higher-spin algebras—are identified; for the collinear case, two families of Lie algebras and one associative (commutative unless extended with internal degrees of freedom) algebra are explicitly constructed.

Conclusion: Admissible higher-spin algebras in 4D Minkowski space fall into two structurally distinct types; the collinear type admits explicit classification via Jacobi identity solutions, yielding both Lie and associative instances, while the chiral higher-spin algebra exemplifies the half-collinear type.

Abstract: We study admissible higher-spin algebras in four-dimensional Minkowski space, namely algebras that admit the tower of massless higher-spin fields as a representation. Under several simplifying assumptions, we show that these can be of two types, which we refer to as collinear and half-collinear. The chiral higher-spin algebra provides an example of the latter. For the collinear ansatz considered here, direct solution of the Jacobi identity yields two families of Lie algebras. We also find an associative collinear algebra, which is commutative unless internal degrees of freedom are added.

</details>


### [55] [Generalised Symmetries, Anomalies, and Maximal Branches of 3d Chern-Simons Matter Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30349)
*Fabio Marino,Francesca Pretto,Marcus Sperling*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies generalized symmetries and 't Hooft anomalies in 3D N≥3 Chern-Simons-matter quiver theories from Type IIB branes, determining 1-form and 0-form symmetries, their anomalies, and how 1-form gauging affects Coulomb/Higgs branches via frozen D3-brane sectors and anyon obstruction; it also proposes a magnetic quiver extension with non-simply laced edges to capture symmetry-anomaly data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically characterize generalized global symmetries (0-form and 1-form), their 't Hooft anomalies—including self- and mixed anomalies—and their interplay with maximal moduli space branches in 3D N≥3 linear unitary CS-matter quivers engineered via Type IIB branes.

Method: Combines brane construction analysis, Wilson-line screening, string lattice techniques, fractional/integer monopole endpoint analysis (Gukov-Witten defects), centre charge classification, and anomaly matching; uses 1-form gauging to probe branch structure and proposes magnetic quiver extensions with non-simply laced edges to encode symmetry-anomaly relations.

Result: Determines the full 1-form symmetry group, its self-anomaly, and maximal anomaly-free subgroup; identifies faithful 0-form symmetry (including connected cover and possible 2-group structures) via integer monopole centre charges; finds mixed anomalies with non-abelian 0-form factors cancel; establishes that at most two maximal branches are affected by 1-form gauging due to anyonic obstruction in resulting CS TQFTs; proposes a non-simply laced magnetic quiver extension reproducing the 1-form symmetry and its mixed anomaly with branch isometry.

Conclusion: Generalized symmetries and anomalies in these 3D SCFTs are fully encoded in monopole data and brane geometry; 1-form gauging acts as a diagnostic for branch structure, with topological obstructions (anyons) limiting affected branches; the non-simply laced magnetic quiver provides a precise combinatorial encoding of symmetry-anomaly interplay on moduli branches.

Abstract: The generalised symmetries, 't Hooft anomalies, and their interplay with maximal branches are analysed for linear unitary three-dimensional $\mathcal{N}\geq 3$ Chern-Simons Matter quiver theories realised in Type IIB brane configurations. The 1-form symmetry, its self-anomaly and its maximal anomaly-free subgroup are determined independently from Wilson-line screening and from the string lattice. Fractional monopole endpoints of the generating Gukov-Witten defect are used to extract the anomaly data, while centre charges of integer monopoles determine the faithful 0-form symmetry group, its connected cover and possible 2-group structures. Mixed anomalies with non-abelian 0-form factors are found to cancel in the linear unitary theories considered. The effect of 1-form gauging on maximal branches is related to frozen D3-brane sectors, which flow to Chern-Simons TQFTs whose anyonic lines obstruct the required defect endpoint. This yields a criterion for affected branches and implies that at most two can be affected simultaneously. In such a case, a magnetic-quiver extension by non-simply laced edges is proposed, wherein the 1-form symmetry and its mixed anomaly with the branch isometry are reproduced.

</details>


### [56] [Brane effective actions and their island rule from $T\overline T$ flows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30533)
*Nele Callebaut,Matteo Selle*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives a 2d effective action for semi-classical AdS₃ gravity with an end-of-the-world (EOW) brane of arbitrary tension via radial Hamiltonian flow in a derivative expansion; identifies it holographically as a T\bar{T}-like deformed CFT; proposes that Neumann/conformal AdS₃/bCFT duals correspond to 'freeing' this deformation; verifies the proposal for (A)dS₂-brane saddles; introduces three physically meaningful matter–gravity splits—including one coupling T\bar{T}-deformed CFT to T\bar{T}-like deformed Liouville gravity—and uses the action to compute island-rule entanglement entropy, matching Ryu–Takayanagi exactly and giving Wald entropy interpretation to T\bar{T} entanglement; finally connects the effective theory to fluctuating radial cutoff AdS₃ gravity via on-shell action matching.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a precise holographic dictionary for semi-classical AdS₃ gravity with EOW branes—especially beyond asymptotic boundaries—by deriving a controlled 2d effective description, linking radial Hamiltonian flow to RG flow, and unifying T\bar{T} deformation, Liouville gravity, and quantum extremal islands.

Method: Radial Hamiltonian reduction of AdS₃ gravity with Neumann boundary conditions on an EOW brane; derivative expansion to solve bulk equations order-by-order; identification of the induced 2d dynamics as a T\bar{T}-like deformation; explicit saddle-point evaluation for (A)dS₂ branes; decomposition of the effective action into matter + gravity sectors using conformal anomaly integration; application to island-rule entanglement entropy computation in AdS/bCFT; on-shell action matching with fluctuating-cutoff AdS₃ gravity.

Result: A fully derived 2d effective action for arbitrary-tension EOW branes in AdS₃; explicit mapping to T\bar{T}-like deformed CFT; verification of duality proposal on nontrivial geometries; three consistent matter–gravity splits, one extending Liouville anomaly description into the bulk; exact island-rule entropy matching Ryu–Takayanagi for non-asymptotic branes; Wald entropy interpretation of T\bar{T} entanglement; direct correspondence between higher-derivative corrections and radial cutoff fluctuations.

Conclusion: The semi-classical bulk physics of AdS₃ with EOW branes is precisely captured by a 2d T\bar{T}-like deformed CFT, whose structure encodes both boundary conditions and bulk gravitational dynamics; this framework consistently unifies holographic renormalization, integrable deformations, braneworld gravity, and quantum extremal surfaces—providing a robust foundation for studying non-asymptotic holography and entanglement in lower-dimensional quantum gravity.

Abstract: We derive a $2d$ effective action for semi-classical AdS$_3$ gravity with an EOW brane of arbitrary tension by solving the radial Hamiltonian flow in a derivative expansion. Interpreting the radial flow as an RG flow, the effective theory is identified with a $T\bar T$-like deformed CFT. This leads us to propose that the holographic dual for semi-classical AdS$_3$ gravity with Neumann or conformal boundary conditions is obtained by setting the $T\bar T$-like deformed CFT free. We verify our proposal explicitly for saddles corresponding to $(A)dS_2$ branes in empty AdS$_3$. We discuss three useful ways to decompose the effective brane theory into a matter sector ($T\bar T$-deformed, $T\bar T$-like deformed, and undeformed CFT, respectively) and a corresponding braneworld gravity sector. In particular, the split into $T\bar T$-deformed CFT coupled to $T\bar T$-like deformed Liouville gravity directly extends the Liouville description of the integrated Weyl anomaly into the bulk. The effective action is then used to perform an island-rule calculation for a non-asymptotic brane in an AdS/bCFT set-up, finding exact agreement with the Ryu-Takayanagi result. This extends previous holographic checks on the island rule to non-asymptotic regimes of application, as well as provides a Wald entropy interpretation for $T\bar T$ entanglement. Finally, we relate our effective theory and its higher derivative corrections to AdS$_3$ gravity with a fluctuating radial cutoff by explicit calculation of the on-shell action.

</details>


### [57] [A Truncated Majorana](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30723)
*Ali Vahedi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies quantum field dynamics of truncating a Majorana wave packet using a time-dependent channel shutter, revealing an infrared soft-mode memory with logarithmic divergence and a causal filtering regime; it unifies global Bogoliubov transformations and local observable equivalences, establishing rigorous foundations for time-resolved control in topological quantum systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how time-dependent control—specifically, 'truncating' a Majorana wave packet via a dynamical channel shutter—affects quantum field structure, especially distinguishing between infrared memory effects and causal information filtering in topological platforms.

Method: Analyzes a 1+1D massless Majorana field under time-dependent chiral rotation (modeling truncation as controlled chiral propagation modification, not spatial cutting); employs asymptotic Bogoliubov analysis, Hilbert–Schmidt norm evaluation, and exact algebraic identities for local even-fermion observables.

Result: Identifies two complementary regimes: (i) a global Bogoliubov transformation inducing universal infrared memory β(ω,ν) ∝ (ω+ν)⁻¹ and logarithmic orthogonality catastrophe; (ii) a number-conserving causal regime where retained sector is exactly single-particle and discarded sector is vacuum within the even local observable algebra.

Conclusion: Global infrared memory and local causal truncation are dual manifestations of the same dynamical operation; this provides a field-theoretic benchmark for time-resolved control in topological quantum devices, decoupling operational signatures from microscopic boundary details.

Abstract: We investigate the quantum field dynamics of truncating a Majorana wave packet via a time-dependent channel shutter. Modeling truncation as a controlled modification of chiral propagation rather than a literal spatial cut, we analyze a $1+1$D massless Majorana field subjected to a time-dependent rotation between its chiral components. We demonstrate that this protocol manifests two complementary physical limits. Globally, a mismatch between early- and late-time channel identifications induces a fermionic Bogoliubov transformation. We prove this asymptotic change universally generates an infrared soft-mode memory, $β(ω, ν) \propto (ω+ν)^{-1}$, leading to a logarithmic divergence in the Hilbert--Schmidt norm. This orthogonality-catastrophe-like obstruction persists even under infinitely smooth switching in the massless limit. Conversely, in the number-conserving regime where pair production vanishes, the shutter acts as a purely causal filter. We establish exact local field identities showing that, restricted to the even local observable algebra, the retained sector is exactly equivalent to a single-particle state while the discarded sector reduces to the vacuum. Ultimately, we show that global infrared memory and local causal truncation are complementary diagnostics of the same dynamical operation. These results provide a rigorous field-theoretic foundation for time-dependent control in topological platforms, cleanly separating effective operational benchmarks from microscopic boundary dynamics.

</details>


### [58] [Anomalies and discrete torsion in Type II and Type I worldsheet theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30774)
*Masashi Kawahira,Shotaro Kawanago,Shota Saito,Hiroki Wada*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper classifies worldsheet anomalies of Type II and Type I strings on general 10D target spaces using invertible field theory and Anderson duality, finding anomaly cancellation requires orientability and spin structure; it also characterizes discrete torsion distinguishing string variants.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically characterize and cancel worldsheet anomalies in Type II and Type I string theories on arbitrary ten-dimensional target manifolds, leveraging modern topological field theory tools—specifically invertible field theories classified by the Anderson dual of bordism—to unify and refine anomaly constraints beyond traditional cohomological criteria.

Method: Applies the Yamashita–Yonekura explicit model of the Anderson dual of the relevant bordism theory (e.g., Spin- or SO-bordism) to compute the anomaly invertible field theory; uses bordism-based obstruction theory to derive necessary and sufficient geometric conditions (orientability + spin structure) for anomaly cancellation; analyzes discrete torsion via mapping-class-group actions on partition functions and identifies its role in distinguishing string theory variants.

Result: Anomaly cancellation for both Type II and Type I worldsheet theories occurs iff the target manifold is orientable and admits a spin structure; discrete torsion is fully classified and shown to encode the physical distinctions between Type IIA/IIB and between ordinary Type I and Sugimoto theories.

Conclusion: The topological consistency of Type II and Type I string worldsheet theories on generic 10D backgrounds is governed solely by the existence of a spin structure—no additional differential-geometric or flux-dependent conditions arise at the anomaly level; discrete torsion emerges as a sharp topological invariant distinguishing otherwise similar string constructions.

Abstract: We provide a comprehensive study of anomalies and discrete torsion in the worldsheet theories of Type II and Type I string theories on a general ten-dimensional target space. In the modern understanding, the anomaly of a worldsheet theory is characterized by an invertible field theory in three dimensions, and invertible field theories are classified by the Anderson dual of a suitable bordism theory. To study the anomaly cancellation condition, we employ the explicit model of the Anderson dual constructed by Yamashita and Yonekura. We find that, for both Type II and Type I string theories, the anomaly of the worldsheet theory is cancelled if and only if the target space is orientable and admits a spin structure. We also determine the discrete torsion and clarify its physical meaning. It includes the distinctions between Type IIA and Type IIB string theories and between ordinary Type I and Sugimoto string theories.

</details>


### [59] [Holographic subregion complexity in insulator/superconductor transition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30852)
*Yu Shi,Chikun Ding,Yuebing Zhou,Weike Deng,Sheng Long*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates holographic subregion complexity (HSC) across a backreacted insulator/superconductor transition in an AdS-soliton background, comparing it with holographic entanglement entropy (HEE) and CV-complexity; HSC detects the second-order transition and exhibits distinct discontinuous and scaling behaviors relative to HEE depending on subsystem width and chemical potential, while half-space HSC and CV complexity densities coincide after proper normalization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe whether holographic subregion complexity can reliably diagnose quantum phase transitions—specifically the insulator/superconductor transition—and how its behavior compares to established probes like HEE and CV-complexity, especially under full gravitational backreaction and varying subsystem geometry.

Method: The authors compute HSC, HEE, and CV-complexity for a strip and half-space subsystem in a fully backreacted AdS-soliton black hole geometry dual to the insulator/superconductor transition; they analyze competing Ryu–Takayanagi surfaces (connected vs. disconnected), study dependence on strip width ℓ and chemical potential μ, and perform analytic normalization/subtraction against the insulating reference state.

Result: HSC signals the second-order transition like HEE; for strip subsystems, HSC shows a finite jump at critical width ℓ_c while HEE remains continuous; HSC grows linearly with ℓ beyond ℓ_c whereas HEE saturates; HSC exhibits non-monotonic μ-dependence for ℓ < ℓ_c but monotonic increase for ℓ > ℓ_c; after normalization, half-space HSC and CV complexity densities are analytically identical.

Conclusion: HSC is a viable diagnostic for the insulator/superconductor transition, but its qualitative response depends sensitively on subsystem scale and entanglement-wedge topology—unlike HEE or CV, it is not universally robust across geometries, highlighting the importance of careful geometric and renormalization choices in holographic complexity proposals.

Abstract: We study holographic subregion complexity (HSC) across a fully backreacted insulator/superconductor transition in an AdS-soliton background and compare it with holographic entanglement entropy (HEE) and holographic complexity based on the complexity=volume (CV) proposal. Both HSC and HEE signal the second-order transition. For a strip subsystem, competing connected and disconnected Ryu-Takayanagi surfaces give rise to a confinement/deconfinement transition. At fixed chemical potential in the superconducting phase, HSC exhibits a finite jump at the critical width, whereas HEE remains continuous. Beyond this width, HSC grows linearly with the strip width, while HEE is constant. At fixed strip width, HSC first decreases and then increases with chemical potential for $\ell<\ell_c$, opposite to HEE, but increases monotonically for $\ell>\ell_c$. After consistent normalization and subtraction of the respective insulating references, the half-space HSC and CV complexity densities are analytically identical. These results show that HSC can diagnose the insulator/superconductor transition, but its qualitative response remains sensitive to the subsystem scale and entanglement-wedge topology.

</details>


### [60] [The large $N$ vector model with angular velocity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31151)
*Justin R. David,Srijan Kumar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the large-N free energy of the critical O(N) vector model on S¹×S² with angular velocity μ̂, finding a pole at μ̂²r²=1 in the leading high-temperature term; the pole’s residue matches that of the massless free theory and the pp-wave geometry result, while subleading terms show non-analytic dependence on μ̂ distinguishing the critical fixed point from the free theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand thermal and rotational effects on critical O(N) vector models beyond the singlet constraint, and to test predictions of thermal effective field theory—particularly the emergence and structure of poles in the free energy induced by rotation.

Method: Analytical large-N expansion of the free energy on S¹×S² with angular velocity μ̂, including perturbative expansions near μ̂r = 0 and μ̂²r² = 1; supplemented by numerical evaluation for global interpolation; cross-checked via mapping to the pp-wave geometry for residue consistency.

Result: The leading high-temperature free energy exhibits a simple pole at μ̂²r² = 1; its residue matches both the massless free theory and the pp-wave computation; subleading terms contain non-analytic (e.g., square-root or logarithmic) dependence on (1 − μ̂²r²), distinguishing the critical theory from the free one.

Conclusion: The pole structure and residue confirm thermal EFT expectations, but the non-analytic subleading terms encode genuine strong-coupling, criticality-specific physics absent in the free theory—highlighting the role of interactions even at large N when rotational symmetry is broken.

Abstract: We study the free energy of the critical $O(N)$ vector model at large $N$ on $S^{1}\times S^{2}$ with an angular velocity $\hatμ$ without the singlet constraint. The leading high-temperature behaviour is determined analytically both as an expansion about $\hatμr=0$ and $\hatμ^{2}r^{2}=1$ where $r$ is the radius of the sphere. We supplement the analytic results with a numerical analysis that agrees with both the analytical expansions in their respective regimes and smoothly interpolates between them. The leading high-temperature contribution to the free energy develops a pole at $\hatμ^{2}r^{2}=1$, in agreement with expectations from the thermal effective field theory. Its residue coincides with that of the massless free theory. Sub-leading terms, however, exhibit non-analytic dependence on the angular velocity and distinguish the critical fixed-point result from the free theory answer. The residue at the pole can also be obtained by placing the $O(N)$ model on the pp-wave geometry. We show that the residue agrees with that obtained from the direct computation.

</details>


### [61] [Coarse-grained models for loop quantum gravity and their renormalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31152)
*Mehdi Assanioussi,Martin Zeiß*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces coarse-grained effective theories for canonical loop quantum gravity, built from systematically coarse-grained spin network states and derived effective Hamiltonians, enabling non-perturbative renormalization to study continuum emergence and phenomenology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a framework for studying the continuum limit and deriving phenomenological models in canonical loop quantum gravity via coarse-graining and renormalization.

Method: Systematic coarse-graining of spin network states to construct parameterized coarse Hilbert spaces; derivation of effective Hamiltonians by analyzing how coarse-graining interacts with LQG Hamiltonian operators; implementation of non-perturbative Hamiltonian renormalization with explicit flow equations.

Result: A family of parameterized effective theories defined by coarse Hilbert spaces and effective Hamiltonians; a complete prescription for Hamiltonian non-perturbative renormalization; explicit renormalization flow equations.

Conclusion: The proposed coarse-grained models provide a concrete arena for investigating the emergence of semiclassical spacetime and low-energy physics from canonical loop quantum gravity.

Abstract: We introduce a family of effective theories and use them to define a specific type of coarse-grained models for canonical loop quantum gravity. Each effective theory is characterized by two parameters and it consists mainly of a Hilbert space of coarse states and an effective Hamiltonian operator. The construction of the coarse Hilbert spaces relies on a systematic coarse-graining procedure for spin network states. The effective Hamiltonians are then obtained from an analysis of the interplay between this coarse-graining procedure and the action of various Hamiltonian operators defined in loop quantum gravity. Finally, we provide a prescription for implementing the Hamiltonian non-perturbative renormalization framework for the coarse-grained models, and we explicitly write down the renormalization flow equations. The aim of this work is to build an arena for studying the emergence of the continuum limit and the derivation of phenomenological models in the context of canonical loop quantum gravity.

</details>


### [62] [Superfluid memory effect](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31172)
*Kristan Jensen,Alfredo Pérez,Stefan Prohazka*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper identifies a phonon-scattering-induced memory effect in superfluids, analogous to electromagnetic and gravitational memory, with experimental relevance in superfluid ^4He.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a laboratory analogue of electromagnetic and gravitational memory effects by identifying a corresponding memory phenomenon in quantum fluids.

Method: Theoretical analysis of phonon scattering in superfluids, incorporating soft factor formalism for phonon emission and corrections from nonlinear dispersion.

Result: A localized phonon scattering event induces a far-field pressure pulse with vanishing time integral but nonzero first temporal moment—implying a permanent shift in the prepotential for velocity—i.e., a memory effect.

Conclusion: Phonon scattering in superfluids exhibits a measurable memory effect controlled by the soft factor and nonlinear dispersion; this provides a controllable quantum fluid platform for studying memory phenomena akin to those in gauge and gravity theories.

Abstract: We identify a memory effect produced by phonon scattering in a superfluid: a localized scattering event generates a far-field pressure pulse with vanishing time integral but a nonzero first temporal moment, corresponding to a permanent shift in a prepotential for the far-field velocity. The effect is controlled by the soft factor for phonon emission, including corrections from nonlinear dispersion. In superfluid $^4$He, scattering macroscopic phonon wave packets produces a signal whose estimated magnitude may lie within experimental reach, thereby providing a laboratory analogue of electromagnetic and gravitational memory.

</details>

### [63] [Constrained functional priors for Bayesian inference of hot QCD matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28786)
*G. Guimarães,L. Perin,M. Luzum*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a nonparametric Gaussian process framework for constructing physically constrained functional priors in Bayesian analyses of heavy-ion collisions, using truncated Karhunen–Loève expansions for emulator inputs and comparing rejection-based vs. pushforward constraint enforcement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the significant uncertainty arising from ad hoc low-dimensional parametrizations of functional inputs (e.g., transport coefficients, equation of state) in Bayesian heavy-ion collision analyses—particularly their artificial correlations and sensitivity to assumed functional forms.

Method: Develops a Gaussian process (GP)-based nonparametric prior framework; employs truncated Karhunen–Loève (KL) expansions for dimensionality reduction; analyzes incorporation of physical constraints via two strategies: (i) rejection sampling (inducing non-Gaussian measures and coefficient correlations), and (ii) pushforward constructions (preserving tractable Gaussianity in a latent space).

Result: Pushforward-constrained KL representations maintain analytically tractable Gaussian priors in latent space while rigorously enforcing physical constraints (e.g., positivity, monotonicity); rejection-based methods yield distorted, non-Gaussian posterior measures and spurious correlations among expansion coefficients; the framework enables systematic, flexible, and statistically sound functional priors for emulator-based inference.

Conclusion: The pushforward GP-KL framework provides a principled, scalable, and physically faithful method for specifying functional priors in Bayesian heavy-ion phenomenology—resolving key limitations of conventional parametric approaches and clarifying how physical constraints shape statistical structure in finite-dimensional emulator inputs.

Abstract: Bayesian analyses of heavy-ion collisions rely on functional inputs, such as temperature-dependent transport coefficients and the equation of state, whose prior specification remains a significant source of uncertainty. Standard approaches employ low-dimensional parametrizations that can impose artificial correlations and leave the resulting inference sensitive to the assumed functional form.
  We develop a nonparametric framework for constructing priors over such functions using Gaussian processes, tailored to emulator-based inference in heavy-ion phenomenology. Truncated Karhunen--Loève expansions yield optimized finite-dimensional representations suitable for use as emulator inputs. We investigate how physical constraints can be incorporated into these priors, demonstrating that rejection-based methods produce non-Gaussian measures and induce nontrivial correlations in the expansion coefficients. By contrast, pushforward constructions enforce the constraints while preserving a tractable Gaussian measure in a latent space.
  These results provide a systematic framework for incorporating physical constraints into functional priors and clarify their effects on the statistical structure of the finite-dimensional representations used in Bayesian inference.

</details>


### [64] [First Evidence for an Unambiguous Triangle Singularity from $ψ(2S) \to p\bar{p}η$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29006)
*Qi Huang,Yi-Jia Zeng,Xiao-Rui Lyu,Rong-Gang Ping,Jia-Jun Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reports the first compelling experimental evidence for a triangle singularity in the $pη$ invariant mass spectrum of $ψ(2S) → p\bar{p}η$, observed as a cusp at 1.564 GeV—matching the predicted kinematic position—and confirmed with 3.8σ significance via χ² improvement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide direct experimental confirmation of the long-predicted (since 1959) triangle singularity—a purely kinematic enhancement from hadronic rescattering loops—whose role in anomalous decays and exotic hadron interpretations remains unverified.

Method: Analysis of the $pη/\bar{p}η$ invariant mass spectrum from $ψ(2S) → p\bar{p}η$ data collected by BESIII; fitting with and without inclusion of the triangle singularity loop contribution, using kinematic constraints and resonance modeling near the $N(1535)$ region.

Result: A clear cusp-like structure is observed at 1.564 GeV, precisely matching the predicted kinematic location of the triangle singularity; inclusion of the triangle loop improves χ²/d.o.f. from 1.22 to 0.90, yielding a 3.8σ statistical significance for the effect.

Conclusion: The observation constitutes the first compelling experimental evidence for a triangle singularity, validating Landau’s 1959 prediction and establishing its physical relevance in hadronic processes.

Abstract: Triangle singularities, predicted by Landau in 1959, are purely kinematic enhancements arising from hadronic rescattering loops. Despite their proposed role in various anomalous decay processes and exotic hadron candidates, a direct experimental confirmation has remained elusive for more than six decades. We analyze the $pη/\bar{p}η$ invariant mass spectrum in $ψ(2S) \to p\bar{p}η$ measured by the BESIII Collaboration. The data exhibit a clear cusp-like structure around 1.564~GeV in the $N(1535)$ region, in precise agreement with the kinematic position predicted for the triangle singularity. Including the triangle singularity loop in the fit substantially improves the description of the data, with $χ^2/\mathrm{d.o.f.}$ decreasing from 1.22 to 0.90, corresponding to a significance of $\sim 3.8σ$ for the triangle singularity contribution. The precise alignment of the observed excess with the predicted kinematic position provides the first compelling evidence for the triangle singularity effect.

</details>


### [65] [Towards power corrections in the factorization of baryon quasi-distribution amplitudes in LaMET](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29042)
*Yu-Ji Shi,Jun Zeng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives an exact closed-form relation resumming target-mass corrections to all orders for the leading-twist baryon quasi-distribution amplitude (quasi-DA), establishes its moment relation to the light-cone distribution amplitude (LCDA), assesses corrections numerically for the Λ baryon using lattice data, and constructs next-to-leading-twist operators in quasi-DA factorization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically quantify power corrections—especially target-mass and higher-twist effects—in the large-momentum effective theory (LaMET) framework for baryon quasi-DAs, which are crucial for precise extraction of light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs) from lattice QCD.

Method: Analytical derivation of moment relations between quasi-DA and LCDA; all-orders resummation of target-mass corrections at leading twist; explicit construction of next-to-leading-twist operators in quasi-DA factorization; numerical evaluation using existing lattice data for the Λ baryon.

Result: An exact closed-form relation resumming target-mass corrections to all orders for leading-twist baryon quasi-DAs; confirmation that such corrections vanish rapidly with increasing hadron momentum and are negligible in endpoint regions for Λ; explicit identification and construction of next-to-leading-twist operators contributing to quasi-DA factorization.

Conclusion: The derived formalism enables more accurate, controlled extractions of baryon LCDAs from lattice QCD via LaMET; the results lay the groundwork for quantifying and mitigating power corrections in future high-precision studies of both light and heavy baryons.

Abstract: Light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs) are essential to precision phenomenological studies. They can be accessed from lattice QCD through the large-momentum effective theory (LaMET) via quasi-distribution amplitudes (quasi-DAs). Factorization of quasi-DAs receive power corrections in inverse powers of the hadron momentum, including target-mass and higher-twist corrections. In this work, we present the first systematic analysis of such power corrections for the leading-twist baryon quasi-DA. Establishing the moment relation between the quasi-DA and the LCDA, we derive an exact closed-form relation that resums target-mass correction to all orders at leading twist. This result also applies to heavy baryons and to quasi-transverse-momentum-dependent distributions. We numerically assess these corrections for the $Λ$ baryon quasi-DA using existing lattice data, finding that the target-mass correction decreases rapidly with increasing baryon momentum and is almost negligible in the endpoint regions. In addition, we explicitly construct the next-to-leading-twist operators entering the quasi-DA factorization. Our results are a first step toward quantifying the power corrections in future lattice determinations of light or heavy baryon LCDAs.

</details>


### [66] [Taming lepton portal dark matter by a non-invertible selection rule](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29051)
*Junichiro Kawamura*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a non-invertible selection rule—via gauging Z2 of a Z5 symmetry—to suppress flavor-violating couplings in lepton-portal dark matter while preserving large PMNS mixing angles, and explores experimental signatures in lepton flavor universality tests (e.g., Z → ℓℓ′).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile lepton-portal dark matter with stringent direct detection bounds and observed large neutrino mixing angles, which invertible symmetries fail to accommodate due to unavoidable flavor violation in portal couplings.

Method: Constructs a non-invertible symmetry by gauging a Z2 subgroup of a global Z5 flavor symmetry; analyzes consistency with the PMNS matrix; embeds the setup in a type-I seesaw framework to assess residual flavor leakage; computes implications for Z-boson decay observables, particularly LFU violation in Z → ℓℓ′.

Result: Demonstrates that the Z2-gauged Z5 model achieves one-flavor-dominated portal couplings without conflicting with PMNS data; identifies suppressed but potentially measurable flavor leakage in seesaw completion; predicts testable deviations in Z → eμ, eτ, μτ branching ratios.

Conclusion: Non-invertible symmetries provide a viable, predictive mechanism to control flavor structure in lepton-portal WIMP dark matter, opening new avenues for indirect and collider probes via precision lepton observables.

Abstract: We point out that a non-invertible selection rule can control the flavor violation inherent in lepton portal dark matter, a weakly interacting massive particle candidate that can evade severe constraints from direct detection experiments. We show that ordinary invertible symmetries cannot suppress flavor-violating portal couplings while accommodating the large mixing angles in the PMNS matrix. We then present a simple construction based on the $\mathbb{Z}_2$ gauging of a $\mathbb{Z}_5$ symmetry, which realizes one-flavor dominance of the portal couplings consistently with the PMNS mixing matrix. We also discuss possible flavor leakage in a type-I seesaw completion and study experimental probes of the model through lepton observables, especially lepton flavor non-universality in $Z$ boson decays.

</details>


### [67] [Higgs boson pair production in SMEFT: shape and truncation studies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29132)
*Manosch Bär,Bakar Chargeishvili,Ramona Gröber,Gudrun Heinrich,Konstantin Schmid*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper compares how Wilson coefficients affect Higgs boson pair production in SMEFT (up to dimension-6, including loop-suppressed operators), incorporating NLO QCD corrections and polynomial parametrizations; it identifies benchmark scenarios with significant shape distortions in $m_{hh}$ and $p_{T,h}$ distributions, revealing cases where the linear SMEFT truncation fails due to unphysical cross sections when quadratic terms are omitted.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the validity and limitations of the linear SMEFT expansion in describing Higgs boson pair production, particularly under large anomalous couplings, by examining differential observables sensitive to higher-order Wilson coefficient contributions.

Method: Comparative analysis of SMEFT dimension-6 Wilson coefficient effects on hh production cross sections, including loop-suppressed operators and NLO QCD corrections; uses updated polynomial parametrizations of the cross section in Wilson coefficients; focuses on $m_{hh}$ and $p_{T,h}$ differential distributions; identifies benchmark scenarios with shape distortions.

Result: Several benchmark scenarios show pronounced distortions in $m_{hh}$ and $p_{T,h}$ distributions; some remain physically consistent under linear SMEFT truncation, while others yield unphysical differential cross sections when quadratic dimension-6 contributions are omitted.

Conclusion: The linear SMEFT approximation breaks down for large anomalous couplings in hh production, as evidenced by unphysical differential cross sections when quadratic Wilson coefficient terms are neglected—highlighting the necessity of including higher-order SMEFT terms for robust phenomenological interpretation.

Abstract: We present a comparative study of the influence of Wilson coefficients on Higgs boson pair production cross sections in SMEFT up to dimension-6, also including loop-suppressed operators. Our study accounts for NLO QCD corrections, and is based on updated parametrizations of the cross section as a polynomial in the Wilson coefficients. We focus on differential distributions for the Higgs boson pair invariant mass, as well as the transverse momenta of the leading Higgs bosons, identifying benchmark scenarios exhibiting pronounced shape distortions in the $m_{hh}$ and $p_{T,h}$ distributions. While some of these scenarios remain physically meaningful under a purely linear truncation of the SMEFT expansion, others yield unphysical differential cross sections when quadratic dimension-6 contributions are omitted, exposing the breakdown of the linear approximation for large anomalous couplings.

</details>


### [68] [Probing strange-sector flavor-changing neutral currents at DUNE-like facilities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29178)
*Xin-Shuai Yan,Miao Liu,Qin Chang,Ya-Dong Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies DUNE-like experiments' sensitivity to flavor-changing neutral-current (FCNC) interactions between down and strange quarks via inclusive deep-inelastic neutrino scattering, using a general effective Lagrangian for Dirac/Majorana neutrinos; it projects near-detector bounds on dimensionless Wilson coefficients at O(10⁻³–10⁻²) (relative to G_F), with far-detector limits ~10× weaker, and identifies background-rate uncertainty as dominant for near-detector and signal-rate uncertainty for far-detector sensitivities; dependence on neutrino mass ordering and δ_CP is mostly limited to electron-flavor FCNC coefficients.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe new physics beyond the Standard Model via flavor-changing neutral-current (FCNC) interactions involving down and strange quarks—highly suppressed in the SM—using high-precision neutrino scattering at DUNE-like facilities.

Method: Employed a general low-energy effective Lagrangian for Dirac and Majorana neutrinos; performed sensitivity projections for CP- and τ-optimized beam configurations; analyzed inclusive deep-inelastic scattering events at near and far detectors; incorporated statistical and systematic uncertainties—including background-rate (near detector) and signal-rate (far detector) uncertainties—and assessed dependence on neutrino mass ordering and δ_CP.

Result: Near-detector projected bounds on dimensionless Wilson coefficients reach O(10⁻³–10⁻²) relative to G_F; far-detector bounds are ~10× weaker; background-rate uncertainty dominates near-detector sensitivity, while signal-rate uncertainty limits far-detector reach; mass ordering and δ_CP affect only electron-flavor FCNC coefficients significantly.

Conclusion: DUNE-like facilities offer competitive sensitivity to ds-quark FCNC interactions, especially at the near detector; systematic uncertainties—not statistics—are the primary limiting factor; results highlight the importance of controlling background and signal modeling, and underscore limited degeneracies with standard oscillation parameters for most FCNC channels.

Abstract: We investigate the sensitivity of DUNE-like facilities to flavor-changing
  neutral-current interactions between down and strange quarks through inclusive
  deep-inelastic neutrino scattering. In the framework of a general low-energy
  effective Lagrangian for Dirac and Majorana neutrinos, we evaluate projected
  sensitivities for CP- and $τ$-optimized beam configurations. The strongest
  bounds on the dimensionless Wilson coefficients, defined relative to the Fermi
  constant $G_F$, reach $\mathcal{O}(10^{-3})\text{--}\mathcal{O}(10^{-2})$ at the
  near detector, whereas far-detector limits are roughly one order of magnitude
  weaker. When systematic uncertainties are included, the near-detector
  sensitivity is dominated by the background-rate uncertainty, while the
  far-detector sensitivity is limited primarily by the signal-rate uncertainty.
  At the far detector,
  the dependence of the projected bounds on the neutrino mass ordering and the Dirac
  CP-violating phase $δ_{\mathrm{CP}}$ is largely confined to coefficients
  involving the electron flavor.

</details>


### [69] [Embedding dependence of fermion mass hierarchies in $S_3$-symmetric Yukawa sectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29259)
*Ye Zhou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how embedding choices of irreducible representations in family-symmetric Yukawa tensors affect mass spectra, focusing on S₃ symmetry with three generations. It shows that varying the embedding (parameterized by CP¹×CP¹) can accommodate arbitrary positive mass hierarchies, while fixed embeddings impose finite hierarchy bounds; a specific algebraic condition (2tκ²=1) characterizes unbounded σ₃/σ₂ ratios, and application to a Clifford-algebraic model reveals an inherent obstruction to reproducing the observed charged-lepton mass hierarchy due to its fixed embedding.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the freedom in embedding degenerate irreducible representations—beyond group-theoretic assignment—affects physical observables like fermion mass spectra in family-symmetric models.

Method: Mathematical analysis of invariant subspaces of complex-symmetric 3×3 tensors under S₃ action, classification of Yukawa subspaces as a CP¹×CP¹ family 𝒯_{t,κ}, solution of inverse singular-value problems, and application to a concrete Clifford-algebraic Yukawa construction.

Result: A necessary and sufficient condition (2tκ² = 1) for existence of rank-one matrices—and thus unbounded σ₃/σ₂—in the embedding family; demonstration that varying embedding lifts finite hierarchy bounds; identification that the Clifford model lies in 𝒯_{2,0}, leading to singular value inequality m₁ + m₂ ≤ m₃ ≤ m₁ + 3m₂, which excludes the observed charged-lepton mass hierarchy.

Conclusion: The obstruction to reproducing realistic fermion mass hierarchies in S₃-symmetric models is not due to the representation content alone but critically depends on the fixed multiplicity embedding; embedding flexibility is essential for spectral versatility.

Abstract: When irreducible representations occur with multiplicity, a family symmetry fixes the representation content of a Yukawa sector but need not fix its embedding in the space of generation tensors. We study the spectral consequences of this additional embedding data for complex-symmetric three-family tensors with the $S_3$ assignment $V=\mathbf{1}\oplus\mathbf{2}$. Since $\operatorname{Sym}^2V=2\,\mathbf{1}\oplus2\,\mathbf{2}$, three-dimensional invariant Yukawa subspaces of type $\mathbf{1}\oplus\mathbf{2}$ form a continuous $\mathbb{CP}^1\times\mathbb{CP}^1$ family, denoted $\mathcal L_{t,κ}$ on a finite affine chart. On the $κ=0$ branch, we solve the inverse singular-value problem and obtain necessary and sufficient conditions for any prescribed ordered positive mass spectrum. A fixed singlet embedding imposes a finite hierarchy bound, whereas varying the embedding accommodates every positive three-family spectrum. For general embeddings, $\mathcal L_{t,κ}$ contains a nonzero rank-one matrix if and only if $2tκ^2=1$; equivalently, these are precisely the finite-chart embeddings for which $σ_3/σ_2$ is unbounded. In a conventional complex-symmetric $S_3$ three-Higgs sector, the same condition becomes $y_2y_5^2=\sqrt{2}\,y_1y_3^2$, so order-one Yukawa coupling ratios can support arbitrarily large mass hierarchies. We then apply the result to a recent Clifford-algebraic construction and show that its family-resolved Yukawa tensors span $\mathcal L_{2,0}$, whose singular values obey $m_1+m_2\leq m_3\leq m_1+3m_2$. This excludes the observed charged-lepton hierarchy for arbitrary complex vacuum alignment within the existing Higgs directions. The resulting obstruction is therefore tied to the fixed multiplicity embedding rather than to the $S_3$ representation content alone.

</details>


### [70] [Probing Ultra-Compressed Scotogenic Dark Matter at the HL-LHC via 4D Spacetime Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29275)
*Renjie Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a novel 4D timing-assisted search strategy at HL-LHC for the ultra-compressed scotogenic model, leveraging ~30 ps precision timing detectors to identify displaced leptons from charged inert scalar decays (cτ ~ 100–1000 mm → Δt ~ 70–700 ps), achieving high signal efficiency (12–36%) and zero background in simulation, enabling exclusion of scalars up to ~670 GeV at 3000 fb⁻¹.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing LHC searches (displaced leptons, disappearing tracks) have poor, unoptimized sensitivity to the ultra-compressed scotogenic regime where co-annihilation yields correct dark matter relic density; the 30 ps timing resolution of HL-LHC detectors provides a new discriminant against prompt SM backgrounds.

Method: Exploits arrival-time delay (70–700 ps) of decay leptons as a fourth observable (beyond x, y, z, p_T), using low-p_T disappearing-track stubs matched to delayed timing hits—without full lepton reconstruction—to isolate long-lived charged scalars with cτ ≈ 100–1000 mm.

Result: Signal efficiency 12–36% (14% at 200 GeV benchmark); zero background in 4.20×10⁶ simulated prompt SM events; projected 95% C.L. exclusion reach up to ≈670 GeV scalar mass at 3000 fb⁻¹; timing discriminates signal from sub-ps prompt backgrounds by >3 orders of magnitude.

Conclusion: The co-annihilation corridor of the scotogenic model—traditionally elusive—is accessible via 4D spacetime tracking at HL-LHC; small Yukawa couplings yielding optimal timing window are compatible with (though not uniquely fixed by) radiative neutrino mass generation.

Abstract: The ultra-compressed scotogenic regime, in which a 2\,GeV mass splitting between the charged inert scalar and the dark-matter fermion is motivated by and compatible with co-annihilation to the observed relic density for suitable choices of the remaining model parameters, produces decay leptons for which existing LHC displaced-lepton and disappearing-track searches retain only partial, unoptimised acceptance, leaving room for a dedicated timing-assisted strategy. We propose exploiting the 30\,ps timing resolution of the HL-LHC precision timing detectors as a fourth observable, converting the macroscopic scalar decay length of 100--1000\,mm into an arrival-time delay of 70--700\,ps---more than three orders of magnitude above the sub-picosecond delays of all prompt Standard Model backgrounds. Operating at the level of a low-$p_T$ disappearing-track stub matched to a delayed timing hit, without requiring full lepton reconstruction, our 4D timing strategy achieves signal efficiencies of 12--36\% across the probed mass range (14\% at the 200\,GeV benchmark) and zero background across $4.20\times10^6$ simulated prompt SM events, projecting 95\%\,C.L.\ exclusion up to scalar masses of $\simeq\!670$\,GeV at 3000\,fb$^{-1}$ under conservative background assumptions. The scalar lifetime is set by the Yukawa coupling $y$ through $cτ\propto y^{-2}$, while sub-eV neutrino masses constrain only the product $y^2λ_5$; the small couplings that place the charged scalar in the optimal timing window are therefore compatible with, though not uniquely fixed by, the radiative neutrino-mass mechanism, making the co-annihilation corridor---conventionally the most elusive regime---an accessible target for 4D spacetime tracking at the HL-LHC.

</details>


### [71] [Finite-Width Dissolution of Radial Spectroscopy in Single-Top Mesonic Correlations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29476)
*Bing-Dong Wan,Shuo Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how the width of a heavy unstable particle (like the top quark) influences bound-state spectra in the heavy-mass expansion, showing that the pole width inherits the constituent width up to $\mathcal{O}(\Lambda_{\rm kin}^2/m_Q^2)$, while radial splittings remain $\mathcal{O}(\Lambda_{\rm rad})$. Using a non-Hermitian Bethe–Salpeter framework with complex pole mass, it confirms width inheritance via Hellmann–Feynman and numerical scans, and demonstrates dissolution of narrow radial peaks into a single broad resonance—implying top-bound states lack resolvable multi-peak structure but may leave qualitative signatures in $Wb\bar q$ final states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the intrinsic width of an unstable heavy constituent (e.g., top quark) affects the spectrum and observability of hadronic bound states, especially in light of its short lifetime and large width relative to typical QCD scales.

Method: Employs the heavy-mass expansion within a non-Hermitian instantaneous Bethe–Salpeter framework; implements the complex top pole mass; uses biorthogonal Hellmann–Feynman theorem to establish width inheritance at operator level; performs artificial $m_Q$-scan to verify $m_Q^{-2}$ scaling; computes low-pole source-projected response and full-matrix resolvent for spectral analysis.

Result: Width inheritance holds up to $\mathcal{O}(\Lambda_{\rm kin}^2/m_Q^2)$; radial splittings remain $\mathcal{O}(\Lambda_{\rm rad})$; spectral response shows only one broad maximum at physical top width across $t\bar b$, $t\bar c$, $t\bar u$; narrow radial peaks dissolve under full width-dependent re-diagonalization; stable-top eigenvalues persist as reference poles but not as resolvable peaks.

Conclusion: Top-quark-containing bound states do not yield a resolvable multi-peak radial spectrum due to width-induced smearing; instead, they produce broad, process-dependent signatures—e.g., threshold enhancements or modified color flow—in $Wb\bar q$ channels, limiting direct spectroscopic observation but allowing indirect phenomenological impact.

Abstract: Within the heavy-mass expansion, the pole width of a system containing one unstable heavy constituent inherits the constituent width up to $\mathcal O(Λ_{\rm kin}^2/m_Q^2)$ corrections, while radial splittings remain $\mathcal O(Λ_{\rm rad})$. The top quark is an extreme realization of this hierarchy. We implement the complex top pole mass in an instantaneous Bethe--Salpeter framework, where a biorthogonal Hellmann--Feynman relation realizes width inheritance at the operator level and an artificial heavy-mass scan confirms the predicted $m_Q^{-2}$ suppression. The low-pole source-projected response has a single broad maximum at the physical top width in the $t\bar b$, $t\bar c$, and $t\bar u$ channels. Full width-dependent non-Hermitian re-diagonalization and a direct full-matrix resolvent evaluation confirm the progressive dissolution of the small-width radial maxima. Thus stable-top eigenvalues survive as reference poles but not as a resolvable multi-peak spectrum; they may instead leave qualitative, process-dependent $Wb\bar q$ signatures, such as a broad threshold enhancement or modified color flow.

</details>


### [72] [Probability-based Estimates of the Uncalculated N$^5$LO QCD Contribution to the Hadronic $W$-Boson Decay Width](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29666)
*Shu-Heng Yang,Jiang Yan,Xing-Gang Wu,Zhi-Fei Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper estimates the missing N^5LO QCD correction to the W boson hadronic decay width using Bayesian analysis on a PMC-improved perturbative series, yielding a QCD correction uncertainty of ±1.0×10⁻⁵ and a hadronic branching ratio of (65.84±1.54)%, consistent with experiment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The exact N^5LO perturbative QCD correction to the W boson hadronic decay width is computationally intractable; thus, a robust estimation method is needed to quantify its contribution and reduce theoretical uncertainty.

Method: Bayesian analysis (BA) is applied to a perturbative series improved via the Principle of Maximum Conformality (PMC), which eliminates renormalization scheme and scale ambiguities by absorbing non-conformal terms into the running coupling α_s, resulting in a scale-invariant series with optimized convergence; the PMC scale Q_* = 100.102 GeV is fixed at NNLL accuracy.

Result: The BA yields a 95.5% credible interval for the N^5LO QCD correction: Δδ_QCD|_PMC,BA^N^5LO = ±1.0×10⁻⁵; the final PMC-predicted hadronic branching ratio is B(W→hadrons)|_PMC = (65.84±1.54)%.

Conclusion: The PMC+BA approach provides a reliable, model-independent estimate of the missing N^5LO term, significantly improving the precision of the theoretical prediction for W hadronic decay, and confirms consistency with current experimental measurements.

Abstract: The perturbative QCD corrections to the hadronic decay width of the $W$ boson are currently known up to next-to-next-to-next-to-next-to-leading order ($\mathrm{N^4LO}$), whereas the exact $\mathrm{N^5LO}$ correction remains unavailable owing to its formidable computational complexity. In this work, we estimate the $\mathrm{N^5LO}$ contributions by adopting Bayesian analysis (BA). Before performing the estimation, the Principle of Maximum Conformality (PMC) is employed to improve the precision of the initial scale-dependent perturbative series. Through recursive application of the renormalization group equation, non-conformal terms are absorbed into the strong running coupling, yielding a scheme-independent, scale-invariant perturbative series with improved convergence. The PMC procedure determines an effective coupling $α_s(Q_*)$, with the PMC scale fixed as $Q_* = 100.102~\mathrm{GeV}$ at next-to-next-to-leading logarithmic accuracy. Based on the improved and more precise series, the $95.5\%$ BA credible interval yields an uncertainty of $Δδ_\mathrm{QCD}|_{\mathrm{PMC, BA}}^{\mathrm{N^5LO}} = \pm 1.0\times 10^{-5}$. The resulting hadronic branching ratio is $\mathcal{B}(W\to \text{hadrons})|_\mathrm{PMC} = (65.84\pm 1.54)\%$, which is consistent with experimental data within reasonable errors.

</details>


### [73] [Meson gravitational D-form factors and symmetry breaking in low-energy QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30434)
*Mamiya Kawaguchi,Kazuhiro Tanaka,Mitsuru Tanaka*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies meson gravitational D-form factors in the three-flavor linear sigma model, linking them to chiral and scale symmetry breaking in low-energy QCD; it derives analytical expressions in symmetric/chiral limits, identifies anomaly-driven contributions for singlets, quantifies flavor-breaking effects on scalar-meson D-form factors, and contrasts predictions with dilaton-augmented chiral perturbation theory—highlighting how D-form factors probe symmetry-breaking mechanisms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how meson gravitational D-form factors encode information about chiral and scale symmetry breaking in low-energy QCD, particularly distinguishing contributions from spontaneous symmetry breaking, the $U(1)_A$ anomaly, and explicit flavor breaking.

Method: Analytical computation of meson D-form factors within the three-flavor linear sigma model; derivation in the exact SU(3) symmetric limit and chiral limit; incorporation of explicit flavor breaking via scalar-meson mass hierarchy; comparison with dilaton-augmented chiral perturbation theory based on canonical mass dimension analysis of interaction terms.

Result: In the chiral limit, octet pseudoscalar D-form factor forward value is fixed at −1; $U(1)_A$ anomaly induces nonzero contributions to singlet pseudoscalar and scalar-meson D-form factors; realistic flavor breaking strongly modifies scalar-meson D-form factors; differences between linear sigma model and dilaton-chPT stem from distinct mass-dimension assignments of symmetry-breaking interactions.

Conclusion: Meson D-form factors are sensitive, model-discriminating probes of symmetry-breaking patterns—including spontaneous chiral breaking, axial anomaly effects, and explicit flavor violation—in low-energy hadron physics.

Abstract: We investigate meson gravitational D-form factors in the three-flavor linear sigma model and their connection to symmetry breaking in low-energy QCD. Within the scalar meson dominance picture, we examine the scalar meson exchange contributions and their relation to meson masses through scalar-meson couplings. In the three-flavor symmetric limit, we first derive analytical expressions for the D-form factors of pseudoscalar and scalar mesons. In the chiral limit, the forward-limit value of the octet pseudoscalar D-form factor is fixed to $-1$, whereas the $U(1)_A$ anomaly generates additional contributions to the singlet pseudoscalar and scalar-meson D-form factors. For realistic flavor breaking, the interaction introduced to reproduce the scalar-meson mass hierarchy below $1$ GeV significantly affects the scalar-meson D-form factors. We further compare the linear sigma model results with those obtained from a chiral perturbation theory Lagrangian including a dilatonic scalar field and show that their differences are governed by the canonical mass dimensions of the interaction terms contributing to meson mass generation. These results indicate that meson D-form factors provide sensitive probes of symmetry-breaking structures in low-energy hadron physics.

</details>


### [74] [On-shell renormalization in NREFT: a simplified description of resonances, bound states, and coupled channel effects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29683)
*Guo-Ying Chen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper generalizes Weinberg's compositeness criterion using on-shell renormalization in Non-Relativistic Effective Field Theory (NREFT), reproducing Weinberg's original result and extending it to describe resonances, bound states, and coupled-channel effects—including a full propagator for near-threshold p-wave states with charged channels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To modernize and extend Weinberg's compositeness criterion—originally formulated for S-wave bound states—to broader physical scenarios including resonances, p-wave states, coupled channels, and charged-particle effects within a consistent effective field theory framework.

Method: Applies on-shell renormalization within Non-Relativistic Effective Field Theory (NREFT) to systematically derive and generalize Weinberg’s compositeness condition; constructs the complete propagator for near-threshold p-wave systems, incorporating charged-channel contributions.

Result: Demonstrates exact reproduction of Weinberg’s original compositeness criterion under on-shell renormalization in NREFT; provides a unified, model-independent description of resonances, bound states, and coupled-channel dynamics; derives the full propagator for near-threshold p-wave states including electromagnetic (charged) channel effects.

Conclusion: On-shell renormalization in NREFT offers a robust, physically transparent, and generalizable framework for compositeness analysis—extending Weinberg’s criterion beyond its original S-wave, neutral-particle limitations while preserving its foundational interpretation.

Abstract: We discuss several issues regarding the modern generalization of Weinberg's compositeness criterion. In particular, we demonstrate that the on-shell renormalization in NREFT reproduces Weinberg's result and provides a simple description of resonances, bound states, and coupled channel effects. Additionally, we present the complete propagator, including the charged channel for the near-threshold p-wave states.

</details>


### [75] [$(g-2)_{\ell}$ and LFV decays in a $U(1)_{L_μ -L_τ}$ left-right model with inverse seesaw neutrinos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29697)
*L. T. Hue,Vo Quoc Phong,T. D. Tham,N. H. T. Nha*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores a U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ} left-right model with inverse seesaw neutrinos and shows that one-loop contributions from heavy singly charged Higgs bosons can explain the (g−2)_μ and (g−2)_e anomalies, while lepton-flavor violating processes are highly suppressed due to the gauged symmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile the observed discrepancies in the anomalous magnetic moments of the muon and electron ((g−2)_μ and (g−2)_e) within a theoretically consistent framework that also suppresses lepton-flavor violation.

Method: Analyzes one-loop contributions from heavy singly charged Higgs bosons to (g−2)_e,μ in a U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ} left-right model with inverse seesaw neutrino mass generation; evaluates constraints from lepton-flavor violating decays (h→e_b±e_a∓, Z→e_b±e_a∓, e_b→e_aγ) imposed by the gauged symmetry.

Result: The model accommodates the (g−2)_μ discrepancy (~10⁻⁹) and the smaller (g−2)_e anomaly (~O(10⁻¹³)); all considered lepton-flavor violating decay rates are highly suppressed due to the U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ} gauge symmetry.

Conclusion: The U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ} left-right model with inverse seesaw provides a viable explanation for both (g−2) anomalies while naturally suppressing dangerous lepton-flavor violating processes.

Abstract: In the framework of a $U(1)_{L_μ -L_τ}$ left-right model with inverse seesaw neutrinos recently proposed, we show that one-loop contributions from heavy singly charged Higgs bosons to the anomalous magnetic moments of charged leptons $(g-2)_{e,μ}$ can accommodate the observed discrepancies, of $10^{-9}$ and $\mathcal{O}(10^{-13})$ for the muon and electron, respectively. Meanwhile, all lepton-flavor violating decay rates for $h\to e_b^\pm e_a^\mp $, $Z\to e_b^\pm e_a^\mp$, and $e_b\to e_a γ$ are found to be highly suppressed as a consequence of the gauged $U(1)_{L_μ-L_τ}$ symmetry.

</details>


### [76] [From Threshold Crossing to Wave-function Renormalization: Defining the Pion Mott Temperature in a Magnetic Field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31125)
*Sidney S. Avancini,Maximo Coppola,Dyana C. Duarte,Ricardo L. S. Farias,Norberto N. Scoccola,William R. Tavares*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies neutral pion dissociation in hot, magnetized quark matter using the two-flavor NJL model, proposing an inflection-point-based definition of the magnetic Mott temperature via the pion wave-function renormalization factor Z_π⁰(T,eB), revealing pion dissociation as a spectral crossover—not a sharp threshold crossing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguity in defining the Mott temperature for neutral pions under strong magnetic fields, where Landau quantization generates multiple quark–antiquark continua and invalidates the conventional threshold-crossing criterion (m_π⁰ = 2M).

Method: Uses the two-flavor Nambu–Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model to compute the neutral pion propagator and pole structure in hot, magnetized quark matter; defines the magnetic Mott temperature via the inflection point of the pion wave-function renormalization factor Z_π⁰(T,eB) associated with the lowest real pole.

Result: The proposed inflection-point criterion reproduces the conventional Mott temperature at eB → 0 and tracks the chiral pseudocritical temperature for both constant and magnetic-field-dependent couplings; shows that pion dissociation evolves smoothly as a spectral crossover under increasing magnetic field.

Conclusion: Pion dissociation in strong magnetic fields is not governed by a sharp threshold but manifests as a continuous spectral crossover; the inflection point of Z_π⁰(T,eB) provides a robust, physically motivated definition of the magnetic Mott temperature.

Abstract: We investigate the dissociation of the neutral pion in hot magnetized quark matter within the two-flavor Nambu--Jona-Lasinio model. At zero magnetic field, the Mott temperature is conventionally determined by $m_{π^0}(T_{\rm Mott})=2M(T_{\rm Mott})$, above which a real pole ceases to exist. At finite magnetic field, Landau quantization replaces this single threshold by a hierarchy of quark--antiquark continua and generates multiple solutions of the pion pole equation, rendering a direct threshold-crossing criterion ambiguous since a real pion solution below the lowest nominal threshold always exists. We therefore propose to define the magnetic Mott temperature through the inflection point of the pion wave-function renormalization factor $Z_{π^0}(T,eB)$ of that lowest pole, corresponding to the fastest loss of its spectral function strength. The prescription reproduces the conventional Mott temperature as $eB\to 0$ and tracks the chiral pseudocritical temperature for both constant and magnetic-field-dependent couplings. Our results characterize pion dissociation in a magnetic field as a spectral crossover rather than a simple threshold crossing.

</details>


### [77] [X(2370) as the pseudoscalar glueball: Another clue from a constituent approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29775)
*F. Buisseret*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a phenomenological model linking hadron decay widths linearly to mass via flux-tube energy, successfully explaining the X(2370) as a pseudoscalar glueball candidate and identifying f₂(2150) and f₂(2300) as tensor glueball candidates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a phenomenological framework that explains the observed masses and decay widths of glueball candidates—especially X(2370)—by extending constituent gluon models grounded in quenched lattice QCD, and to test glueball identification across scalar and tensor channels.

Method: Assumes total decay width ∝ energy stored in adjoint flux tube; constructs a linear mass–width relation; fits model first on light unflavoured mesons, then extrapolates to glueballs using constituent two-gluon bound-state picture with adjoint string.

Result: X(2370) mass and width are consistent with predictions; f₂(2150) and f₂(2300) emerge as viable tensor glueball candidates; model reproduces quenched lattice QCD glueball spectrum trends.

Conclusion: The flux-tube–based linear mass–width model supports X(2370) as the strongest pseudoscalar glueball candidate and provides a robust, predictive tool for glueball identification in other J^PC channels.

Abstract: The pseudoscalar state X(2370) is the clearest glueball candidate experimentally identified so far. From a phenomenological point of view, constituent approaches, in which the lightest glueballs are modelled as bound states of two transverse constituent gluons linked by an adjoint flux tube, reach a good agreement with quenched lattice QCD pure gauge spectrum. Based on the assumption that the total decay width of glueballs or light unflavoured mesons is proportional to the energy stored in the flux tube, a model is developed that linearly correlates the hadron decay width to its mass. The model is first fitted on light unflavoured mesons and then extended to glueballs. Both the mass and decay width of the X(2370) are compatible with the results of the constituent approach. Other experimental glueball candidates in the scalar and tensor channels are also reviewed to show the ability of the developed approach to identify glueballs: The f$_2$(2150) and f$_2$(2300) states appear as promising tensor glueball candidates.

</details>


### [78] [The inclusive $\bar B \to X_s γ$ decay rate with higher precision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29863)
*M. Misiak,H. M. Asatrian,K. Brune,M. Czaja,M. Czakon,M. Fael,C. Greub,T. Huber,F. Lange,L. T. Moos,M. Niggetiedt,A. Rehman,K. Schönwald,M. Steinhauser*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents an improved theoretical calculation of the inclusive B→X_sγ decay in the Standard Model, incorporating complete O(α_s) corrections and exact charm-mass dependence at O(α_s²), while using up-to-date non-perturbative parameters from semileptonic fits. It predicts BR(B→X_sγ) = (3.54 ± 0.14)×10⁻⁴ (E_γ > 1.6 GeV), consistent with current experimental average, and sets a 95% CL lower bound M_H± > 670 GeV in Type-II 2HDM.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reduce theoretical uncertainties—especially from charm-quark mass dependence at O(α_s²) and outdated non-perturbative inputs—in the SM prediction of the inclusive B → X_s γ branching ratio, thereby tightening constraints on beyond-SM physics.

Method: Perturbative QCD calculation including full O(α_s) corrections and exact m_c dependence at O(α_s²); non-perturbative contributions parameterized via b-quark kinetic-scheme mass and HQE matrix elements, taken from recent O(α_s³) semileptonic global fits; CP- and isospin-averaged branching ratio computed with E_γ > 1.6 GeV cut.
