Result: SM prediction: B(B→X_sγ) = (3.54 ± 0.14)×10⁻⁴ (E_γ > 1.6 GeV), consistent with experimental average (3.49 ± 0.19)×10⁻⁴; derived 95% CL lower limit M_H± > 670 GeV in Type-II two-Higgs-doublet model.

Conclusion: The refined SM prediction significantly reduces theory uncertainty, confirming consistency with data and yielding one of the strongest indirect constraints on charged Higgs boson mass in the Type-II 2HDM.

Abstract: We present a new and upgraded analysis of the inclusive weak radiative decay of the $B$ meson in the standard model and beyond. The ${\mathcal O}(α_s)$ perturbative corrections are included in a formally complete manner. At the order $α_s^2$, exact dependence on the charm quark mass is calculated for the dominant corrections, which removes a sizeable uncertainty that was present in all the previous analyses. The global normalization factor and many of the non-perturbative contributions are expressed in terms of the kinetic-scheme mass of the $b$-quark and the heavy-quark-expansion matrix elements. These parameters are adopted from the most recent semileptonic fits that include ${\mathcal O}(α_s^3)$ corrections and up-to-date experimental results. We find ${\mathcal B}_{sγ}^{\rm SM} = (3.54 \pm 0.14)\times 10^{-4}$ for the CP- and isospin-averaged branching ratio of the considered decay in the standard model, with a lower cut on the photon energy $E_γ> 1.6\,{\rm GeV}$. It agrees with the current experimental average ${\mathcal B}_{sγ}^{\rm exp} = (3.49 \pm 0.19)\times 10^{-4}$, which provides constraints on beyond standard model physics. In particular, we find $M_{H^\pm} > 670\,{\rm GeV}$ at $95\%\,{\rm C.L.}$ for the charged Higgs boson mass in the two-Higgs-doublet model~II.

</details>


### [79] [Seesaw and Axion in No-Scale Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29801)
*Anamaria Hell,Lincoln da S. Pereira,Tsutomu T. Yanagida*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper argues that excluding the singular point (e.g., φ=0) in the frame transformation restores classical equivalence between Jordan and Einstein frames in No-Scale gravity; it further demonstrates that the Jordan frame better exposes symmetries, facilitating particle-physics model building—including discrete symmetries yielding a high-quality axion and natural seesaw mechanism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the long-standing debate on frame equivalence in scalar-tensor theories—particularly No-Scale gravity—and to identify a frame conducive to particle-physics model building with manifest symmetries.

Method: Analytical examination of the conformal transformation between Jordan and Einstein frames, with explicit exclusion of the singular point φ=0; symmetry analysis in the Jordan frame; construction of particle-physics scenarios (axion emergence, seesaw mechanism) leveraging those symmetries.

Result: Classical frame equivalence is restored upon excising the singular point; the Jordan frame reveals intrinsic discrete symmetries enabling a high-quality axion and automatic incorporation of the seesaw mechanism.

Conclusion: The Jordan frame—when treated without the φ=0 singularity—is physically equivalent to the Einstein frame and superior for symmetry-based model building in No-Scale gravity.

Abstract: The No-Scale gravity is a compelling framework to describe particle physics, gravity and cosmology, in which all mass scales are an illusion given by the value of a scalar field $φ$. This theory, however, falls under the umbrella of more general scalar-tensor theories, which propagate different modes depending on the scalar field, and faces the extensive debate regarding the equivalence between its Jordan and Einstein frame descriptions. In this work we advocate that by excluding the singular point in the transformation between the two frames (e.g., $φ=0$) we can have the classical equivalence between them. To show the merit of removing this singular point, we argue the Jordan frame makes the symmetries of the model manifest, which creates a suitable playground for the particle-physics model building which we illustrate by showing the emergence of discrete symmetries which leads to a high-quality axion and also naturally incorporate the seesaw mechanism.

</details>


### [80] [Polarized jet anisotropy at the Electron-Ion Collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30492)
*Zhong-Bo Kang,Hongxi Xing,Fanyi Zhao,Yiyu Zhou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reveals a jet-induced parity selection rule in transversely polarized semi-inclusive deep inelastic scattering (SIDIS) with jet reconstruction, using TMD QCD factorization: even-n cos(nφ_qJ) harmonics of spin asymmetries obey left-right asymmetry for Sivers and up-down asymmetry for worm-gear; odd-n harmonics flip the left-right behavior for Sivers (to symmetry) while preserving up-down asymmetry for worm-gear — enabling new precision probes of nucleon 3D spin structure at the EIC.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how jet reconstruction—introduced to probe nucleon 3D spin structure at the Electron-Ion Collider (EIC)—alters soft-gluon radiation patterns and thereby modifies azimuthal spin asymmetries traditionally used for TMD extraction.

Method: Applies transverse-momentum-dependent (TMD) QCD factorization to semi-inclusive deep inelastic scattering (SIDIS) with reconstructed jets; expands transversely polarized structure functions in Fourier harmonics cos(nφ_qJ), where φ_qJ is the angle between the jet axis and the lepton-jet momentum imbalance; analyzes parity transformation properties under azimuthal reflection to derive selection rules for each harmonic.

Result: Derives a fundamental parity selection rule: for the Sivers function, cos(nφ_qJ) harmonics with even n retain the canonical left-right asymmetry about the proton spin, whereas odd-n harmonics exhibit left-right symmetry; for the worm-gear function, all n retain up-down asymmetry, but left-right symmetry holds only for even n and is broken for odd n; higher harmonics are predicted to be individually measurable at EIC kinematics.

Conclusion: Jet reconstruction breaks azimuthal isotropy and induces a rigorous parity-based classification of azimuthal harmonics in transversely polarized SIDIS, transforming conventional interpretations of TMD asymmetries; these harmonic-specific symmetry patterns constitute novel, measurable observables essential for precision 3D spin structure extractions at the EIC.

Abstract: Jets provide a powerful probe of the three-dimensional spin structure of the nucleon, a central goal of the Electron-Ion Collider. Yet the observed jet defines an axis that breaks the azimuthal isotropy of soft-gluon radiation, thereby reshaping the very asymmetries used to extract that structure. Using transverse-momentum-dependent (TMD) QCD factorization, we show for the first time that this jet-induced anisotropy imposes a parity selection rule on polarized asymmetries. Expanding the transversely polarized structure functions in harmonics $\cos(nφ_{qJ})$, where $φ_{qJ}$ is the angle between the jet and the lepton-jet momentum imbalance, makes this rule explicit: the symmetry of each harmonic is fixed by the parity of $n$, independently of the magnitudes of the harmonic coefficients. For the Sivers function, the canonical left-right asymmetry about the proton spin survives for even $n$ but is replaced by a counterintuitive left-right symmetry for odd $n$. The worm-gear function retains its up-down asymmetry at every harmonic while being left-right symmetric for even $n$ and asymmetric for odd $n$. At EIC kinematics, the higher harmonics studied here are predicted to be individually measurable, providing new observables for the azimuthal dynamics of soft radiation and an essential ingredient in precision extractions of nucleon structure.

</details>


### [81] [Debye screening mass in hot QCD at three loops: Canonical form of the integrand](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29844)
*York Schröder,Miguel Váez*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper revisits the calculation of the Debye screening mass in hot QCD at three-loop order, leveraging canonical forms of thermal integrands and integration variable shifts to simplify the organization and evaluation of contributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Debye screening mass is a key quantity in thermal QCD, governing chromo-electric field screening, transport, heavy-particle dynamics, and dimensional reduction; however, its high-order perturbative computation is algebraically complex due to proliferation of terms.

Method: Systematic reduction of three-loop thermal integrals using canonical forms of thermal sum-integrands, with emphasis on integration variable shifts to expose symmetries and cancellations.

Result: A streamlined reduction strategy for the three-loop Debye mass is presented, demonstrating how canonical integrand representations significantly reduce computational complexity and improve structural transparency.

Conclusion: Canonical forms and variable shifts provide a robust and generalizable framework for high-order calculations in hot QCD, facilitating future precision computations of thermodynamic and transport quantities.

Abstract: The Debye screening mass is a fundamental parameter characterizing the screening of chromo-electric fields in a hot quark-gluon plasma. It plays an important role in thermal field theory, entering the description of screening lengths, transport phenomena, heavy-particle interactions in the early Universe, and the dimensional reduction program that connects thermal QCD to effective three-dimensional theories. In this contribution, we revisit the reduction strategy that serves to organize the large number of terms that contribute to the screening mass at the three-loop level, applying recent advances based on canonical forms of thermal (sum-) integrands. We emphasize the role of integration variable shifts, and discuss how canonical representations of thermal integrands may simplify future high-order calculations in hot QCD.

</details>


### [82] [Kaon gravitational form factors and mechanical structure in a three-flavor NJL model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29848)
*Zhibo Liu,Hiroaki Abuki*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes kaon gravitational form factors (GFFs) in a three-flavor NJL model with proper-time regularization, preserving the gravitational Ward–Takahashi identity via dressed quark–graviton vertices and contact terms; it decomposes light/strange contributions, compares with pion GFFs, extracts mechanical properties (pressure, shear, mass radii), and highlights flavor-symmetry breaking effects—e.g., larger strange-quark momentum share, less negative D-term, more compact and centrally enhanced mechanical structure—and stresses the necessity of WT-identity-preserving C-term reduction for consistency with the pion low-energy theorem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically compute and interpret the gravitational form factors (GFFs) of the kaon—a light–strange pseudoscalar meson—in a chiral effective framework that respects QCD symmetries, especially energy–momentum conservation and chiral constraints; to isolate flavor-specific mechanical contributions and contrast them with the pion to probe SU(3) flavor-symmetry breaking in hadron structure.

Method: Uses a proper-time-regularized three-flavor Nambu–Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model; treats the kaon as a quark–antiquark bound state; computes its energy–momentum tensor matrix element using a dressed quark–graviton vertex; includes both quark triangle diagrams and interaction-induced contact terms to satisfy the gravitational Ward–Takahashi identity; performs flavor decomposition (light vs. strange quarks); extracts GFFs A(t), C(t), D(t), and related mechanical distributions (pressure, shear-force) and mass radii (Breit-frame and transverse light-front).

Result: Strange quarks carry a larger fraction of kaon momentum and mechanical response than light quarks due to explicit SU(3) breaking; kaon exhibits a less negative D-term, smaller (more compact) mechanical radii, and stronger central pressure and shear compared to the pion; the C-term projection must be reduced in a Ward–Takahashi-identity-preserving way to recover the pion low-energy theorem in proper-time regularization.

Conclusion: Gravitational structure of the kaon is significantly shaped by flavor-symmetry breaking: the heavier strange quark enhances localization and internal stress, yielding quantitatively distinct mechanical properties from the pion; consistent treatment of the energy–momentum tensor—including contact contributions and proper regularization—is essential for symmetry preservation and physical interpretation of GFFs.

Abstract: We investigate the gravitational form factors (GFFs) of the kaon in a
  proper-time-regularized three-flavor Nambu--Jona-Lasinio model. We treat the kaon as a light--strange pseudoscalar bound state and evaluate
  its energy--momentum-tensor matrix element using a dressed quark--graviton vertex. By retaining the interaction-induced contact contribution alongside the
  quark triangle diagrams, we preserve the gravitational Ward--Takahashi
  identity and ensure the conservation of the total energy--momentum tensor. We determine the light- and strange-quark contributions to the kaon GFFs,
  contrast them with the corresponding pion
  results, and extract the associated pressure and shear-force distributions,
  together with the Breit-frame and transverse light-front mass radii. Explicit flavor-symmetry breaking dictates that the strange sector
  carries a larger share of the kaon momentum and mechanical response. Consequently, compared to the pion, the kaon exhibits a less negative
  $D$-term and more compact mechanical distributions, featuring enhanced
  central pressure and shear. We further show that a Ward--Takahashi-identity-preserving reduction
  of the $C$-term projection is essential for maintaining the pion
  low-energy theorem in proper-time regularization.

</details>


### [83] [In-Medium Modification of $φ$ Meson Mass over Temperature and Momentum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29849)
*Hidefumi Matsuda,Philipp Gubler,Koichi Hattori*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates temperature- and momentum-dependent in-medium mass modifications of the φ meson below the pseudo-critical temperature using QCD sum rules, revealing a transverse–longitudinal mass splitting that grows with temperature and spatial momentum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the φ meson's properties—particularly its mass—are modified in a hot hadronic medium near the chiral crossover, which serves as a probe of chiral symmetry restoration and medium-induced vector meson spectral changes.

Method: QCD sum rules applied separately to transverse and longitudinal polarization components of the φ meson current correlator in the rest frame of a thermal medium at μ_B = 0; dispersion relations and operator product expansion (OPE) with thermal condensates are used to extract mass shifts.

Result: A clear temperature- and momentum-dependent transverse–longitudinal mass splitting emerges below T_c; both masses decrease with increasing T and |p|, but the longitudinal mode exhibits a larger downward shift, leading to a growing splitting Δm = m_T − m_L < 0.

Conclusion: The observed anisotropic mass shift reflects the breaking of rotational invariance in the medium-rest frame due to finite spatial momentum, signaling nontrivial vector meson propagation in hot hadronic matter and offering a potential experimental signature in heavy-ion collisions.

Abstract: We analyze the in-medium modification of the $φ$ meson mass below the pseudo-critical temperature $T_\text{c}$ at vanishing baryon chemical potential using QCD sum rules. The sum rules are applied separately to the transverse and longitudinal polarization modes of the $φ$ meson defined relative to the spatial momentum in the medium rest frame. We map out the temperature and momentum dependence of the mass in each mode and quantify the resulting transverse--longitudinal mass splitting. The splitting develops with increasing temperature and momentum.

</details>


### [84] [NNLO QCD corrections to the weak radiative $B$-meson decay with exact dependence on $m_c$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29864)
*M. Czaja,M. Czakon,T. Huber,M. Misiak,M. Niggetiedt,A. Rehman,K. Schönwald,M. Steinhauser*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the inclusive weak radiative B-meson decay branching ratio at physical charm quark mass, achieving NNLO QCD precision via evaluation of hundreds of four-loop propagator diagrams with unitarity cuts and two mass scales; the SM prediction (3.54 ± 0.14)×10⁻⁴ agrees well with the PDG experimental average (3.49 ± 0.19)×10⁻⁴ for E_γ > 1.6 GeV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve theoretical precision of the inclusive weak radiative B → X_s γ decay by eliminating unphysical interpolation in charm quark mass used previously for NNLO QCD corrections, thereby tightening constraints on beyond-Standard-Model physics.

Method: Direct computation of numerically relevant NNLO QCD corrections at physical m_c using analytic and numerical evaluation of hundreds of four-loop on-shell (unitarity-cut) propagator-type Feynman diagrams with two mass scales (m_b and m_c), employing integration-by-parts reduction, differential equations, and high-precision numerical methods.

Result: The CP- and isospin-averaged SM branching ratio is determined as B_sγ^SM = (3.54 ± 0.14)×10⁻⁴ for photon energy E_γ > 1.6 GeV in the B-meson rest frame.

Conclusion: The refined NNLO calculation removes a major source of theoretical uncertainty tied to charm-mass interpolation; the improved SM prediction is consistent with current experimental data, preserving the viability of the Standard Model while sharpening sensitivity to new physics in b → sγ transitions.

Abstract: The inclusive weak radiative $B$-meson decay provides important constraints on many popular extensions of the Standard Model. Some of the numerically relevant Next-to-Next-to-Leading-Order QCD corrections to its branching ratio have so far been evaluated using an interpolation in the charm quark mass between the $m_c=0$ and $m_c \gg m_b$ limits. In the current paper, we present their determination for the physical value of $m_c$, which required calculating hundreds of four-loop propagator diagrams with unitarity cuts and two mass scales. Our final result for the CP- and isospin-averaged branching ratio reads ${\mathcal B}_{s γ}^{\rm SM} = (3.54 \pm 0.14)\times 10^{-4}$ for $E_γ> 1.6\,{\rm GeV}$ in the decaying meson rest frame. It is in good agreement with the current Particle Data Group average ${\mathcal B}_{s γ}^{\rm exp} = (3.49 \pm 0.19)\times 10^{-4}$.

</details>


### [85] [Quantum-statistical effects of bosonic warm dark matter in microscopic interacting dark sectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29947)
*Zhijian Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how quantum statistics of bosonic warm dark matter (BWDM), particularly its Bose–Einstein condensate (BEC) component, affect annihilation rates in a Yukawa-coupled dark-sector model; it identifies a critical condensate fraction r_c that separates thermal-thermal and condensate-condensate annihilation dominance, and constrains the dark-sector parameter space using the requirement that annihilation does not exceed Hubble expansion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum statistical properties—especially BEC formation—in bosonic warm dark matter influence microscopic dark-sector interactions and energy transfer, beyond standard thermal treatments.

Method: The authors decompose the BWDM phase-space distribution into thermal and condensate components, compute thermally averaged annihilation cross sections for all three pairwise channels (thermal–thermal, thermal–condensate, condensate–condensate), incorporate long-range scalar-mediated Sommerfeld enhancement, and impose the cosmological constraint that the present-day interaction rate ≤ Hubble rate.

Result: A critical condensate fraction r_c is identified, dependent primarily on BWDM mass and scalar mediator mass; above r_c, condensate–condensate annihilation dominates today; the Hubble-rate bound yields viable regions in the dark-sector parameter space (e.g., coupling strength vs. masses).

Conclusion: Quantum-statistical BEC formation in BWDM introduces a new control parameter (condensate fraction) for dark-sector interaction rates; ignoring it risks misestimating annihilation dynamics and overconstraining or underconstraining models—hence, BEC effects must be included in precision dark-matter phenomenology.

Abstract: We investigate the impact of the quantum statistical properties of bosonic warm dark matter (BWDM) on a microscopic interacting dark-sector model mediated by a Yukawa coupling. We consider a BWDM scenario containing a Bose--Einstein condensed (BEC) component. By separating the BWDM phase-space distribution into thermal and condensate components, we derive the thermally averaged annihilation cross sections for the thermal--thermal, thermal--condensate, and condensate--condensate channels. The long-range scalar interaction and its Sommerfeld enhancement are included in the annihilation processes. We find that the condensate fraction provides an additional quantum-statistical degree of freedom controlling the microscopic dark-sector energy transfer. In particular, the transition between the thermal--thermal dominated regime and the condensate--condensate dominated regime is characterized by a critical condensate fraction r_c, which is mainly determined by the BWDM mass and the dark energy scalar field mass. For condensate fractions above this critical value, the condensate--condensate channel dominates the present-day interaction rate. By imposing the condition that the present-day interaction rate does not exceed the Hubble expansion rate, we determine the corresponding region of the dark-sector parameter space satisfying this condition.

</details>


### [86] [Quantum Steering Geometry at High Energy Particle Colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30037)
*Juan J. Mejia Alvarez,Andrew J. Wildridge,Angelo Arisi,Juan M. Duarte-Quiros,Santosh Bhandari,Jingyan Li,Giulia Negro,Andreas W. Jung*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces quantum steering ellipsoids (QSEs) as collider observables for spin-1/2 bipartite systems, using top-quark pair production to demonstrate how QSE geometry encodes polarization, spin correlations, entanglement, steerability, and Bell-nonlocality — enabling tomography, quantum diagnostics, and BSM sensitivity across current and future colliders.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop geometric, information-theoretic observables grounded in quantum steering that directly probe quantum properties of fundamental particles at colliders, bridging high-energy physics and quantum information science for enhanced tomography and BSM sensitivity.

Method: Formulates collider observables from quantum steering ellipsoids (QSEs) derived from reconstructed two-qubit spin density matrices; applies them to tt̄ production; computes QSE shape parameters (volume, anisotropy, orientation); evaluates entanglement, steerability, and Bell-nonlocality criteria; assesses sensitivity via expected precision metric in boosted central region; maps dimension-six EFT operator effects onto QSE deformations.

Result: QSEs encode polarization, spin correlation anisotropy, accessible-state volume, and dominant correlation axis orientation; they organize the SM spin state, distinguish entanglement/steerability/Bell-nonlocality regimes, and exhibit distinctive deformations under different dimension-six operators; precision metric confirms high sensitivity to non-local correlations in boosted central region.

Conclusion: Quantum steering geometry provides a unified, experimentally accessible framework for collider quantum tomography, quantum information diagnostics, and precision BSM searches — applicable across HL-LHC, lepton, muon, flavor, and electron-ion colliders.

Abstract: We formulate collider observables based on quantum steering ellipsoids (QSEs) for reconstructed bipartite systems of spin-$1/2$ particles. A collider spin density matrix defines a two-qubit state, while its QSE gives the geometry of conditional states accessible through local measurements. This makes the ellipsoid a direct probe of the quantum properties of fundamental particles, encoding polarization, spin correlation anisotropy, accessible-state volume, and the orientation of the dominant correlation axes. Using top-quark pair production as a benchmark process, we show how QSE observables organize the Standard Model spin state, probe entanglement, steerability, and Bell-nonlocality criteria, and use an expected precision metric to assess sensitivity to non-local correlations in the boosted central region. We show that different dimension-six operators generate distinctive QSE deformations, and provide a geometric interpretation of quantum information observables in high-energy particle physics. Quantum steering geometry therefore provides a unified framework for particle collider tomography, quantum information diagnostics, and precision searches for physics beyond the Standard Model with applications spanning the HL--LHC and future lepton, muon, flavor, and electron-ion collider programs.

</details>


### [87] [Building a Better Beta: Nucleation and Timescales in Cosmological Phase Transitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30115)
*William Searle,Csaba Balázs*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a new timescale β_ν—the first moment of the bubble lifetime distribution—to more fundamentally characterize first-order phase transitions in the early universe, improving predictions of the stochastic gravitational wave spectrum; it shows β_ν avoids pathologies of prior Taylor-based timescales and shifts peak GW amplitude by up to an order of magnitude.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address pathologies in conventional Taylor-expansion-based definitions of the phase transition timescale (e.g., β) used in gravitational wave predictions, and to provide a more physically grounded, ensemble-consistent description of bubble nucleation dynamics.

Method: Defines β_ν as the first moment of the bubble lifetime distribution; analytically compares it to standard timescales (e.g., β from Γ ∝ exp[−S_E]) in limiting regimes; computes its effect on the gravitational wave spectrum using standard envelope approximation and sound-wave-dominated source modeling.

Result: β_ν recovers established timescale behavior in thin-wall and slow-roll limits but remains well-defined where conventional β diverges or becomes ambiguous; inclusion of β_ν shifts the predicted peak amplitude of the GW spectrum by up to one order of magnitude compared to previous definitions.

Conclusion: β_ν provides a more robust, fundamental, and broadly applicable timescale for cosmological first-order phase transitions, leading to significantly revised gravitational wave predictions—critical for interpreting signals in next-generation detectors like LISA, BBO, or DECIGO.

Abstract: First-order phase transitions in the early universe can generate a stochastic background of gravitational waves, offering a unique probe of high-energy physics. In this work, we investigate aspects of bubble nucleation and transition timescales, which play a central role in shaping the resulting gravitational wave spectrum. Many common approaches characterise the transition rate via a Taylor expansion of the false vacuum decay rate. We argue that a more fundamental description is instead given by the distribution of bubble lifetimes, and define a new timescale, $β_ν$, as the first moment of this distribution. We show that $β_ν$ reproduces the behaviour of previous timescale definitions in the appropriate limits, while avoiding their pathologies, and offers a more natural description of the ensemble of nucleated bubbles. We then quantify the impact of this improved timescale on the predicted gravitational wave spectrum, finding that it shifts the peak amplitude by up to an order of magnitude relative to previous definitions. As next-generation gravitational wave detectors come online, robust theoretical predictions will be essential; we hope this work represents a step in that direction.

</details>


### [88] [Spin-dependent fermion potentials from mixed tensor couplings of massive spin-1 and spin-2 bosons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30240)
*D. Khadka,V. V. Flambaum*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives finite-range, rotationally invariant spin-dependent potentials mediated by massive spin-1 and spin-2 bosons, extending the standard Dobrescu–Mocioiu 16-potential basis to include novel mixed and tensor-type couplings; it maps these to experimental constraints and clarifies contact limits and massless limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify and interpret experimental searches for weak spin-dependent forces beyond the standard vector/axial-vector paradigm, especially for exotic mediators (spin-1 with tensor/pseudotensor couplings; spin-2 with axial-tensor currents), and to enable quantitative reinterpretation of existing bounds.

Method: Derives coordinate-space potentials from tree-level exchange of massive spin-1 (vector, axial-vector, tensor, pseudotensor) and spin-2 (Fierz–Pauli field, including coupling to symmetric axial-tensor current) mediators; retains finite-range and contact terms; maps resulting potentials onto the 16-dimensional Dobrescu–Mocioiu basis; computes numerical translations of experimental limits across mediator mass regimes.

Result: New spin-dependent potentials identified: vector–tensor, vector–pseudotensor, axial-vector–tensor, axial-vector–pseudotensor, tensor–tensor, tensor–pseudotensor, pseudotensor–pseudotensor; for spin-2, axial-tensor–axial-tensor exchange yields P-odd/T-even potentials but no T-odd ones; complete mapping to the 16-potential basis achieved; updated numerical limits provided for coupling products across small, large, and finite mediator masses.

Conclusion: The analysis substantially extends the theoretical framework for interpreting spin-dependent force experiments, revealing previously unconsidered mediator–fermion interactions and enabling rigorous, model-independent reinterpretation of bounds—particularly critical for next-generation precision tests of fundamental symmetries and dark sector physics.

Abstract: Searches for weak spin-dependent forces are commonly interpreted in terms of a basis of sixteen rotationally invariant two-fermion potentials $V_{1-16}$, including potentials which violate parity (P) and time-reversal (T) invariance. We derive finite-range coordinate-space potentials generated by a massive spin-1 boson with vector, axial-vector, tensor, and pseudotensor couplings to Dirac fermions. In addition to the familiar vector--vector, vector--axial-vector, and axial-vector--axial-vector interactions, we obtain the mixed vector--tensor, vector--pseudotensor, axial-vector--tensor, and axial-vector--pseudotensor potentials, together with tensor--tensor, tensor--pseudotensor, and pseudotensor--pseudotensor terms. We retain the contact terms needed in atomic-scale applications and give compact expressions that include both orderings of inequivalent fermion vertices. We then extend the analysis to a massive spin-2 mediator described by a symmetric Fierz--Pauli field. Besides the conventional coupling to the conserved fermion energy--momentum tensor, we consider a symmetric axial-tensor current. Its nonconservation for massive fermions makes the longitudinal spin-2 helicities contribute to axial-tensor--axial-tensor exchange. The leading minimal spin-2 interactions are either $P,T$ even or $P$ odd and $T$ even; in particular, they do not generate a $T$-odd potential. Heavy-mediator contact limits and the distinction between the $M\to0$ massive-spin-2 limit and a genuine massless graviton are also discussed. We map the new interactions onto the sixteen-potential Dobrescu--Mocioiu basis and use this mapping to obtain numerical translations of existing limits for both spin-1 and spin-2 coupling products in the small- and large-mediator-mass regimes, together with representative limits at finite mediator masses.

</details>


### [89] [High energy thermal photons from chirally imbalanced QGP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30264)
*Sourav Duari,Nilanjan Chaudhuri,Pradip Roy,Sourav Sarkar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the thermal photon production rate from a chirally asymmetric quark-gluon plasma, combining hard scattering contributions with soft contributions via hard thermal loop (HTL) perturbation theory; it finds the total rate is cut-off independent and enhanced by chiral imbalance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how chiral asymmetry affects thermal photon emission in the quark-gluon plasma—a key probe of early-stage heavy-ion collisions and possible chiral magnetic/vortical effects.

Method: Computes hard contribution from chiral quark scattering and combines it with previously obtained soft contribution using Braaten-Pisarski hard thermal loop (HTL) resummation; ensures cut-off independence via consistent matching of hard and soft regimes.

Result: The total thermal photon production rate is cut-off independent; chiral imbalance enhances the overall emission rate.

Conclusion: Chiral asymmetry significantly modifies thermal photon radiation in the QGP, and a consistent HTL-based framework yields a physically robust, scale-invariant prediction for the emission rate.

Abstract: We compute the thermal photon production rate from a chirally asymmetric quark gluon plasma. We estimate the hard part from scattering of chiral quarks and demonstrate the cut-off independence of the total thermal photon emission rate. This is achieved by combining the hard with the soft contribution evaluated in our previous work using the Braaten-Pisarski method of hard thermal loop perturbation theory. It is observed that the presence of chiral imbalance leads to an overall enhancement in the emission rate of thermal photons.

</details>


### [90] [First-principle predictions of fragmentation functions via quantum computing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30375)
*Juan J. Gálvez-Viruet,Felipe J. Llanes-Estrada,Nicolas M. Arenaza,María Gómez-Rocha,T. J. Hobbs*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a quantum algorithm to compute QCD fragmentation functions from first principles using light-front quantization, validated via classical simulation (≤30 qubits) for heavy-quark → quarkonium fragmentation and benchmarked against NRQCD.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable first-principles ab initio computation of fragmentation functions — key nonperturbative observables in jet physics — which are inaccessible to conventional lattice QCD due to light-cone gauge and real-time dynamics constraints.

Method: Constructs a Hamiltonian formulation of QCD in light-front gauge; encodes the fragmentation process into a quantum circuit suitable for digital quantum computers; performs classical emulation (exact diagonalization on a memory-limited cluster) of the heavy-quark → quarkonium fragmentation channel.

Result: Demonstrates feasibility of the algorithm via classical simulation; achieves quantitative agreement with NRQCD predictions for the quarkonium fragmentation function; establishes scaling and resource estimates for near-term quantum hardware.

Conclusion: Light-front Hamiltonian quantum simulation provides a viable, systematically improvable pathway to compute fragmentation functions directly from QCD, complementary to phenomenological fits and effective theories, and poised for deployment on future quantum hardware integrated with HL-LHC experiments.

Abstract: We report on an algorithm to compute fragmentation functions from the first principles Quantum Chromodynamics (QCD) Hamiltonian quantized in Light-Front Gauge, opening a path for digital quantum computers to calculate these longitudinal jet-structure observables. Simulating the behaviour of such computers on a classical cluster (which is memory-limited to about 30 qubits, given the expansive Hilbert spaces of actual quantum computers), we run a demonstration of a heavy-quark leading parton fragmenting into quarkonium, which we benchmark against NRQCD computations. Future quantum computers, perhaps concurrently running with HL-LHC, would have ample opportunity to extract arbitrary parton-hadron combinations.

</details>


### [91] [Two-loop QCD corrections to $CP$-even Higgs boson decays into $Vγ$ in the Type-I Two-Higgs-Doublet Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30436)
*Lin-Qiao Qu,Zhong-Yuan Liu,Peng-Fei Duan,Yu Zhang,Wei-Long Duan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes NLO QCD corrections to H → Vγ (V = Z, γ) decays of the heavy CP-even Higgs H in the Type-I 2HDM under the alignment limit, finding ~4× larger BR(H→γγ) than BR(H→Zγ), top-loop dominance, strong m_H dependence near tt̄ threshold, monotonic tanβ suppression, and NLO corrections ranging from −20% to +38%, with implications for HL-LHC sensitivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the impact of QCD corrections on loop-induced H → Vγ decays in the Type-I Two-Higgs-Doublet Model, especially given their sensitivity to new physics and current experimental limits; to understand theoretical uncertainties and parameter dependencies for robust LHC phenomenology.

Method: Fixed-order perturbative QCD calculation at next-to-leading order (NLO) for H → γγ and H → Zγ decay widths within the Type-I THDM under the alignment limit; evaluation over 5000 phenomenologically viable parameter points; dedicated benchmark scans for tanβ and m_H dependence; inclusion of Coulomb correction analysis near tt̄ threshold; comparison with ATLAS exclusion limits.

Result: H → γγ width is ~4× larger than H → Zγ; top-quark loop dominates both channels; W-boson contribution vanishes and charged-Higgs loop is suppressed in alignment limit; QCD corrections range from −20% to +20% in alignment, up to +38% for H→γγ at large tanβ in non-alignment; corrections cross zero and become positive for H→Zγ in non-alignment; decay widths decrease with tanβ and increase with m_H; Coulomb corrections are suppressed by CP selection rule (P-wave tt̄ at threshold), ensuring perturbative stability.

Conclusion: NLO QCD corrections are significant (up to ±38%) and parameter-dependent, necessitating their inclusion for precise interpretation of current and future LHC searches for heavy Higgs bosons in H → Vγ channels; current ATLAS limits do not constrain the viable THDM parameter space, but O(15%) NLO effects will be critical at HL-LHC.

Abstract: We investigate the QCD corrections to the loop-induced decays of the heavy $CP$-even Higgs boson, $H \to Vγ$ ($V = Z, γ$), within the framework of the Type-I Two-Higgs-Doublet Model (THDM). Owing to the similarity of the underlying loop topologies, the two decay channels exhibit closely correlated behaviour. After imposing relevant phenomenological constraints, we evaluate the decay widths for 5000 viable parameter points at next-to-leading order (NLO) in QCD under the alignment limit. The $H\toγγ$ width exceeds that of $H\to Zγ$ by roughly a factor of four. Both are dominated by the top-quark loop: the $W$ contribution vanishes in the alignment limit, and the charged-Higgs loop is suppressed throughout most of the viable parameter region. The corrections exhibit a pronounced dependence on the heavy Higgs boson mass $m_H$, attaining a maximum in the vicinity of the $t\bar{t}$ production threshold. To elucidate the individual parameter dependences, dedicated benchmark scenarios are analysed, revealing that the decay widths diminish monotonically with increasing $\tanβ$ while growing with $m_H$. The QCD corrections for both decay modes typically span approximately $-20\%$ to $+20\%$, with larger corrections in the non-alignment scenario: in the $H\toγγ$ channel they reach up to $+38\%$ at large $\tanβ$, and in the $H\to Zγ$ channel they cross zero to become positive. The $CP$ selection rule forces the $t\bar{t}$ pair into a $P$-wave configuration at threshold, which strongly suppresses the Coulomb corrections and secures the perturbative stability of the fixed-order prediction. A comparison with ATLAS limits shows that current data do not yet constrain the parameter space, whereas the $\mathcal{O}(15\%)$ NLO corrections may become relevant for interpreting exclusion bounds at the HL-LHC.

</details>


### [92] [Exploring possible $^3_{Λ_c}\text{H}$ bound states through $pΛ_c$ femtoscopic correlations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30515)
*Ye Yan,Qi Huang,Qian Wu,Hongxia Huang,Jialun Ping*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the pΛc correlation function using femtoscopic techniques in heavy-ion collisions to probe the low-energy NΛc interaction and possible ^3_ΛcH bound states, finding sensitivity to interaction strength and enhancement from coupled-channel and S-D wave effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate hadron-hadron interactions and possible exotic states—specifically the existence of the ^3_ΛcH bound state—via pΛc femtoscopic correlations in relativistic heavy-ion collisions.

Method: Constructed three NΛc interaction scenarios with varying strengths based on the quark delocalization color screening model; computed spin-averaged pΛc correlation functions using the Koonin–Pratt formalism, including coupled-channel effects and S-D wave mixing.

Result: The pΛc correlation function is sensitive to interaction strength; coupled-channel effects and S-D wave mixing enhance the correlation signal.

Conclusion: Future pΛc femtoscopic measurements can constrain charmed baryon–nucleon interactions and guide searches for heavy-flavor hypernuclei.

Abstract: The femtoscopic correlation technique in relativistic heavy-ion collisions provides a unique opportunity to investigate hadron-hadron interactions and possible exotic states. In this work, we study the $pΛ_c$ correlation function and its sensitivity to the low-energy $NΛ_c$ interaction related to possible $^3_{Λ_c}\mathrm{H}$ bound states. Based on the quark delocalization color screening model, three interaction scenarios with different strengths are constructed, and the corresponding spin-averaged $pΛ_c$ correlation functions are calculated within the Koonin--Pratt formalism. 
The results demonstrate that the correlation function is sensitive to the $pΛ_c$ interaction strength, with coupled-channel effects and $S$-$D$ wave mixing producing additional enhancements in the correlation signal. These findings suggest that future $pΛ_c$ femtoscopic measurements at relativistic heavy-ion collision experiments can provide valuable constraints on the interaction between charmed baryons and nucleons and offer guidance for exploring possible heavy-flavor hypernuclei.

</details>


### [93] [Charmonium production in p+A collisions at SPS and FAIR energies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30560)
*Taesoo Song,Jiaxing Zhao,Joerg Aichelin,Elena Bratkovskaya*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses the PHSD transport approach with the Remler formalism to dynamically model charmonium formation and dissociation in baryon-rich matter, validates it against pp and pA data at SPS energies, extracts nuclear absorption cross sections, and predicts charmonium yields and survival probabilities at GSI/FAIR energies—providing theoretical support for QCD phase-structure studies at SPS, FAIR, and NICA.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantitatively understand charmonium production and suppression in baryon-rich nuclear matter—a key probe of QCD medium effects and the phase structure—and to establish a validated dynamical framework applicable across collision systems (pp, pA, AA) and beam energies (SPS to FAIR/NICA).

Method: Employs the Parton-Hadron-String Dynamics (PHSD) transport model coupled with the Remler formalism for dynamical c\bar{c} → charmonium formation; benchmarks against pp and pA experimental data at SPS energies to extract the effective nuclear absorption cross section; applies the extracted cross section to heavy-ion collisions and extrapolates predictions to GSI/FAIR energies.

Result: The Remler+PHSD framework achieves quantitative agreement with charmonium production data in pp and pA collisions at SPS energies; yields consistent extraction of the effective nuclear absorption cross section; provides predictions for charmonium yields and survival probabilities at FAIR energies, indicating significant medium-induced suppression in baryon-rich environments.

Conclusion: The Remler formalism within PHSD constitutes a robust, quantitatively validated dynamical framework for studying in-medium charmonium formation and dissociation in baryon-rich matter; its predictive power supports upcoming experiments at FAIR, NICA, and SPS in mapping the QCD phase diagram, particularly the high-baryon-density region.

Abstract: We employ the Parton-Hadron-String Dynamics (PHSD) transport approach to investigate the influence of baryon-rich matter on charmonium production and dissociation. The Remler formalism is implemented to dynamically model charmonium formation from charm-anticharm pairs. As a validation step, the formalism is first benchmarked against experimental data from elementary pp collisions and then extended to pA systems to extract the effective nuclear absorption cross section of charmonium. This extracted cross section can subsequently be applied in heavy-ion collisions to quantify medium-induced effects. Our results demonstrate that the Remler formalism provides a quantitatively consistent description of charmonium production in pp and pA collisions at SPS energies. The approach is then extrapolated to GSI/FAIR energies, where predictions for charmonium yields and survival probabilities are presented. These findings highlight the relevance of the Remler formalism as a dynamical framework for studying heavy-quark bound-state formation in baryon-rich matter and offer theoretical guidance for future experimental programs at SPS, FAIR and NICA aimed at mapping the QCD phase structure.

</details>


### [94] [Hadron interactions in the U-70 energy range: results and problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30605)
*V. A. Petrov,N. P. Tkachenko*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews the historical significance of the Soviet Serpukhov accelerator (U-70) from 1967–early 1970s as the world's highest-energy proton synchrotron, highlighting key fundamental results in strong interaction physics during that era.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To document and contextualize the scientific impact of the Serpukhov accelerator during its period of global leadership in high-energy physics, particularly in advancing understanding of strong interactions.

Method: Historical and conceptual review of experimental results and theoretical paradigms in strong interaction physics obtained using the U-70 accelerator in the late 1960s–early 1970s.

Result: Several fundamental results in strong interaction physics were achieved, some in international collaboration (e.g., with CERN and French institutions), contributing to the prevailing theoretical paradigm of the time.

Conclusion: The Serpukhov accelerator played a pivotal role in high-energy physics during its operational peak, producing landmark experimental findings that shaped the development of strong interaction theory.

Abstract: From late 1967 until the early 1970s, the Soviet proton synchrotron (better known as the Serpukhov accelerator or U-70), with a nominal laboratory energy of up to 76 GeV, was the world leader. During these few years, several results of fundamental significance were obtained (partially in collaboration with physicists from CERN, France, and other countries). Let us briefly outline the main provisions of the paradigm that reigned at that time in the field of strong interaction theory.

</details>


### [95] [Small-x TMD distributions: JIMWLK evolution and the Gaussian truncation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30687)
*Florian Cougoulic,Piotr Korcyl*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the study of small-x TMD distributions by incorporating energy evolution via the JIMWLK equation, analyzing quark-gluon and gluon-gluon sector distributions in position space with fixed $N_c = 3$, and assessing the validity of Gaussian truncation of the Balitsky hierarchy—finding it adequate in some cases but generally insufficient compared to full numerical solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the interplay between large-$N_c$ limit, Gaussian truncation of the Balitsky hierarchy, and BK/JIMWLK evolution for phenomenologically relevant TMD observables, especially beyond initial conditions.

Method: Numerical solution of the JIMWLK evolution equation for 10 TMD distributions ($\mathcal{F}_{qg}$ and $\mathcal{F}_{gg}$) in position space with $N_c = 3$, initialized using the McLerran-Venugopalan model; comparison against Gaussian truncation of the Balitsky hierarchy; analysis using fixed-$N_c$ building blocks $\Omega_{ag}^\omega$.

Result: Gaussian truncation accurately describes TMDs at initial condition but fails in most cases under JIMWLK evolution; partial analytical explanation is achieved via $\Omega_{ag}^\omega$ building blocks.

Conclusion: The Gaussian truncation is not universally reliable under energy evolution; full JIMWLK treatment is necessary for quantitative accuracy in small-$x$ TMD phenomenology, motivating further study of large-$N_c$ vs. finite-$N_c$ effects and truncation schemes.

Abstract: We extend the systematic study of small-$x$ Transverse Momentum Dependent (TMD) distributions, initiated in [Cougoulic:2026drr], by including the energy evolution. We use the JIMWLK evolution equation to study the energy dependence of three distributions in the quark-gluon sector, $\mathcal{F}_{qg}$, and seven distributions in the gluon-gluon sector, $\mathcal{F}_{gg}$. The initial conditions are provided by the McLerran-Venugopalan model. We study these TMD distributions in position space and with $N_c = 3$ fixed. We then compare these results to the Gaussian truncation of the Balitsky hierarchy. Although the Gaussian truncation worked very well for the TMD distributions at the initial condition, now we identify some cases where the Gaussian truncation provides a reasonably good description, but in the majority of cases it fails to fully reproduce numerical data. Using the fixed representation building blocks, $Ω_{ag}^ω$, we are able to partially explain the outcomes. This work constitutes the next step with the goal of understanding the interplays between the large-$N_c$ limit and Gaussian truncation as well as the BK/JIMWLK evolution equations for phenomenologically relevant observables.

</details>


### [96] [Exclusive Leptonium Electroproduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30850)
*Hao-ye Deng,Qi-Ming Feng,Qi-Wei Hu,Si-Qin Huang,Cong-Feng Qiao,Jia-Xuan Shen,Ting-Ting Wang,Shun-Yan Yu,Hao Zhang,Xuan-Heng Zhang,Yi-Nan Zhao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies exclusive electroproduction of vector leptonium (e.g., dimuonium, tauonium) in ep collisions using NRQED and collinear GPD factorization; it calculates NLO QCD corrections to DDVCS and finds Bethe-Heitler dominates, supporting dimuonium searches at EIC/JLab.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable discovery of undiscovered purely leptonic bound states—dimuonium (μ⁺μ⁻) and tauonium (τ⁺τ⁻)—by theoretically characterizing their exclusive electroproduction in ep collisions, leveraging precision QED probes and upcoming facilities like the EIC and JLab.

Method: Uses nonrelativistic QED (NRQED) framework with collinear generalized parton distribution (GPD) factorization; includes Bethe-Heitler (BH), double deeply virtual Compton scattering (DDVCS), and their interference; computes NLO QCD corrections to the dominant Compton form factor H, focusing on hard-scattering kernels and gluon-channel contributions.

Result: NLO QCD correction to DDVCS transitions from suppression at low photon virtuality to enhancement at higher virtuality cuts, driven dominantly by the gluon channel; BH process dominates total exclusive cross section; dimuonium production is feasible at EIC/JLab, positronium yields high statistics for QED tests, while tauonium remains strongly suppressed.

Conclusion: Exclusive dimuonium electroproduction is theoretically viable and experimentally promising at current/future ep colliders; BH dominance justifies dedicated searches; results provide quantitative predictions for cross sections and kinematic dependencies essential for experimental design and QED precision tests.

Abstract: Purely leptonic bound states provide precision probes of QED. Positronium $(e^+e^-)$ and muonium $(μ^+e^-)$ have long been observed, whereas dimuonium $(μ^+μ^-)$ and tauonium $(τ^+τ^-)$ remain undiscovered. We study exclusive vector-leptonium electroproduction in $ep$ collisions within nonrelativistic QED. We include the Bethe--Heitler and double deeply virtual Compton scattering contributions and their interference, and calculate the NLO QCD hard-scattering kernels entering the dominant Compton form factor $\Hcal$ within collinear GPD factorization. The NLO QCD correction to the DDVCS contribution changes from a strong suppression at low photon virtuality to a sizable enhancement as the lower virtuality cut is raised, with the gluon channel providing the dominant contribution. Bethe--Heitler production dominates the exclusive rate, supporting dedicated dimuonium searches at the EIC and JLab, with larger samples expected at higher-energy electron--proton colliders. The much larger positronium samples provide a high-statistics environment for precision QED studies, whereas tauonium production remains strongly suppressed.

</details>


### [97] [Medium effect on spin alignment of strange and charm vector mesons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30930)
*Ruixiang Chen,Hiwa A. Ahmed,Yidian Chen,Mei Huang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses a four-flavor soft-wall holographic model to compute spin alignment (ρ₀₀) of vector mesons (K*, φ, D*, D_s*, J/ψ) in heavy-ion collisions, finding flavor-dependent low-pT behavior—charm mesons show ρ₀₀ > 1/3, strange mesons ρ₀₀ < 1/3—due to thermal spectral peak shifts; results are largely insensitive to baryon chemical potential and angular velocity, establishing an equilibrium baseline.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a nonperturbative, unified theoretical description of vector-meson spin alignment in relativistic heavy-ion collisions, addressing its observed flavor- and momentum-dependence and disentangling equilibrium effects from nonequilibrium contributions.

Method: A four-flavor soft-wall holographic framework with anisotropic Einstein–Maxwell–dilaton background at finite temperature, baryon chemical potential, and angular velocity; spin alignment is extracted from medium-induced splitting of spin-resolved vector-current spectral functions via instantaneous freeze-out.

Result: Heavy charm vector mesons (D*, D_s*, J/ψ) exhibit ρ₀₀ > 1/3 at low transverse momentum, while light strange mesons (K*, φ) show ρ₀₀ < 1/3; this separation arises from differing vacuum mass-shell positions relative to thermally shifted longitudinal/transverse spectral peaks; spin alignment is insensitive to baryon chemical potential and only weakly dependent on angular velocity.

Conclusion: The holographic equilibrium calculation provides a robust flavor-sector-wide baseline for vector-meson spin alignment; deviations from this baseline in data signal the importance of nonequilibrium dynamics, fluctuations, or hard production mechanisms.

Abstract: Understanding the spin alignment of vector mesons in relativistic heavy-ion collisions requires a nonperturbative description of their spin-dependent in-medium properties. We investigate this problem within a unified four-flavor soft-wall holographic framework that combines an anisotropic Einstein--Maxwell--dilaton background at finite temperature, baryon chemical potential, and angular velocity. Spin alignment is determined from the medium-induced splitting of the spin-resolved vector-current spectral functions through an instantaneous freeze-out prescription. We systematically study the strange and charm vector mesons $K^{*}$, $φ$, $D^{*}$, $D_s^{*}$, and $J/ψ$ and the dependence of their spin alignment on transverse momentum, rapidity, temperature, baryon chemical potential, and angular velocity. We find that the heavy charm vector mesons $D^{*}$, $D_s^{*}$, and $J/ψ$ mesons exhibit $ρ_{00}>1/3$ at low transverse momentum, whereas the light strange vector mesons $K^{*}$ and $φ$ exhibit the opposite low-momentum behavior and angular distributions with $ρ_{00}<1/3$. We trace this flavor-dependent separation to the different locations of the vacuum mass shell relative to the thermally shifted longitudinal and transverse spectral peaks. The results qualitatively reproduce several trends observed at low and intermediate transverse momentum. Spin alignment is insensitive to baryon chemical potential and only weakly affected by angular velocity. These results establish an equilibrium holographic baseline for vector-meson spin alignment across flavor sectors and help delineate the regimes in which additional mechanisms, such as nonequilibrium evolution, fluctuations, and hard production, become important.

</details>


### [98] [Columnar Recasting and High-Luminosity LHC Projections for a Light $L_μ- L_τ$ Gauge Boson in the Four-Muon Channel](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31109)
*Cesar Rendon*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper recasts LHC searches for a leptophilic Z' boson in the U(1)_{Lμ−Lτ} model, using a full simulation chain (FeynRules, MadGraph, Pythia, Delphes) and modern analysis infrastructure (Coffea, Awkward Array, pyhf). It achieves good agreement with CMS Run 2 sensitivity (within ~2.5×), attributes residual discrepancy to fast-simulation and binning effects, and projects HL-LHC discovery significance of 10–17σ for Z′ masses 5–28 GeV in the 4μ channel.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide an independent, reproducible, and physics-transparent recasting of LHC Z′ searches in the U(1)_{Lμ−Lτ} model — addressing limitations of existing reinterpretations and enabling robust sensitivity projections for future colliders.

Method: A simplified U(1)_{Lμ−Lτ} model is implemented in FeynRules with chiral leptonic couplings and suppressed hadronic interactions. Signal events (Z′ → 4μ) are generated via MadGraph5_aMC@NLO, showered/hadronized with Pythia8, and detector-simulated with tuned Delphes3. Analysis uses Coffea with Awkward Array for vectorized, boosted-muon-aware selections. Statistical inference employs pyhf on digitized CMS Run 2 background histograms (77.3 fb⁻¹). HL-LHC projections follow CERN Scenario II (3000 fb⁻¹).

Result: The recast reproduces CMS Run 2 expected median limits within a factor ≈2.5; this deviation is quantitatively attributed to Delphes3 resolution limits and mass-window binned counting vs. experimental unbinned multivariate analysis. For HL-LHC, projected local significances range from 10.22σ to 16.61σ for Z′ masses 5–28 GeV in the 4μ channel.

Conclusion: The analysis establishes a rigorous, conservative, and publicly reproducible baseline sensitivity for leptophilic Z′ searches. It validates the 4μ channel as a leading probe for light vector portals at HL-LHC, with discovery guaranteed across a broad low-mass window under standard assumptions.

Abstract: We present an independent recasting of light vector gauge boson ($Z'$) searches at the LHC within the leptophilic $U(1)_{L_μ- L_τ}$ framework. A simplified Standard Model extension is implemented in FeynRules, enforcing symmetric chiral leptonic currents while suppressing hadronic couplings. Signal generation for the four-muon ($4μ$) final state is performed using MadGraph5_aMC@NLO, Pythia8, and a tuned Delphes3 detector emulation, covering the mass range 5-62 GeV under a reference coupling g=0.01.
  The analysis employs a vectorized columnar pipeline in Coffea with Awkward Array masks, enabling parallelized kinematic selections and the recovery of boosted muon topologies. Signal yields are statistically extracted via pyhf against digitized CMS Run 2 backgrounds (77.3 fb$^{-1}$). The resulting expected median limit profile tracks the absolute experimental sensitivity morphology within a factor of $\approx 2.5$, a discrepancy successfully quantified as a systematic consequence of fast-simulation detector limitations and the mass-window binned counting strategy relative to experimental unbinned multivariate profiling, thereby establishing a rigorous and conservative baseline sensitivity floor.
  Furthermore, we project the discovery potential for the future High-Luminosity LHC (HL-LHC) phase at $\mathcal{L}_{\text{int}} = 3000$ fb$^{-1}$ under the official CERN Scenario II recommendations. We demonstrate quantitatively that the HL-LHC will achieve exceptional expected local statistical discovery significances between 10.22$σ$ and 16.61$σ$ within the 5-28 GeV mass window, comfortably clearing the discovery threshold and establishing the multi-muon channel as a primary probe for lightweight vector portals in upcoming collider phases.

</details>


### [99] [Quantum Simulation of Markovian and Non-Markovian Open Quantum Dynamics in Heavy-Ion Collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31173)
*Doojin Kim,Balbeer Singh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a quantum computing framework to simulate open-quantum-system dynamics—both Markovian and non-Markovian—in the context of heavy-ion collisions, using an auxiliary two-level pseudomode to encode memory; tracing it out recovers exact non-Markovian evolution, and the circuit smoothly reduces to the Markovian limit, enabling future quantum simulation of hard probes (e.g., jets, heavy quarks, quarkonia).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable quantum simulation of open quantum systems in high-energy nuclear physics—specifically, non-Markovian decoherence and dissipation effects relevant to hard probes in heavy-ion collisions—where classical simulation is intractable due to exponential scaling.

Method: Introduces an auxiliary two-level 'pseudomode' that stores memory and couples to both the subsystem and a residual Markovian bath; constructs a quantum circuit where tracing out the pseudomode yields exact non-Markovian dynamics governed by a prescribed memory kernel; verifies convergence to the Markovian limit under appropriate time-scale hierarchy.

Result: Demonstrates exact recovery of non-Markovian evolution via partial trace over the pseudomode; shows smooth circuit-based transition to the Markovian limit; establishes feasibility of quantum simulation for realistic bath memory kernels relevant to heavy-ion collision phenomenology.

Conclusion: The pseudomode-based framework provides a scalable, physically grounded method for quantum simulation of both Markovian and non-Markovian open-system dynamics, laying groundwork for quantum computation of hard-probe observables in QCD matter.

Abstract: We present a quantum computing framework for simulating open-quantum-system approaches based on Markovian and non-Markovian dynamics, which is relevant to heavy-ion collisions. To simulate the non-Markovian evolution on quantum computers, we introduce an auxiliary two-level pseudomode that carries the memory forward and couples to both the subsystem and the residual Markovian bath. We explicitly show that tracing out the pseudomode reproduces the non-Markovian evolution with the exact memory kernel. Moreover, in the relevant time scale hierarchy, the quantum circuit construction of the pseudomode smoothly converges to the Markovian limit. For a given bath memory kernel, our results demonstrate the feasibility of quantum simulations of both Markovian and non-Markovian dynamics, establishing a framework for future studies of hard probes such as jets, heavy quarks, and quarkonia in heavy-ion collisions.

</details>


<div id='gr-qc'></div>

# gr-qc [[Back]](#toc)

### [100] [Inspiralling Binary merger parameter reconstruction with lunar and satellite Laser Ranging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28753)
*Miguel Vanvlasselaer*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates using Earth-Moon and Earth-satellite ranging (LLR and GUEST) to detect gravitational waves from massive black hole binaries, focusing on reconstructing chirp mass (Mc) and luminosity distance (DL). It finds Mc recoverable with single-baseline ranging, but DL requires multi-baseline (e.g., two-satellite GUEST) to break sky-orientation degeneracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore low-frequency gravitational wave detection via precision ranging as a complement to space-based interferometers, and to assess feasibility of parameter estimation—particularly chirp mass and luminosity distance—from ranging data.

Method: Develops a parameter estimation framework assuming unperturbed Keplerian Earth-satellite motion; models perturbations induced by gravitational waves from inspiralling massive black hole binaries on ranging observables; performs synthetic data analysis for Lunar Laser Ranging (LLR) and two-satellite GUEST configurations; quantifies parameter recovery accuracy (σ_Mc/Mc, σ_DL/DL) versus SNR and identifies degeneracies.

Result: Chirp mass recovery scales as σ_Mc/Mc ∝ SNR^α with α ≈ −1 to −1.8 for both LLR and GUEST. Luminosity distance recovery fails in LLR due to SNR-independent degeneracy with sky-orientation angles (fractional uncertainty >100%); GUEST breaks this degeneracy, enabling SNR-dependent DL recovery comparable to Mc scaling.

Conclusion: Multi-baseline ranging architectures (e.g., GUEST) are essential for robust luminosity-distance inference in ranging-based GW astronomy, whereas chirp mass can be reliably estimated even with a single baseline like Earth–Moon.

Abstract: Precision tracking of Earth--Moon and Earth--satellite ranges offers a novel, low-frequency avenue for detecting gravitational waves from inspiralling massive black hole binaries, complementary to space-based interferometers. In this paper, we propose a first study of the reconstruction of the binary parameters from a synthetic set of range observations. \textit{Set-up}: Assuming an ideal unperturbed Keplerian motion for the earth-satellite binary, we develop a framework for reconstructing the chirp mass $\mathcal M_c$ and luminosity distance $D_L$ of chirping sources from the perturbations they induce on ranging observables, using both Lunar Laser Ranging (LLR) and a two-satellite ranging configuration (GUEST) as concrete case studies.
  \textit{Result}: We find that chirp-mass recovery tightly converges as a power law in SNR ($σ_{M_c}/M_c \propto \mathrm{SNR}^α$, $α\simeq -1$ to $-1.8$) for both LLR and GUEST. In contrast, the luminosity distance suffers from a severe, SNR-independent degeneracy with the sky-orientation angles in the LLR configuration, yielding fractional biases and uncertainties of tens to over a hundred percent regardless of signal strength. The two-satellite GUEST configuration breaks this degeneracy and restores a convergent, SNR-dependent recovery of $D_L$ comparable in scaling to that of the chirp mass. These results demonstrate that multi-baseline ranging architectures are essential for extracting luminosity-distance information from ranging-based gravitational wave searches, while chirp-mass measurements remain reliably accessible even with a single ranging baseline such as the Earth--Moon system.

</details>


### [101] [A Quasi-local Entropy for Black Hole Space-times](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28755)
*Raymond Isichei*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper demonstrates that the angular components of the Brown-York tensor on a finite-radius boundary around static, spherically symmetric black holes encode a quasi-local entropy that matches the Tolman-Ehrenfest temperature and converges to the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy at the horizon—thereby verifying the covariant entropy conjecture.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a quasi-local, geometrically grounded definition of black hole entropy consistent with thermodynamic expectations and to test the covariant entropy conjecture in a concrete setting.

Method: Analysis of the Brown-York stress-energy tensor on timelike boundaries of static, spherically symmetric black hole spacetimes; decomposition into angular components; identification of a temperature–entropy product structure using the Tolman-Ehrenfest redshifted temperature.

Result: A well-defined, monotonic quasi-local entropy is derived from angular Brown-York components; it vanishes at spatial infinity, increases inward, and equals the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy exactly at the horizon.

Conclusion: The derived entropy satisfies all key criteria of the covariant entropy conjecture, offering explicit, boundary-based confirmation in static spherical black hole geometries.

Abstract: We show that for static & spherically symmetric black hole geometries, the angular components of the Brown-York tensor on a finite radius boundary can be interpreted as the product of the Tolman-Ehrenfest temperature and a quasi-local entropy. This entropy vanishes at spatial infinity and monotonically increases as radius decreases, taking its maximum value on the horizon where it is equal to the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy. Thus, the behaviour of this entropy provides explicit verification of the covariant entropy conjecture.

</details>


### [102] [Acceleration and Rotation in Three Dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28789)
*Cameron R D Bunney,Ruth Gregory,Robert B Mann*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a new family of exact solutions in 3D Einstein gravity describing accelerating, rotating point particles and black holes, analyzes their geometry and AdS₃ embeddings, and highlights applications in thermodynamics, asymptotic symmetries, and holography.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the known catalogue of exact solutions in three-dimensional Einstein gravity and provide a framework for studying black hole thermodynamics, asymptotic symmetries, and holographic dualities.

Method: Construction and analysis of exact solutions to the three-dimensional Einstein equations; investigation of geometric properties (e.g., curvature, causal structure) and global isometric embedding into three-dimensional anti-de Sitter (AdS₃) space.

Result: A new family of exact solutions representing accelerating, rotating point particles and black holes in 3D Einstein gravity; characterization of their geometry and explicit embedding into AdS₃.

Conclusion: These solutions enrich the landscape of tractable models in lower-dimensional gravity and serve as valuable tools for probing fundamental aspects of quantum gravity via the AdS₃/CFT₂ correspondence and related frameworks.

Abstract: We present a new family of exact solutions representing accelerating, rotating, point particle and black hole solutions in three-dimensional Einstein gravity. We investigate their geometric properties and the global embedding in three dimensional anti-de Sitter space. Our results extend the catalogue of exact solutions in three-dimensional Einstein gravity and provide a useful setting for the study of black hole thermodynamics, asymptotic symmetries, and holography.

</details>


### [103] [Torsion and Nonmetricity Constraints From Neutron Spin Rotation in Polarized Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28832)
*Sepehr Samiei,Ralf Lehnert,W. Michael Snow*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops an effective field theory framework to characterize couplings of electrons and neutrons to spacetime torsion and nonmetricity up to mass dimension six, predicts new neutron-polarization effects in electron backgrounds, and places first experimental constraints on axially symmetric, direction-dependent in-matter torsion and nonmetricity using neutron spin-rotation data from a polarized-electron ferrimagnet.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe non-Riemannian geometric degrees of freedom (torsion and nonmetricity) in modified gravity theories via low-energy, high-precision laboratory experiments—particularly in matter environments where such backgrounds may be enhanced or aligned.

Method: Effective field theory construction of Lorentz- and CPT-violating operators coupling electron and neutron fields to torsion and nonmetricity up to mass dimension six; derivation of observable neutron spin-precession signatures induced by polarized electron backgrounds; analysis of existing neutron spin-rotation data from a compensated ferrimagnet with polarized electrons to extract limits.

Result: First experimental constraints on specific axially symmetric, direction-dependent combinations of in-matter torsion and nonmetricity; quantitative bounds on dimension-six coupling coefficients governing neutron–electron–geometry interactions.

Conclusion: Torsion and nonmetricity backgrounds in matter are testable via precision spin-dependent measurements; the EFT framework enables systematic interpretation of spin-rotation experiments as probes of metric-affine geometry; future improvements in polarized target density and neutron polarization control will enhance sensitivity.

Abstract: Many modified-gravity frameworks employ non-Riemannian connection degrees of freedom, including torsion and nonmetricity. In Einstein-Cartan-type theories, torsion is associated with spin density, while more general metric-affine frameworks can be investigated through phenomenological searches for in-matter torsion and nonmetricity backgrounds. This work applies an effective field theory approach to characterize general couplings of electron and neutron operators to spacetime torsion and nonmetricity up to mass dimension six, predicting novel neutron-polarization signatures in the presence of an electron background. Neutron spin-rotation data from a polarized-electron ensemble inside a compensated ferrimagnet are used to constrain for the first time axially symmetric, direction-dependent in-matter torsion and nonmetricity combinations.

</details>


### [104] [Stationary Rotating Geometries Associated with Nonsingular Pulsating Collapse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28850)
*A. D. Souza,F. A. Brito,J. Spinelly*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a rotating black hole solution by applying the Newman–Janis algorithm to a singularity-avoiding static geometry inspired by Loop Quantum Cosmology and braneworld models; it recovers Kerr/Schwarzschild limits as corrections vanish, and performs extended thermodynamic analysis including Davies-type singularities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To model quantum/braneworld-inspired corrections that avoid gravitational singularities predicted by classical GR, while extending such models to physically realistic rotating (Kerr-like) spacetimes.

Method: Applies the Azreg-Aïnou variant of the Newman–Janis algorithm to a static, comoving, singularity-avoiding exterior geometry (Gao–Lu–Shen–Faraoni model) to generate a stationary, axisymmetric rotating metric; conducts thermodynamic analysis including horizon properties, surface gravity, Hawking temperature, and extended state-space formalism.

Result: A new rotating black hole metric is obtained whose horizons, angular velocity, surface gravity, and Hawking temperature reduce smoothly to Kerr and Schwarzschild in the classical limit (l → 0); thermal response reveals Davies-type singularities and yields an extended thermodynamic state-space relation.

Conclusion: The constructed solution provides a consistent rotating extension of singularity-avoiding static models, supporting the viability of effective high-density corrections in black hole physics and enabling refined thermodynamic characterization beyond standard black hole mechanics.

Abstract: Since the first exact solutions of General Relativity (GR) were obtained, it became clear that the theory predicts a new class of compact objects: black holes. The Hawking--Penrose singularity theorems show that, under appropriate assumptions, gravitational collapse in GR leads to singular behavior. Motivated by the expectation that such regimes require physics beyond classical GR, effective approaches inspired by Loop Quantum Cosmology (LQC) and braneworld scenarios introduce high-density corrections. In this work we consider the static exterior geometry introduced by Gao, Lu, Shen, and Faraoni, associated with a comoving interior model in which the singularity is avoided through a sequence of collapse and bounce phases. Using the Newman--Janis algorithm in the Azreg-Aïnou formulation, we construct a stationary rotating exterior extension of this geometry. The resulting line element describes a stationary axisymmetric rotating spacetime whose horizon geometry, angular velocity, surface gravity, and Hawking temperature recover the Kerr and Schwarzschild limits when the quantum/braneworld correction becomes negligible ($l\to 0$). We further analyze the thermal response at fixed rotational state-space parameter, identify Davies-type singular points, and formulate an extended thermodynamic state-space relation.

</details>


### [105] [Spin-Graviton-Spin: a unique probe of quantum gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28901)
*Radmehr Fathi,Nima Khosravi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper identifies a unique low-energy signature of quantum gravity—specifically, a spin–momentum–dependent dipole-dipole interaction between two spin-1/2 particles mediated by a spin-2 graviton, scaling as 1/r³—distinguishable from spin-0 or spin-1 mediators; confirmed via both direct calculation and Foldy-Wouthuysen transformation, and proposed to be probed via four-partite entanglement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify an experimentally distinguishable low-energy signature uniquely attributable to quantum gravity—specifically, the spin-2 nature of the graviton—rather than environmental or other force-mediated effects.

Method: Analytical quantum field-theoretic calculation of the graviton-mediated interaction between two spin-1/2 particles; verification using the Foldy-Wouthuysen transformation to obtain the effective nonrelativistic Hamiltonian; analysis of spin-momentum coupling structure and scaling behavior in real space.

Result: A unique 1/r³ dipole-dipole interaction emerges that simultaneously entangles both particles' spins and momenta—a feature forbidden for spin-0 or spin-1 mediators at leading order; the interaction is invariant under Lorentz transformations and stems directly from the tensor structure of the spin-2 propagator.
