Conclusion: The spin-2 character of gravity manifests in a distinctive, testable entanglement signature between fermions—providing a concrete pathway toward low-energy quantum gravity phenomenology, with experimental proposals based on four-partite entanglement.

Abstract: The existence of quantum gravity is the most important question in theoretical physics. Besides the theoretical development in this direction, looking for its low-energy consequences can provide clues. However, besides availability, it is crucial to ensure that what is observed uniquely comes from quantum gravity and not from other forces in the environment. What is the unique feature of gravity? The spin-$2$ nature of its propagator. In this Letter, we calculate what the unique feature of the spin-$2$ graviton is in the interaction of two spin-$\frac{1}{2}$ particles. This unique feature shows itself in intertwining the spin and momentum of two particles simultaneously as a dipole-dipole interaction. This cannot happen for any spin-$0$ or spin-$1$ propagator, at least at leading order, which scales as $\frac{1}{r^3}$ in real space. We re-calculate the results using an independent approach, i.e., the Foldy-Wouthuysen transformation, and reach the same results. Due to simultaneous coupling of both particles' spins and momenta, a four-partite entanglement framework is suggested to observe this effect.

</details>


### [106] [Schwarzschild spectral ladders on the negative imaginary axis: Endpoint nonselection, branch-cut phase, and Jost classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29095)
*Davide Batic,Denys Dutykh,Mark Essa Sukaiti*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper investigates spectral discretisations for axial Schwarzschild perturbations and shows that stable negative-imaginary-axis (NIA) eigenvalue ladders—though numerically reproducible—do not correspond to true quasinormal modes (QNMs), as they fail the invariant Jost/Evans pole criterion; instead, they are artefacts tied to endpoint regularity choices (C¹ vs C²), branch-cut structure, and representation-dependent discretisation, with implications for convergence to the physical cut response and Price tail.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether numerically observed stable NIA eigenvalue ladders in compactified spectral discretisations of axial Schwarzschild perturbations represent genuine quasinormal poles or spurious artefacts arising from endpoint regularity, discretisation choice, and branch-cut physics.

Method: Analytical factorisation of ingoing–outgoing solutions; exact Chebyshev-grid root-radius analysis; arbitrary-precision matrix pencil computations; Jost determinant evaluation; argument-principle winding number calculations; comparison of C¹ and C² coordinate mappings; finite-frequency branch-strength zero detection; damping-controlled QNM recovery.

Result: A reproducible 68-point C¹ ladder is shown to be nonphysical: its frequencies yield stably nonzero physical lateral Jost determinants, zero winding in continuation strips, and no zeros on high-resolution on-cut scans; same-damping controls successfully recover known QNMs (n=60,100,130); the ladder aligns with Casals–Ottewill branch-cut phase and pairs with QNM damping projections (n=62–129); a branch-strength zero at α_q≈15.078 confirms cut-phase locking at the first member.

Conclusion: Stable finite-pencil NIA eigenvalues can be representation-dependent and fail the invariant Jost/Evans pole criterion; thus, they do not constitute quasinormal poles. The existence of a convergent representation yielding the Schwarzschild cut response and Price tail remains an open question.

Abstract: Compactified spectral discretisations may yield stable negative-imaginary-axis (NIA) eigenvalue ladders, but finite-matrix convergence does not establish quasinormal poles. For axial Schwarzschild perturbations, ingoing--outgoing factorisation leaves an unwanted horizon solution behaving as $t^{4α}$ at $Ω=-iα$, and a $C^\infty$-flat unwanted infinity solution. Thus endpoint $C^k$ regularity is nonselective for $4α>k$, so the $C^2$ continuum problem cannot have a discrete NIA spectrum over $α\simeq15$--32. Exact Chebyshev-grid formulas give roots-grid maximum radius $O(n^{2p})$ for $x\sim C(1-y)^{-p}$, explaining C1, $x=2/(1-y)$, versus C2, $x=4/(1-y)^2$. Arbitrary-precision pencils nevertheless reveal a reproducible 68-point C1 ladder, while C2 reorganises it. Both physical lateral Jost determinants remain stably nonzero at every C1 frequency. A 0.05-spaced on-cut scan finds no zero, and refined argument-principle calculations give zero winding in both continuation strips. This rejects all 68 candidates, although the mesh and strip evidence is not interval-certified. Same-damping controls recover Schwarzschild QNMs $n=60,100,130$, with $|\widehat{\mathcal D}|=10^{-48}$--$10^{-56}$. The ladder has surface-gravity quarter spacing, follows the parameter-free Casals--Ottewill branch-cut-strength phase, and pairs with damping projections of QNMs $n=62,\ldots,129$. At its first member, a finite-frequency calculation finds a branch-strength zero at $α_q=15.07832396512359$, between the asymptotic prediction and the C1 root, supporting, but not proving, sequence-wide cut-phase locking. Thus accurate finite-pencil eigenvalues can fail the invariant Jost/Evans pole criterion. Whether representation-dependent nodes with Keldysh weights converge collectively to the Schwarzschild cut response and Price tail remains open.

</details>


### [107] [Exact dynamics and the spin wall for large-spin particles in Schwarzschild spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29115)
*Chao-Jun Feng,Rui-Hui Lin*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper provides an exact, nonperturbative treatment of spinning test particle motion in Schwarzschild spacetime using the MPD equations under the Tulczyjew–Dixon spin condition—revealing a novel 'spin wall' (a double-root effective potential barrier of purely spin origin), horizon shielding beyond critical spin, orbital filtering, disappearance of ISCO at large spin, and exact weak-field perihelion precession—all absent in linear-in-spin approximations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond the standard linear-in-spin approximation for spinning particles in general relativity and uncover qualitative dynamical features inaccessible to perturbative treatments—especially those arising from strong spin-curvature coupling in strong-field regimes.

Method: Exact, nonperturbative analysis of planar MPD equations in Schwarzschild spacetime under the Tulczyjew–Dixon spin supplementary condition; elimination of four-velocity yields a closed algebraic system; radial motion is reduced to an effective-potential problem without any spin expansion; analytical solutions derived in weak-field limit and full nonlinear regime.

Result: Discovery of a spin-induced 'spin wall'—an impenetrable radial barrier (double root of effective potential) determined solely by mass and spin; for spin above critical value, wall lies outside event horizon and shields it; only specific (E, L, S) orbits penetrate; ISCO vanishes beyond critical spin; exact perihelion precession formula derived; all results recover standard geodesic limits as spin → 0.

Conclusion: Spin-curvature coupling induces genuinely nonperturbative phenomena—most notably the spin wall and orbital filtering—that are invisible to any finite-order spin expansion; these have potential astrophysical implications for accretion physics and gravitational-wave modeling of extreme-mass-ratio inspirals.

Abstract: The motion of a spinning test particle in a curved spacetime is governed by the Mathisson--Papapetrou--Dixon (MPD) equations and deviates from geodesic motion already at first order in the spin. While essentially all existing studies truncate the dynamics at linear order in the spin, we present an exact, nonperturbative treatment of planar motion in Schwarzschild spacetime under the Tulczyjew--Dixon spin supplementary condition: eliminating the four-velocity recasts the MPD system into a closed algebraic form and reduces the radial motion to an effective-potential problem, with no expansion in the spin at any stage. This exact framework uncovers qualitative features that are absent from---and in fact unattainable within---the linearized description. Most notably, for sufficiently large spin the effective potential develops a double root at a characteristic radius determined solely by the particle mass and spin, marking an impenetrable \emph{spin wall} of purely spin origin; beyond a critical spin the wall lies outside the event horizon and shields it from generic infalling particles. Moreover, the wall is a filter for particle: only orbits with a specific combinations of spin, angular momentum and energy can penetrate it, all others being reflected before reaching the horizon. In addition, the innermost stable circular orbit, which in linear treatments merely shifts continuously with spin, is obtained in closed form in the weak-field limit and is shown to cease to exist at sufficiently large spin. We further compute the spin correction to the perihelion precession in the weak-field limit and verify that all results reduce to the standard ones at vanishing spin. The spin wall and its filtering rule are genuine nonperturbative phenomena, invisible to any finite-order expansion in the spin, with potential observational signatures in accretion flows around compact objects.

</details>


### [108] [Yukawa modification of Traversable Wormholes supported by Holographic Dark Energy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29205)
*Remo Garattini,Phongpichit Channuie,Kirill Zatrimaylov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends holographic dark energy (HDE) models by incorporating Yukawa deformations into various HDE profiles (Bekenstein-Hawking, Moradpour, Renyi, Mixed), analyzes their behavior under zero tidal forces with an inhomogeneous equation of state, identifies a divergence in ωᵣ(r) for one Yukawa-BH profile at large r and proposes a large-distance modification to regularize it, and finds that only Yukawa-BH yields globally positive energy density—others exhibit limited positivity or full negativity, indicating Yukawa terms generically induce negative energy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend holographic dark energy models by incorporating Yukawa-type corrections and assess their impact on energy density positivity, equation-of-state behavior, and viability under zero tidal force conditions in wormhole spacetimes.

Method: Yukawa deformations are applied to four HDE profiles; an inhomogeneous equation of state p_r(r) = ω_r(r)ρ(r) is adopted under zero tidal force condition; asymptotic analysis identifies divergences in ω_r(r); a large-r modification is introduced to regularize the divergent case; stress-energy tensor components are computed and energy density sign is analyzed across all profiles.

Result: Only the Yukawa-deformed Bekenstein-Hawking HDE yields globally positive energy density; Yukawa-Moradpour and Yukawa-Renyi show positivity only in a finite radial region; Yukawa-Mixed Energy Density is entirely negative; a divergence in ω_r(r) → ∞ as r → ∞ occurs for one Yukawa-BH variant, resolved by a finite-large-distance cutoff preserving throat physics.

Conclusion: Yukawa deformations generally introduce negative energy contributions, with only the Yukawa-Bekenstein-Hawking profile sustaining global energy positivity; the zero tidal force condition is viable only in a large but finite domain for the divergent case, implying limitations on extended traversable wormhole geometries in these deformed HDE frameworks.

Abstract: Inspired by holographic dark energy models, we extend the analysis performed in Ref.\cite{RGPC} taking into account Yukawa deformations on the different energy density (HDE) profiles. The profiles we have explored are: the Bekenstein-Hawking HDE, the Moradpour energy density, the Standard Renyi HDE and the Mixed Energy Density. With the help of an inhomogeneous Equation of State of the form $p_{r}(r)=ω_{r}\left( r\right) ρ(r)$, we have considered Zero Tidal Forces. Differently from the original case, only a particular case of the family of the Bekenstein-Hawking HDE profiles distorted by the Yukawa term has developed a divergent $ω_{r}\left( r\right) $ for $r\rightarrow \infty $. To cure such a divergence we have introduced a modification at large distances in order to have a finite result. This means that the Zero Tidal Forces can be imposed in a very large but limited region of the spacetime. Such modification did not change the behavior of the Equation of State close to the throat. For every proposal we have computed the components of the Stress-Energy Tensor. What we have found is that the Yukawa-Bekenstein-Hawking HDE is the only positive profile, while the Yukawa-Moradpour and the Yukawa-Renyi have a limited region where the energy density is positive. The remaining Yukawa-Mixed Energy Density is always negative. This means that the Yukawa distortion introduces a kind of negative energy density.

</details>


### [109] [Resonant bound orbits and kludge waveforms in rotating Konoplya-Zhidenko black hole spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29234)
*Qiyu Dai,Xiongjun Fang,Xiao-Mei Kuang,Jiliang Jing*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies timelike bound orbits and resonant periodic trajectories in the rotating Konoplya-Zhidenko black hole spacetime, computes associated gravitational-wave signatures using quadrupole-kludge waveforms, and finds that black hole deformation shifts resonance locations and alters both orbital dynamics and GW signals—placing them in the millihertz band detectable by space-based observatories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how deviations from Kerr geometry—specifically in the rotating Konoplya-Zhidenko black hole spacetime—affect relativistic orbital dynamics and gravitational-wave emission, especially for resonant bound orbits relevant to extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs).

Method: Uses separability of the Hamilton-Jacobi equation to parameterize eccentric and inclined timelike bound orbits via $(p,e,z_1)$; derives constants of motion $(E,L_z,Q)$; computes fundamental orbital frequencies to identify radial-polar and radial-azimuthal resonances; constructs physically scaled quadrupole-kludge waveforms for equatorial resonant orbits; analyzes frequency-domain properties of resulting gravitational-wave signals.

Result: KZ deformation shifts resonance locations, modifies orbital trajectories, and alters gravitational-wave signal morphology; characteristic strain peaks in the millihertz band—compatible with LISA and other space-based detectors.

Conclusion: Resonant orbits in rotating KZ spacetimes produce distinct, detectable gravitational-wave signatures whose features encode spacetime deformation parameters, offering a pathway to test strong-field gravity beyond Kerr using future space-based GW observatories.

Abstract: We investigate timelike bound motion, resonant periodic orbits, and their gravitational-wave signatures in the rotating Konoplya-Zhidenko (KZ) black hole spacetime. Using the separability of the Hamilton-Jacobi equation, we parameterize eccentric and inclined bound orbits by $(p,e,z_1)$, derive the corresponding constants of motion $(E,L_z,Q)$, and use the orbital frequencies to identify resonant configurations. We study a range of resonances, including radial-polar resonances of inclined orbits and radial-azimuthal resonances of equatorial eccentric orbits. We further construct physically scaled quadrupole-kludge waveforms for representative equatorial resonant orbits and analyze their frequency-domain characteristics. Our results show that the KZ deformation shifts the resonance locations and modifies both the orbital trajectories and the resulting gravitational-wave signals. The corresponding characteristic strain lies predominantly in the millihertz band, placing these signals in the frequency range relevant to space-based gravitational wave detectors.

</details>


### [110] [Energy extraction processes and thermodynamics in rotating Weyl conformal black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29277)
*Dilmurod Ortiqboev,Farruh Atamurotov,Ahmadjon Abdujabbarov,Chen Zhou,G. Mustafa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies the Penrose process and black hole thermodynamics in conformal Weyl gravity, deriving horizon properties, geodesic dynamics in the equatorial plane, and assessing energy extraction efficiency and validity of the four laws of BH thermodynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend understanding of energy extraction (Penrose process) and thermodynamic laws for rotating black holes beyond general relativity, specifically within conformal Weyl gravity—a fourth-order gravitational theory with parameters γ (galactic rotation curve curvature) and κ (linked to cosmological constant Λ).

Method: Analytical derivation of event horizon locations and extremality conditions for rotating black holes in conformal Weyl gravity; perturbative and exact computation of horizon radius; restriction of geodesic analysis to the equatorial plane; evaluation of particle trajectories, energy extraction efficiency, angular momentum loss (deceleration), and verification of the zeroth–fourth laws of black hole thermodynamics.

Result: Exact and perturbative expressions for event horizon radius are obtained; the original Penrose process remains feasible but with modified efficiency due to γ and κ; black hole deceleration occurs under particle emission; the four laws of black hole thermodynamics hold, though temperature and entropy acquire corrections dependent on γ and κ.

Conclusion: Conformal Weyl gravity supports physically consistent rotating black hole solutions where the Penrose process operates and thermodynamic laws remain valid—albeit with parameter-dependent modifications—affirming robustness of key relativistic astrophysical phenomena beyond Einstein gravity.

Abstract: This work investigates the original Penrose process (PP), as well as black hole (BH) thermodynamics, within the framework of conformal Weyl gravity. The theory is characterized by the dimensionful constants $γ$, which encodes the asymptotic rotational curvature of galactic rotation curves in an otherwise asymptotically flat spacetime, and $κ$, which is associated with the cosmological background constant $Λ$. We determine the locations of the event horizon and the extreme configurations for rotating BHs in this theory, and we provide both perturbative and exact expressions for the event horizon radius. The geodesic equations are restricted to the equatorial plane, and the corresponding dynamics, including energy extraction processes, are studied exclusively in this plane. As the principal results, we examine the feasibility and efficiency of the original PP, analyze BH deceleration mechanisms, and test the validity of the four laws of BH thermodynamics in the context of conformal Weyl gravity.

</details>


### [111] [Optimizing Superconducting Microwave Cavities for Gravitational Wave Sensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29293)
*Lars Fischer,Wolfgang Hillert,Tom Krokotsch,Gudrid Moortgat-Pick*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a formalism for optimizing superconducting microwave cavity detectors for high-frequency gravitational wave searches, showing geometry choice alone can improve signal-to-noise ratio by 10× and that optimal cavity design depends on specific physics goals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve sensitivity of superconducting microwave cavities for detecting high-frequency gravitational waves from cosmological or astrophysical sources—current sensitivities are insufficient to probe theoretically motivated parameter spaces.

Method: Develops a comprehensive formalism incorporating electromagnetic field–cavity structural back-action and interdependencies among all key experimental parameters; applies it via a 2D model to derive figures of merit and optimize cavity geometries.

Result: Geometry optimization yields up to one-order-of-magnitude improvement in signal-to-noise power ratio over existing same-size prototypes; distinct physics objectives (e.g., different GW frequency bands or coupling models) require distinct optimal cavity designs.

Conclusion: Early-stage incorporation of physics-motivated figures of merit is essential for effective detector design; the proposed formalism enables systematic, performance-driven optimization across diverse high-frequency gravitational wave search scenarios.

Abstract: Superconducting microwave cavities loaded with radio frequency fields are a powerful tool to search for weak forces or electromagnetic perturbations due to new physics. One source of such signals can be high-frequency gravitational waves emitted from cosmological or unknown astrophysical events. However, large sensitivity improvements are still necessary to reach the parameter space motivated by theoretical models. In this work, we present a formalism to guide the design of such detectors across a broad range of frequencies and signal forms. By incorporating the interdependencies of all relevant parameters and the back-action of the electromagnetic fields on the cavity structure, we describe how figures of merit can be derived for a broad class of relevant experimental setups. Using a two-dimensional model, we demonstrate this formalism and present examples for optimized cavity geometries. We find that the choice of geometry alone can increase the signal-to-noise power ratio by an order of magnitude compared to an existing prototype of the same size. We also show that different physics goals lead to different optimal cavities, thus proving the need to consider such figures of merit at an early stage when designing a new detector.

</details>


### [112] [Automated identification and subtraction of gravitational-wave glitches using boundary refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29295)
*Mohammad Abu Thaher Chowdhury,Soumya D Mohanty*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces three novel methods (AMPS, FLARE, CRISP) to precisely identify the time boundaries of transient noise glitches in gravitational wave data, enabling more accurate and efficient glitch subtraction—significantly improving signal recovery (95–97% SNR recovery vs. ~64% for wavelet shrinkage alone) while reducing computational cost and signal loss.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing glitch detection tools lack precise temporal boundary identification, forcing padded-window subtraction that wastes computation and erases astrophysical signal; precise boundary localization is needed to minimize signal loss and maximize subtraction fidelity.

Method: Three boundary identification methods: AMPS (robust amplitude thresholding), FLARE (fitness-optimized segmented spline fitting), CRISP (connected-region detection in time-frequency spectrogram power); paired with subtraction techniques: adaptive spline fitting, wavelet shrinkage, and their combination; evaluated on five GravitySpy glitches from LIGO O1–O3.

Result: Combined subtraction recovers 95–97% of injected chirp SNR on four broadband glitches; CRISP yields most uniform boundary width; AMPS and CRISP compute boundaries in <100 ms (2–3 orders faster than FLARE); residual low-frequency artifact remains for GW170817’s glitch under default CRISP/AMPS boundaries—a deliberate signal-preserving tradeoff.

Conclusion: Precise glitch boundary identification is feasible and impactful: AMPS and CRISP offer real-time-capable, high-fidelity alternatives to heuristic padding; future work must address scattered-light glitches.

Abstract: Transient noise artifacts, or glitches, in gravitational wave strain data elevate the false alarm rate of astrophysical searches and degrade parameter estimation when overlapping a signal. No method previously identified a glitch's time boundary: existing detection and classification tools flag and label glitches without resolving their extent, forcing subtraction to run over padded windows that cost time and erase signal beyond the glitch itself. We present three boundary identification methods, AMPS (Amplitude-based Multi-glitch Pulse Segmenter), FLARE (Fitness-based Localization And Refinement Extractor), and CRISP (connected-region identification via spectrogram power), paired with three subtraction techniques, adaptive spline fitting, wavelet shrinkage, and their combination, across five glitches from the GravitySpy database spanning Advanced LIGO's first three observing runs. AMPS sets a boundary from a robust amplitude threshold, FLARE from the best fitness of a segmented spline fit, and CRISP from spectrogram power. Injecting a chirp signal on four broadband glitches, the combined technique recovers 95 to 97 percent of the injected signal-to-noise ratio, against roughly 64 percent for wavelet shrinkage alone. CRISP gives the most uniform boundary width across glitches; AMPS and CRISP both identify a boundary in a fraction of a second, two to three orders of magnitude faster than FLARE. For the glitch overlapping GW170817, a low-frequency residual persists under the default boundary, a deliberate tradeoff against removing signal power. Scattered light glitches remain untested and are the primary direction for future work.

</details>


### [113] [How much of the black hole ringdown is sourced within the light ring?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29466)
*Mark Ho-Yeuk Cheung*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper challenges the idea that a 'direct wave' component in black hole ringdown gravitational waveforms originates near the event horizon; instead, it shows—using Teukolsky equation solutions for a plunging particle—that <10% of waveform amplitude arises from within the prograde light ring, supporting the view that both quasinormal modes and direct waves primarily probe the light ring, not the near-horizon region. For GW250114, the light-ring-sourced component has SNR ≲ 1 post-peak in filtered data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the claim that rational filtering of black hole ringdown waveforms isolates a 'direct wave' sourced near the event horizon, thereby probing near-horizon physics.

Method: Solves the Teukolsky equation for a point particle plunging into a Kerr black hole; computes and compares amplitudes of gravitational waves sourced inside vs. outside the prograde light ring, with and without rational filtering.

Result: Waves sourced within the prograde light ring contribute ≤10% of total amplitude regardless of filtering; for GW250114, the light-ring-sourced component yields SNR ≲ 1 after the peak of filtered waves.

Conclusion: Quasinormal modes and the so-called 'direct wave' are predominantly sensitive to the light ring geometry—not the near-horizon region—undermining interpretations that use the direct wave as a probe of horizon-scale physics.

Abstract: Not much. It has been proposed that applying a rational filter to a black hole ringdown gravitational waveform reveals a direct wave component sourced near the event horizon. By solving the Teukolsky equation for a point particle plunging into a Kerr black hole, I show that the maximum amplitude of the waves sourced within the prograde light ring is $\lesssim 10\%$ that of those sourced by the full trajectory no matter if the waves are filtered or not. This agrees with the interpretation of quasinormal modes as waves orbiting the light ring and the direct wave as the power of these modes redistributed in time. Therefore, both quasinormal modes and direct waves are more a probe of the light ring than the near-horizon region. For GW250114, I estimate that the component of the waves sourced within the light ring have a signal-to-noise ratio $ρ\lesssim 1$ measured after the peak of the filtered waves.

</details>


### [114] [Cutoff-Stable Null Convergence and Directional Rigidity in Bianchi I Spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29556)
*Ye Zhou,Alan Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proves an endpoint-free optical rigidity theorem for Bianchi I spacetimes, distinguishing two rigidity regimes without assuming diagonal spatial metrics; it shows that two-sided completeness of null congruences forces vanishing optical tensor and null Ricci contraction, leading to metric freezing on a conserved kernel, classifies saturation geometries (0/1/2 unoriented saturated lines), and under stronger null convergence, implies global Minkowski structure; periodic models saturate the four-ray bound in a weaker regime where pointwise null convergence fails.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a geometric rigidity criterion for Bianchi I spacetimes using null geodesic congruences without restrictive assumptions (e.g., diagonal spatial metric), and to clarify the interplay between optical geometry, null energy conditions, and global spacetime structure.

Method: Derives an endpoint-free version of the affine Raychaudhuri equation for twist-free null congruences; analyzes liminf behavior of expansion boundary terms under independent past/future cutoff removal; applies Bianchi I symmetry and conservation laws to constrain the spatial metric evolution; classifies saturation via algebraic and geometric properties of the optical tensor's kernel and null Ricci contraction.

Result: Two-sided completeness of a smooth, regular, twist-free null congruence implies pointwise vanishing of the optical tensor and null Ricci contraction; in Bianchi I, this freezes the spatial metric on the kernel of a conserved covector; saturation geometry admits at most two unoriented saturated lines (four oriented rays); under pointwise null convergence, existence of one two-sided complete null geodesic implies globally constant spatial metric and full cosmic-time interval ℝ, yielding Minkowski universal cover; periodic models achieve maximal four-ray saturation only in the weaker cutoff-stable regime where pointwise null convergence fails on open time intervals.

Conclusion: Optical rigidity provides a sharp dichotomy: either the spacetime exhibits nontrivial dynamics with incomplete or cutoff-dependent null congruences, or it is highly constrained—either static or globally Minkowski—depending on the strength of the null energy condition and completeness assumptions.

Abstract: We derive an endpoint-free optical rigidity theorem and use it to separate two rigidity regimes in Bianchi I spacetimes, without assuming that the spatial metric is diagonal in a fixed basis. For a smooth, regular, twist-free null congruence, the affine Raychaudhuri equation expresses a finite null-Ricci integral as an expansion boundary term minus a nonnegative optical bulk. If the past and future cutoffs are removed independently and their two-end liminf is nonnegative, two-sided completeness forces the optical tensor and the null Ricci contraction to vanish pointwise. In Bianchi I this freezes the spatial metric on the fixed kernel of the conserved covector. We classify the resulting saturation geometry: a non-static metric has zero, one, or two unoriented saturated lines, equivalently zero, two, or four oriented rays, and a third line forces staticity. Under the stronger pointwise null convergence condition, the existence of a single two-sided complete null geodesic already forces the spatial metric to be constant and the cosmic-time interval to be all of $\mathbb R$; on the Cartesian universal cover the spacetime is Minkowski. Periodic models attain the four-ray saturation bound in the weaker cutoff-stable regime, with $I_p^{\mathrm{ind}}=-\infty$ in every nonsaturated direction, while pointwise null convergence fails on open time intervals. Matter and achronal averaged-null-energy consequences are stated under an explicit matching assumption on field equations and cutoff prescriptions.

</details>


### [115] [Testing $f(Q)$ Gravity with Logarithmic Equation of State Using Latest Cosmological Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29718)
*Chaymae Karam,Dalale Mhamdi,Taoufik Ouali,Rachid Ahl Laamara,Mohamed Bennai*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores a logarithmic dark energy parametrization in power-law $f(Q)$ gravity, derives an analytical Hubble parameter, and constrains the model using Pantheon+, DESI DR2 BAO, and Cosmic Chronometers; statistical tests (AIC/BIC) show it is a flexible, competitive alternative to ΛCDM, especially at low redshifts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test whether a logarithmic redshift-dependent equation of state $ω_{de}(z)=ω_0+ω_1\ln(1+z)$—offering smooth, non-constant evolution—can improve phenomenological richness and observational consistency in $f(Q)$ gravity compared to standard dark energy or ΛCDM.

Method: Analytical derivation of the Hubble parameter in the $f(Q)=Q+6γH_0^2(Q/Q_0)^n$ model with logarithmic $ω_{de}(z)$; cosmological parameter estimation via Markov Chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) using Pantheon+ SNe Ia, DESI DR2 BAO, and Cosmic Chronometers; model comparison via Akaike and Bayesian Information Criteria.

Result: The logarithmic $f(Q)$ model fits current data well; MCMC yields constrained parameters; AIC/BIC indicate it is statistically competitive with ΛCDM—slightly disfavored but not ruled out—and offers enhanced flexibility and richer low-redshift dynamics.

Conclusion: The logarithmic $ω_{de}(z)$ parametrization in power-law $f(Q)$ gravity is a physically viable and observationally consistent extension of standard cosmology, providing greater descriptive power at low redshifts without significant statistical penalty.

Abstract: In this paper, we investigate the late-time accelerated expansion of the Universe in power law $f(Q)$ gravity, where a logarithmic dark energy parametrization is considered: $ω_{de}(z)=ω_0+ω_1\ln(1+z)$. This description gives a smooth deviation from a constant equation of state within the complete range of redshifts. We obtain an analytical expression for the Hubble parameter and we use a Markov Chain Monte Carlo to compare the model with the most recent observational data obtained from Pantheon$^+$ Type Ia supernovae, Baryon Acoustic Oscillation measurements from the second data release (DR2) of the Dark Energy Spectroscopic Instrument, and Cosmic Chronometers. We then perform a statistical comparison between our model and the standard $Λ$CDM model using the Akaike Information Criterion and the Bayesian Information Criterion. From our results, we conclude that the use of logarithmic parametrization in the $f(Q)$ gravity model $f(Q)=Q+6γH_0^2\left(\frac{Q}{Q_0}\right)^n$ is a valid and more flexible alternative to standard dark energy models, as it provides a richer phenomenology at low redshifts.

</details>


### [116] [Peeling property for the Einstein scalar field equations with the nonzero cosmological constant](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29723)
*Jialue Li,Xiao Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies Einstein-scalar field equations with nonzero cosmological constant Λ in Bondi-Sachs coordinates, deriving asymptotic expansions under outgoing radiation condition; it identifies four Λ-independent free functions, shows one can be gauged away, proves peeling when one vanishes, and derives a Bondi energy-momentum loss formula relevant to gravitational wave observations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand gravitational wave dynamics in Λ-dominated spacetimes—motivated by interactions among gravitational waves, dark matter, and dark energy—and extend the Bondi-Sachs framework to nonzero cosmological constant.

Method: Analysis of asymptotic structure of Einstein-scalar field equations in Bondi-Sachs metric; imposition of outgoing radiation condition; coordinate gauge analysis; derivation of peeling behavior and Bondi energy-momentum evolution via null infinity expansion.

Result: Asymptotic expansions depend on four Λ-independent functions (B, X̃, Ỹ, Ĩ); B can be set to zero via coordinate transformation; peeling holds iff Ĩ = 0; a Bondi energy-momentum loss formula is derived for Λ ≠ 0, with physical implications for observed gravitational wave data.

Conclusion: The presence of nonzero Λ fundamentally alters the asymptotic structure and conservation laws at null infinity: the Bondi energy-momentum is no longer conserved and exhibits loss governed by Ĩ and Λ-dependent terms, offering a theoretical basis for interpreting energy dissipation in Λ-coupled gravitational wave signals.

Abstract: Inspired by interaction of gravitational waves, dark matters and dark energy, we study the Einstein scalar field equations with the nonzero cosmological constant for the Bondi-Sachs metrics. We provide the asymptotic expansions under the outgoing radiation condition. Unlike the case of the zero cosmological constant, the asymptotic expansions depend on four additional $Λ$-independent functions $B(u, θ, φ)$, $\tilde{X}(u, θ, φ)$, $\tilde{Y}(u, θ, φ)$ and $\tilde{I}(u)$. We show that, under suitable coordinate transformation, we can make $B(u, θ, φ)=0$. We prove the peeling property for the Einstein scalar field equations if $\tilde{I}=0$, and derive a loss formula of the Bondi energy-momentum for the nonzero cosmological constant. We remark that, for certain real data from gravitational waves, the loss formula is likely to indicate the loss property of the Bondi energy-momentum.

</details>


### [117] [Two Languages for the Same Resonance: From Nuclear Decay to Black-Hole Ringdown](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29806)
*Okuto Morikawa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper traces the historical genealogy of quantum resonances and black-hole quasinormal modes, showing their mathematical equivalence while distinguishing shared formalism from independent historical development.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconstruct the historical genealogy—not just assert the mathematical equivalence—between nuclear resonance theory and black-hole perturbation theory, clarifying how parallel developments converged.

Method: Chronological reconstruction of key milestones (1920s–1970s) in both fields, supplemented by a conceptual dictionary mapping physical and mathematical correspondences (e.g., boundary conditions, pole locations, residues).

Result: A unified timeline linking Sommerfeld’s radiation condition, Regge–Wheeler/Vishveshwara/Press work on BH QNMs, and Aguilar–Balslev–Combes analytic dilation; plus a precise operational dictionary bridging terminology and interpretation across disciplines.

Conclusion: Quantum resonances and black-hole QNMs are mathematically equivalent but historically distinct; their convergence reflects deep structural unity in scattering theory, not conceptual derivation from one field to the other.

Abstract: Quantum resonances and black-hole quasinormal modes (QNMs) are described by closely related mathematics: radiative boundary conditions, analytic continuation of Green functions or resolvents, poles on nonphysical sheets, and non-self-adjoint spectral representations. This article does not introduce that equivalence but reconstructs its genealogy. Nuclear resonance theory grew from radioactive decay, reaction cross sections, metastable compound states, and complex energies, whereas black-hole perturbation theory grew from spacetime stability and causal response.
  We place the two developments on a common chronology, from Sommerfeld's radiation condition and early nuclear-decay theory through Regge--Wheeler perturbations, Vishveshwara's 1968 dissertation and 1970 papers, Press's 1971 quasi-normal terminology, and the independent Aguilar--Balslev--Combes theory of analytic dilation, then follow their later mathematical reunification in scattering-resonance theory.
  Alongside the chronology, we give a working dictionary relating no-incoming and horizon-ingoing/infinity-outgoing conditions, pole energy and complex frequency, width and damping rate, residues and excitation factors, and nonresonant background with branch-cut and prompt contributions. The aim is to make the cross-disciplinary equivalence operational without flattening its history, and to distinguish mathematical equivalence from historical genealogy.

</details>


### [118] [Exotic Spinor Deformation of a Schwarzschild Black Hole: Perturbations and Stability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30024)
*Luís Rodolfo dos Santos Filho,Bertha Cuadros-Melgar*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores how exotic spinorial topology affects fermion propagation in Schwarzschild spacetime by deforming the Dirac operator via a tetrad incorporating a topological gradient; it predicts a topological impedance surface at r_topo = 1/q that suppresses radial group velocity, induces birefringence, and modifies greybody factors and quasinormal modes—revealing background stability and broken isospectrality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how exotic spinorial topology—encoded in a topological gradient ∂_μφ—affects fermionic dynamics in curved spacetime, particularly whether it induces effective geometric modifications beyond standard GR and alters quantum field behavior near black holes.

Method: The authors incorporate a topological gradient ∂_μφ into the Dirac operator via tetrad deformation in Schwarzschild geometry; they analyze resulting modified spinor equations, identify an emergent topological impedance surface, compute radial group velocity suppression, derive birefringent propagation, and perform spinorial perturbation analysis to obtain greybody factors and quasinormal mode spectra using superpartner potentials.

Result: A topological impedance surface forms at r_topo = 1/q, suppressing radial group velocity of exotic spinorial modes and inducing birefringence; greybody factor is modified; quasinormal spectrum confirms linear stability of the Schwarzschild background and shows absence of isospectrality between superpartner potentials.

Conclusion: Exotic spinorial topology acts as a source of effective geometric deformation in Schwarzschild spacetime, leading to observable wave-propagation effects (birefringence, impedance) and spectral signatures (non-isospectral QNMs), suggesting topological degrees of freedom may leave imprints on black hole spectroscopy and scattering observables.

Abstract: In this work we investigate how exotic spinorial topology modifies the propagation of fermionic modes in the Schwarzschild geometry. By incorporating a topological gradient, $\partial_μ\varphi$, into the Dirac operator through a tetrad deformation, we show that the exotic spinorial sector naturally induces an effective geometric deformation of the Schwarzschild background. The central consequence of this construction is the emergence of a topological impedance surface at $\rtopo=1/q$, which suppresses the radial group velocity of the exotic spinorial modes, yielding a birefringent propagation pattern. Using a spinorial perturbation we discuss the greybody factor and the quasinormal response. The quasinormal spectrum shows the stability of the background metric and the absence of isospectrality for the superpartner potentials.

</details>


### [119] [Periodic orbits around a magnetically charged black hole in $f(R,T)$ gravity coupled with Euler-Heisenberg electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30049)
*Jun-Long Huang,Chen-Kai Qiao,Jun Tao*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies zoom-whirl periodic orbits of a small compact object around a magnetically charged black hole in f(R,T) gravity with Euler-Heisenberg nonlinear electrodynamics, analyzing how magnetic charge Q_m and the Euler-Heisenberg parameter a affect ISCO, MBO, precession parameter q, orbital trajectories, and gravitational waveforms via the numerical kludge method.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how magnetic charge and nonlinear electrodynamics modify relativistic orbital dynamics and gravitational-wave signatures in extended theories of gravity, particularly for extreme-mass-ratio inspirals.

Method: Systematic numerical analysis of geodesic motion; computation of ISCO and MBO radii; evaluation of precession parameter q; trajectory integration for zoom-whirl orbits; generation of gravitational waveforms using the numerical kludge (NK) method.

Result: Magnetic charge Q_m strongly modifies the precession parameter q, zoom-whirl orbital morphology, and induces significant phase shifts in gravitational waveforms; the Euler-Heisenberg parameter a has comparatively weaker effects; ISCO and MBO radii are sensitive to Q_m.

Conclusion: Magnetic charge is a dominant modulator of strong-field orbital dynamics and gravitational-wave phasing in this modified gravity–nonlinear electrodynamics framework, suggesting its potential detectability in future space-based GW observatories.

Abstract: We systematically investigate the zoom-whirl periodic orbits of a small compact object orbiting a magnetically charged black hole in $f(R, T)$ gravity coupled with Euler-Heisenberg nonlinear electrodynamics. We evaluate the impact of the magnetic charge $Q_m$ and the Euler-Heisenberg parameter $a$ on the characteristics of the innermost stable circular orbit (ISCO) and the marginally bound orbit (MBO). Particularly, we focus on the precession parameter q and systematically examine how the magnetic charge and other parameters affect the trajectories of periodic orbits. Using the numerical kludge method, we generate gravitational waveforms for these periodic orbits. The results presented in our work demonstrate that the magnetic charge can significantly modify not only the precession parameter but also the orbital trajectories of the periodic orbits. Moreover, changing the magnetic charge $Q_m$ could cause a significant phase shift in the gravitational waveforms, while other parameters exert a relatively weaker influence on the gravitational waves.

</details>


### [120] [Spacelike reduction of the gravitational topological terms and associated helicity densities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30154)
*Hongxi Huang,Jiang Long,Gan Zhao,Xin-Hao Zhou*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper identifies and classifies four distinct helicity densities in linearized gravity by reducing the Pontryagin and Nieh-Yan topological terms to a spacelike hypersurface and applying the scalar-vector-tensor (SVT) decomposition; it then applies these densities to several physically relevant weak-field gravitational systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The existence of multiple inequivalent definitions of helicity in gravity motivates a systematic, geometrically grounded classification—especially one tied to topological invariants and mode decomposition—to clarify physical interpretations and enable applications in astrophysical and gravitational-wave contexts.

Method: Reduction of the Pontryagin and Nieh-Yan topological 4-forms to a constant-time spacelike hypersurface; application of the scalar-vector-tensor (SVT) decomposition at the linearized level; derivation and classification of helicity densities by spin content (spin-1/vector vs. spin-2/tensor); mode and multipole expansions; evaluation on benchmark weak-field spacetimes (Newtonian two-body, boosted Kerr, gyratonic pp-wave, linearized gravitational Hopfion).

Result: Four distinct helicity densities are obtained: two from the Nieh-Yan term (spin-1 and spin-2 gravitomagnetic helicity) and two from the Pontryagin term (spin-1 and spin-2 gravito-current helicity); their mode structure and multipole behavior are explicitly characterized; concrete expressions are derived for all four densities in the listed test spacetimes.

Conclusion: Helicity in linearized gravity admits a natural fourfold decomposition tied to topological invariants and SVT modes; this framework unifies prior notions, provides gauge-invariant local densities, and enables quantitative helicity analysis in realistic weak-field settings—including potential relevance for gravitational-wave memory, vorticity, and topological solitons like the gravitational Hopfion.

Abstract: Helicity in gravity is a multifaceted concept, and several inequivalent definitions exist in the literature. In this work, we reduce the topological Pontryagin and Nieh-Yan terms to a constant time spacelike hypersurface. Remarkably, upon applying the SVT decomposition at the linearized level, we obtain four distinct helicity densities. The helicity density arising from the Nieh-Yan term is termed the spin-1 and spin-2 gravitomagnetic helicity density, depending on whether it originates from vector or tensor modes. Meanwhile, the helicity density arising from the Pontryagin term is termed the spin-1 and spin-2 gravito-current helicity density, according to the corresponding mode contributions. We study the mode and multipole expansions of these helicity functionals and apply them to leading order Newtonian two-body systems, weak field boosted Kerr, a locally defined slowly varying gyratonic pp-wave model, and a linearized gravitational Hopfion.

</details>


### [121] [Black hole correspondences of quasi-topological gravity : Shadows, quasinormal modes, graybody factors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30169)
*Sihao Fan,Chen Wu,Wenjun Guo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores correspondences among black hole observables—shadow, quasinormal modes (QNM), and graybody factors (GBF)—for regular black holes in 5D quasi-topological gravity, validating and refining GBF–QNM and shadow–GBF correspondences using WKB and numerical methods, and extending the latter to higher dimensions with Langer correction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test and extend theoretical correspondences among key black hole observables—shadow, QNMs, and GBFs—in higher-dimensional (D=5) quasi-topological gravity, enabling multi-messenger gravitational tests via single-observable predictions.

Method: Applies the WKB approximation and GrayHawk numerical integration to compute QNMs and GBFs; implements the Langer correction (κ = √[l(l+D−3)] ≈ l + (D−3)/2) to improve low-mode (l=2) accuracy of the shadow–GBF correspondence in D=5.

Result: The GBF–QNM correspondence shows small deviation from numerical results; the Langer-corrected shadow–GBF correspondence achieves accuracy comparable to GBF–QNM at low modes; the shadow–GBF correspondence is successfully extended to D=5 regular black holes.

Conclusion: The shadow–GBF correspondence is robust and extendable to higher dimensions with appropriate angular-momentum rescaling, offering a practical tool for predicting Hawking radiation and QNM spectra from shadow measurements—enabling unified, multi-messenger tests of quantum-gravity-inspired black hole models.

Abstract: Within the framework of quasi-topological gravity, we systematically investigate the correspondences among three classes of observables---the shadow, the quasinormal modes, and the graybody factors---of regular black holes in $D = 5$ dimensions. Using the WKB approximation and GrayHawk numerical integration, we test the applicability of the GBF--QNM correspondence and the shadow--GBF correspondence in the low-mode ($l=2$) and higher-dimensions ($D=5$): the difference between the GBF--QNM correspondence and the numerical results remains small in magnitude; we apply the known Langer correction (replacing $l$ by the effective angular momentum $κ= \sqrt{l(l+D-3)} \simeq l + (D-3)/2$), which reduces the error of the shadow--GBF correspondence at low modes to a level comparable with that of the GBF--QNM correspondence. Our work extends the shadow--GBF correspondence to higher-dimensional regular black holes, providing a viable route to predict the black hole spectrum and the Hawking radiation profile from a single observable, and thereby to realize multi-messenger tests of gravity.

</details>


### [122] [External magnetic-field effects on dipolar-particle orbits and critical collisions in Kerr--Bertotti--Robinson spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30755)
*Haowei Chen,Hengyu Xu,Shao-Jun Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how strong magnetic fields influence particle dynamics near black holes by separating gravitational backreaction (spacetime deformation) from direct magnetic dipole coupling, using the exact Kerr–Bertotti–Robinson spacetime; it finds that both effects modify orbital structure and ISCO properties, alter periapsis precession, and impact the Bañados–Silk–West (BSW) mechanism—yet the ultrarelativistic limit of critical orbits remains governed solely by null circular geodesics, and near-horizon collision energies diverge only for extremal horizons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To disentangle and quantify two distinct physical mechanisms—gravitational backreaction of strong magnetic fields on spacetime geometry versus direct coupling of particle magnetic dipoles to the field—in relativistic orbital dynamics and high-energy collisions near black holes.

Method: Analytical study of equatorial geodesic and magnetized-particle motion in the exact Kerr–Bertotti–Robinson (KBR) spacetime—a solution describing a rotating black hole immersed in a uniform magnetic field; derivation and analysis of effective potentials, turning points, circular orbit conditions, innermost stable circular orbit (ISCO), periapsis precession, and center-of-mass energy in near-horizon collisions, including BSW-type configurations.

Result: Gravitational backreaction alone shifts turning points, modifies circular-orbit domains, eliminates bound orbits by flattening the effective potential well, and induces zero-precession orbits; direct dipole coupling breaks B-reversal symmetry and orientation-dependently shifts ISCO radius, energy, and angular momentum; however, ultrarelativistic critical orbits asymptote to circular null geodesics determined solely by background geometry; in BSW collisions, magnetic effects modify finite-radius potential barriers but do not alter the leading near-horizon divergence of center-of-mass energy for extremal horizons, while for nonextremal horizons, exactly critical particles cannot reach the horizon and collision energy remains finite.

Conclusion: Magnetic fields affect black-hole particle dynamics via separable geometric and dipolar channels; although both strongly influence orbital stability and near-horizon accessibility, the ultimate ultrarelativistic behavior—and thus the possibility of divergent collision energies—is dictated exclusively by the background null geodesic structure, not by magnetic parameters. This constrains the physical realizability of infinite-energy collisions in magnetized environments.

Abstract: Strong magnetic fields affect particle dynamics through two distinct channels: gravitational backreaction deforms the spacetime, while direct coupling to an intrinsic magnetic moment depends on the relative orientation of the field and the dipole. We disentangle these effects by studying equatorial orbits and near-horizon collisions of electrically neutral magnetized particles in the exact Kerr--Bertotti--Robinson spacetime. The field-induced geometric deformation shifts turning points and circular-orbit domains and can eliminate a finite effective-potential well together with its bound orbits. Even without direct dipole coupling, it can also offset the Kerr periapsis advance and produce a finite-radius zero-precession orbit. Direct dipole coupling breaks the symmetry under magnetic-field reversal and shifts the radius, energy, and angular momentum of the innermost stable circular orbit in an orientation-dependent manner. The formal ultrarelativistic endpoints of these orbit branches, however, remain fixed by the background geometry and approach circular null orbits. In the Bañados--Silk--West mechanism, both magnetic effects modify finite-radius potential barriers and hence the ability of a critical particle to reach the near-horizon collision region. In the representative nonzero-field cases examined here, an exactly critical particle released from infinity is blocked before reaching an extremal horizon, although a locally admissible collision between critical and usual particles can still produce unbounded center-of-mass energy. Finite dipole coupling shifts the barriers but does not change the leading near-horizon divergence. Near a nonextremal horizon, an exactly critical particle is excluded and the collision energy remains finite.

</details>


### [123] [Geometric origin of intrinsic rigidity for extremal horizons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30759)
*Wojciech Kamiński,Adam Szereszewski*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper identifies the origin of enhanced symmetry in near-horizon geometries by linking it to a specific foliation by non-expanding horizons, provable under the null convergence condition via geometric analysis of null hypersurfaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the origin of enhanced symmetry observed in near-horizon geometries.

Method: Geometric analysis of null hypersurfaces in near-horizon geometry spacetimes; proof of existence of a foliation by non-expanding horizons under the null convergence condition.

Result: Enhanced symmetry of near-horizon geometries arises from the existence of a foliation by non-expanding horizons, which is guaranteed whenever the null convergence condition holds.

Conclusion: The enhanced symmetry is not ad hoc but geometrically mandated by the structure of null hypersurfaces and energy conditions.

Abstract: Our work clarifies the origin of enhanced symmetry of near horizon geometries. We relate this property to an existence of specific foliation by non-expanding horizons. The existence of this foliation can be shown in any spacetime satisfying null convergence condition. Our proof is geometric and based on analyzing null hypersurfaces in near horizon geometry spacetimes.

</details>


### [124] [Gravitational-Wave Echoes from Layered Compact Objects: A Double-Shell Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30784)
*Qi Su,Ding-Fang Zeng*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies gravitational-wave echoes from a two-shell compact object model to probe internal layering, identifying a novel 'spectral-peak queueing' (SQ) pattern in the echo spectrum correlated with inner-shell mass.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether gravitational-wave echoes can reveal the layered internal structure of astrophysical compact objects—specifically motivated by the persistence of progenitor stellar layering in physical black holes under external-synchronization time coordinates.

Method: Analyzes linear perturbations and GW echo spectra from a spherically symmetric compact object composed of two concentric thin shells; derives and solves the wave equation in the resulting four-cavity effective potential geometry; examines spectral evolution as a function of inner-shell mass ratio q.

Result: The double-shell structure creates an additional barrier and four coupled cavities; increasing inner-shell mass ratio q induces entry of new high-frequency spectral peaks and systematic shifts—second+ peaks monotonically increase in frequency, while the lowest peak exhibits non-monotonic behavior (decrease then return), collectively termed spectral-peak queueing (SQ).

Conclusion: Spectral-peak queueing is a robust signature of multi-layered internal structure in compact objects, demonstrating that GW echoes carry diagnostic information about fine-grained composition and layering, potentially enabling structural inference from future echo observations.

Abstract: Layering is a ubiquitous feature of astrophysical objects. Motivated by the fact that physical black holes retain the layered structure of their progenitor stars when viewed in the time concepts synchronizable with the clock of an outside fixed-position probe, we investigate linear perturbations and gravitational-wave (GW) echoes from a compact object composed of two concentric thin shells. Compared with the single-shell case, the double-shell structure introduces an extra barrier in the effective potential and partitions the wave propagation space into four coupled effective cavities. As the mass ratio of the inner shell increases, new spectral peaks enter from the high-frequency side of the echo spectrum; the second and later peaks shift toward higher frequencies; and the lowest-frequency peak first shifts toward lower frequencies and then returns to its $q=0$ position. We call this variation pattern spectral-peak queueing (SQ). Its existence suggests that GW echoes can be used as probes for the internal structure of compact objects under consideration.

</details>


### [125] [Asymptotic Gravitational Radiation of Photon Rockets with $Λ$: Point, String, and Sheet Sources](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31129)
*Francisco Fernández-Álvarez,José M. M. Senovilla*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates gravitational radiation at infinity for Robinson-Trautman spacetimes with a cosmological constant Λ ≠ 0 and one spacelike Killing vector, modeling 'photon rockets' (point/string/sheet sources). It establishes tensorial criteria—based on the asymptotic super-Poynting vector, Cotton-York tensor, and holographic stress tensor—for absence of radiation at scri, distinguishing cases Λ > 0 and Λ < 0, and links these to principal null directions of the rescaled Weyl tensor.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the understanding of gravitational radiation in Robinson-Trautman spacetimes beyond the Λ = 0 case, particularly for physically relevant 'photon rocket' geometries with arbitrary Λ, and to establish robust, geometrically meaningful criteria for radiation absence at null infinity (scri).

Method: Analyzes asymptotic structure of Robinson-Trautman metrics with one spacelike Killing vector and arbitrary Λ. Uses the asymptotic super-Poynting vector (relative to scri’s normal or timelike vectors tangent to scri) as a radiation detector; for Λ < 0, introduces equivalence with proportionality of Cotton-York and holographic stress tensors at scri. Computes principal null directions (PNDs) of the rescaled Weyl tensor at scri and relates their alignment with scri’s normal to the radiation criteria.

Result: For point sources: only Kinnersley-type rockets are radiation-free for all Λ ≠ 0. For string/sheet sources: new radiation-free solutions emerge when Λ ≠ 0. For Λ > 0: vanishing of the canonical (normal-directed) super-Poynting vector fully characterizes no-radiation. For Λ < 0: radiation absence requires proportionality (not just commutativity) of Cotton-York and holographic stress tensors at scri—or equivalently, vanishing of super-Poynting components normal to scri for all timelike vectors tangent to scri. Explicit counterexamples show commutativity ≠ proportionality ≠ no radiation. PND geometry at scri is shown to encode the radiation criteria.

Conclusion: The presence or absence of gravitational radiation in these Robinson-Trautman photon rockets is governed by precise, Λ-dependent tensorial conditions at scri—super-Poynting vanishing (Λ > 0), Cotton-York/holographic stress proportionality (Λ < 0)—and is geometrically reflected in the alignment of the rescaled Weyl tensor’s principal null directions with scri’s normal vector.

Abstract: We study the existence of gravitational radiation at infinity for pure-radiation Robinson-Trautman metrics with one spacelike Killing vector and a cosmological constant $Λ$ of arbitrary sign. These metrics describe "photon rockets" with point, string, and sheet sources moving through spacetime while emitting null radiation. For $Λ\neq 0$, we use criteria for the existence of gravitational radiation based on the asymptotic super-Poynting vector. For point sources, we extend to $Λ\neq 0$ the known $Λ=0$ result that the Kinnersley rocket is the only rocket without gravitational radiation. However, for string and sheet sources, new possibilities without gravitational radiation arise. For $Λ>0$, we show that the vanishing of the canonical asymptotic super-Poynting vector---computed with respect to the unit normal to scri---exactly characterizes the absence of radiation. On the other hand, for $Λ<0$, we show that the appropriate criterion is the vanishing of the components normal to scri of the asymptotic super-Poynting vectors associated with any unit timelike vector tangent to scri or, equivalently, the proportionality between the Cotton-York tensor and the holographic stress tensor at scri. In particular, we provide explicit examples of metrics for which the Cotton-York and holographic stress tensors commute, yet gravitational radiation is present because the two tensors are not proportional. We also determine the principal null directions (PNDs) of the rescaled Weyl tensor at scri for both signs of $Λ$ and relate their geometry relative to the normal to scri to the tensorial criteria for gravitational radiation.

</details>


### [126] [Saha's ionization in the Schwarzschild spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30950)
*Lázaro L. Sales,Jonatas A. Silva,Amilcar R. Queiroz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives the Saha ionization equilibrium in Schwarzschild spacetime, showing how gravitational redshift modifies particle energies and thermodynamic variables for an asymptotic observer; it recovers the flat-spacetime Saha equation when expressed in locally measured quantities via Tolman–Ehrenfest and Klein conditions, and confirms that relativistic corrections (Maxwell–Jüttner) are negligible where neutral hydrogen dominates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To consistently describe ionization equilibrium in a static gravitational field—specifically, to disentangle genuine gravitational redshift effects from spurious modifications of local atomic physics—and to clarify how global (asymptotic) vs. local thermodynamic descriptions relate in general relativity.

Method: Derives conserved energy from the timelike Killing vector using the mass-shell condition; takes its nonrelativistic limit via the Schwarzschild lapse function; incorporates this energy into the Maxwell–Boltzmann distribution for ionization balance as seen by an asymptotic observer; applies Tolman–Ehrenfest (thermal equilibrium under gravity) and Klein (chemical equilibrium) conditions to rewrite the relation in terms of locally measured quantities; compares with the relativistic Maxwell–Jüttner distribution to assess correction magnitude.

Result: Gravitational field modifies the Saha relation for asymptotic observers via redshift of both particle rest energies and thermodynamic variables (e.g., temperature, number density); the locally formulated Saha equation—using Tolman–Ehrenfest and Klein conditions—exactly reduces to the standard flat-spacetime form; gravitational redshift of ionization energy and fractional departure from flat-space ionization ratio are quantified as functions of asymptotic temperature; leading-order Maxwell–Jüttner correction is negligible where neutral H is abundant (i.e., T ≲ 10⁴ K).

Conclusion: Ionization equilibrium in Schwarzschild spacetime is fully consistent with standard local physics when properly accounting for gravitational redshift and adopting locally measured thermodynamic variables; apparent deviations in asymptotic descriptions arise solely from redshift—not from altered atomic structure or local kinetics.

Abstract: We investigate the Saha ionization equilibrium in the exterior Schwarzschild spacetime. Starting from the mass-shell condition for a massive particle, we derive the conserved energy associated with the timelike Killing symmetry and obtain its nonrelativistic limit in terms of the Schwarzschild lapse function. This energy is then incorporated into the Maxwell--Boltzmann distribution to construct the ionization balance as seen by an asymptotic observer. We show that the gravitational field modifies the Saha relation through the redshift of both the particle energies and the thermodynamic variables. By imposing the Tolman--Ehrenfest and Klein equilibrium conditions, the formulation expressed entirely in terms of locally measured quantities reduces to the standard flat-spacetime Saha equation. We further analyze the gravitational redshift of the ionization energy, the relative departure from the flat-spacetime ionization ratio, and the corresponding shift of the ionization fraction as a function of the asymptotic temperature. We also present the relativistic classical counterpart based on the Maxwell--Jüttner distribution and show explicitly that its leading correction is negligible in the temperature range where neutral hydrogen is appreciably abundant. These results provide a consistent description of ionization equilibrium in a static gravitational field and clarify the distinction between gravitational redshift effects and intrinsic modifications of local atomic physics.

</details>


### [127] [Constraining equation of state of neutron star using neutron star-black hole mergers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30995)
*Vasudev Dubey,Rahul Kashyap,Yugesh Bhoge*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates the feasibility of measuring the tidal deformability of neutron stars in neutron star–black hole (NSBH) mergers using current and future gravitational-wave detectors, finding that ≥20 events are required with next-generation observatories (Einstein Telescope, Cosmic Explorer) to distinguish between neutron star equations of state.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Neutron star tidal deformability encodes information about the equation of state (EOS) and internal composition; unlike binary neutron star mergers where tidal effects are degenerate (via \tilde{\Lambda}), NSBH systems offer a cleaner probe of the neutron star's bare tidal deformability \Lambda.

Method: Bayesian parameter estimation using the Bilby framework on simulated NSBH gravitational-wave signals across current and future detector networks (including Einstein Telescope and Cosmic Explorer), analyzing posterior distributions for tidal deformability and EOS discrimination.

Result: At least 20 NSBH merger detections are required with third-generation detectors to robustly distinguish between different neutron star equations of state via tidal deformability measurements.

Conclusion: NSBH mergers provide a clean channel for EOS inference, and upcoming detectors will enable statistically significant constraints on neutron star matter properties—provided sufficient event statistics (≥20 high-SNR detections) are accumulated.

Abstract: In this work, we study tidal effects that can be observed in gravitational wave transient from neutron star-black hole (NSBH) mergers. It has been well known that neutron star's tidal deformation has imprint of equation of state and hence composition of neutron star matter. Compared to binary neutron star mergers, where we observe a combined effect of two neutron stars by measuring the parameter known as lambda tilde. In neutron star black hole mergers, we measure the tidal deformability of the neutron star component clearly. In this work, we study the feasibility of measuring the bare tidal deformability of a neutron star with planned gravitational detectors in the near and far future. We use the parameter estimation computation framework, Bilby is used to perform Bayesian inference for hundreds of NSBH mergers in current and future GW detector networks and obtain the posterior probability distribution functions of binary parameters. We find that we need at least 20 events to clearly distinguish the equation state of neutron stars in future detectors, including the Einstein Telescope and Cosmic Explorer.

</details>


### [128] [Bekenstein--Hod Bound: A $3.3σ$ Confirmation from GW250114](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31039)
*Hai-Tian Wang,Shao-Peng Tang,Yi-Zhong Fan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper tests the Bekenstein-Hod bound—a fundamental limit on black hole relaxation rate—using GW150914's successor, GW250114, by independently estimating remnant temperature (from pre-merger data) and longest-lived decay time (from post-merger ringdown), avoiding data reuse; it confirms the bound at 3.3–3.6σ, surpassing prior confidence and enabling a precision astrophysical test of black hole relaxation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To observationally test the Bekenstein-Hod bound—a theoretical lower limit on black hole relaxation time at fixed temperature—which bridges gravity, thermodynamics, and information theory but requires independent thermodynamic and dynamical characterization of the remnant black hole.

Method: Uses GW250114: infers remnant Hawking temperature from pre-merger strain data truncated ≥10M before merger peak; measures longest-lived quasinormal mode (QNM) damping time from post-merger ringdown, allowing frequencies and damping times to vary freely (not restricted to Kerr QNMs); explicitly includes the first overtone in waveform modeling; avoids data reuse by strictly separating pre- and post-merger datasets.

Result: The Bekenstein-Hod bound is satisfied at 3.3–3.6σ significance for primary analysis (t_< −10M), a marked improvement over GW150914’s 91% CL (~1.7σ); robust across varying pre-merger cutoffs and with first-overtone inclusion.

Conclusion: This constitutes the first high-significance, separated-data empirical verification of the Bekenstein-Hod bound, transforming it from an abstract information-theoretic constraint into a precision test of black hole physics in a single astrophysical system.

Abstract: Black holes link gravity, thermodynamics and information via limits on the relaxation rate of a perturbed system. The Bekenstein-Hod bound imposes a minimum relaxation time at fixed temperature, but its observational test demands both black hole thermodynamic characterization and decay time measurement. Here we test this bound with GW250114, the loudest gravitational-wave signal yet observed from a binary black-hole merger. We infer the remnant temperature from pre-merger data truncated at least $10\,M$ before the peak and its longest-lived decay time from post-merger data, thereby avoiding direct reuse of the same strain samples. The ringdown frequencies and damping times are allowed to vary independently rather than being fixed to the Kerr spectrum. For the primary $t_{<}=-10\,M$ analysis, the bound is verified at $3.3-3.6σ$ across the focal ringdown start times, representing a substantial improvement over the $91\%$ confidence level set by GW150914. The conclusion remains robust under varied pre-merger cutoffs and explicit inclusion of the short-lived first overtone in waveform modelling. This separated-data measurement converts an information-theoretic relaxation bound into a precision test of a single astrophysical black hole.

</details>


### [129] [Constants of motion and fundamental frequencies for elliptic orbits at fourth post-Newtonian order: aligned-spin contributions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31072)
*David Trestini,Jan Steinhoff*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes spin-dependent contributions to the gauge-invariant conservative dynamics of eccentric, aligned-spin binary systems at fourth post-Newtonian (4PN) order, extending prior nonspinning results; it provides full spin-structured maps between constants of motion and orbital frequencies, derives redshift/gyroscopic invariants and circular links, reveals a novel connection between the Blanchet-Iyer-Favata ISCO stability criterion and periastron advance, and completes the 4PN scattering angle for unbound orbits including all spin effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the gauge-invariant conservative description of compact binary dynamics to include all spin effects—linear through quartic, plus spin-deformability—at 4PN order for eccentric, aligned-spin systems, thereby enabling high-precision gravitational-wave modeling and testing of strong-field gravity.

Method: Analytic post-Newtonian computation using effective-one-body and Hamiltonian methods within the Arnowitt–Deser–Misner (ADM) formalism; derivation of gauge-invariant relations via canonical transformations and frequency-domain analysis; systematic inclusion of spin-induced multipole moments and tidal deformability parameters; application of the Blanchet-Iyer-Favata stability criterion to relate ISCO frequency to periastron advance.

Result: Complete 4PN conservative map between (E, L_z) and (Omega_r, Omega_phi) for aligned-spin eccentric binaries, including all spin-linear to spin-quartic and spin-deformability terms; derived redshift and gyroscopic invariants; circular-orbit links; identification of equivalence between ISCO stability condition and periastron advance for circular orbits; full 4PN spin-complete scattering angle for hyperbolic orbits.

Conclusion: The spin-extended 4PN conservative dynamics significantly enriches the theoretical framework for modeling spinning compact binaries across bound and unbound regimes, strengthens connections between orbital stability and precession observables, and provides essential inputs for next-generation gravitational-wave data analysis and fundamental tests of general relativity.

Abstract: For eccentric systems with aligned spins, we compute all spin contributions to the gauge-invariant conservative map between the constants of motion (energy and angular momentum) and the fundamental (radial and azimuthal) frequencies at the fourth post-Newtonian order. This extends the nonspinning map derived in [arXiv:2511.10735] to include terms that are linear, quadratic, cubic, and quartic in spin, as well as the associated spin-deformability parameters. We also obtain derived quantities such as the redshift and gyroscopic invariants, as well as circular links. We notice that the Blanchet-Iyer-Favata post-Newtonian stability criterion that determines the dimensionless frequency of the innermost stable circular orbit (ISCO) is in fact intricately related to the periastron advance for circular orbits. Finally, in the case of unbound orbits, we complete the 4PN scattering angle with all spin contributions.

</details>


### [130] [Evolution of Cosmic String Loops under Gravitational Backreaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31163)
*Lasse Gerblich,Richard A. Battye,E. P. S. Shellard*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how gravitational backreaction modifies the gravitational wave emission from Nambu-Goto cosmic string loops, particularly at cusps; it finds that cusps persist locally but their bursts are weakened and acquire a high-frequency cutoff, reducing detectability in current and near-future GW detectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how gravitational backreaction—often neglected—affects cusp-driven gravitational wave bursts from cosmic string loops, crucial for accurate interpretation of GW search results.

Method: Reformulates the Nambu-Goto equations of motion in a convenient gauge to self-consistently include the loop’s dynamically sourced gravitational field, enabling the first continuous numerical evolution of loops under full gravitational backreaction.

Result: Local cusps survive backreaction, but the emitted gravitational waveform is significantly modified: cusp bursts are weakened and exhibit a high-frequency cutoff at $f_c \propto (G\mu)^{-3/2}$, suppressing signal power above this scale.
